# Ночное ревью — сырые находки (НЕ проверены)

Результат всех 34 ревьюеров (`wf_2e6c265c-8c7`); проверка находок не выполнена (лимит). Среди них есть дубли (сквозные проходы повторяют модульные), ложные срабатывания и уже принятые решения.

Всего: 362 — high 8, medium 97, low 257

## `examples/viewer/main.js`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L504: loadEnv calls a removed helper `invalidate`: every environment load ends in a false red error**
  - description: `scene.whenReady().then(invalidate)` references an identifier that no longer exists anywhere in the viewer (the only match for `invalidate` in examples/viewer/*.js and index.html is this line; docs/review-style.md records 'invalidate() calls and helper gone'). In an ES module this throws ReferenceError synchronously, inside the try block, after setEnvironmentHdr and status("") have already run. The catch then reports it as a load failure.
  - scenario: Open the viewer, or pick any entry in the environment dropdown. The HDR is fetched and applied, then the status line shows "Can't load Venice sunset: invalidate is not defined" in the error style, and it stays there (at startup it lasts until the model load overwrites the status). The environment did load, so the message is false.
  - fix: Delete the line (bake completion already bumps the context's content version, which needsRender sees), or replace it with `scene.whenReady().then(() => scene.invalidate())`.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L273: Two-finger gestures: a still two-finger tap picks, and coincident pointers jump the zoom to its limit**
  - description: `moved` is reset to 0 on every pointerdown, including the second finger, and `release` treats the last pointer up as a tap whenever `moved < 6`. So a two-finger touch with under 6 px of total movement ends in `pickAt` (npm/orbit.js handles the same case with `_moved = Infinity`). Separately, in pointermove `zoom(pinch / d)` is not guarded against `d === 0`, which gives `zoom(Infinity)`.
  - scenario: Rest two fingers on the canvas and lift them without moving: the inspector switches to whatever is under the second finger. If two pointers report the same coordinates during a pinch, `orbit.distance` is clamped straight to `radius * 50`.
  - fix: On the second pointerdown set `moved = Infinity` (as orbit.js does), and require `d > 0` before calling `zoom(pinch / d)`.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L457: 'Save image' changes the framing when twice the canvas exceeds the capture limit**
  - description: The viewer asks for `canvas.width * 2` by `canvas.height * 2`. `Scene.capture` clamps each side independently to min(MAX_RENDERBUFFER_SIZE, 8192) (packages/luxel/src/js/capture.rs:56-60) and the camera aspect follows the clamped buffer. When only one side is clamped the picture has a different aspect ratio from the view.
  - scenario: A 5120x2880 canvas (5K display at device resolution): the request is 10240x5760, clamped to 8192x5760, so the PNG is 1.42:1 instead of 16:9 and its sides are cropped compared with the screen. A phone with a 4096 limit and a 1080x2400 canvas gets 2160x4096.
  - fix: Compute one scale, `Math.min(2, limit / canvas.width, limit / canvas.height)`, and pass both sides multiplied by it.
- [ ] **[low/async] L692: pickAt applies a pick result that can outlive the model, and has no rejection handling**
  - description: `scene.pick` resolves a frame or more later. `pickAt` then calls `inspect(hit.entity, …)` without checking that the model picked from is still the one shown. `scene.pick` also throws when the context is lost (packages/luxel/src/js/picking.rs:26-28) and its promise can reject; `release` calls `pickAt(e)` without a catch.
  - scenario: (1) Tap the model at the moment a load finishes (`despawn(root)` + `inspect(0, "")` in load): the late pick result reopens the inspector for a despawned entity, showing 'entity N', material '#0' and a copy button whose handler throws from cloneMaterial. (2) Press 'Simulate a WebGL context loss' and tap the canvas within the following second: an unhandled promise rejection.
  - fix: Capture `root` before the await and drop the result if it changed (or if `!scene.isAlive(hit.entity)`); wrap the body in try/catch.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L716: Materials made by 'Give this part its own copy' are never destroyed**
  - description: `scene.cloneMaterial` returns a scene-owned material that is freed only by `destroyMaterial` or `scene.destroy()` (packages/luxel/src/js/materials.rs:296-298). `Scene.despawn` removes only the model's own material groups (packages/luxel/src/js/mod.rs:291-295). The viewer keeps no list of its clones and never calls destroyMaterial, neither when the button is pressed again for the same part nor when the model is replaced in `load()`.
  - scenario: Press the button a few times and/or on several parts, then load another model, and repeat. Each clone's parameter group and MaterialRecord stay in the scene for the life of the page, with texture references to a model that has been destroyed.
  - fix: Track clones in an array; destroy a part's previous clone when replacing it, and all of them in `load()` right after `scene.despawn(root)`.

## `examples/viewer/profiling.js`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L47: The long-task fallback never runs: observe() with an unsupported type does not throw**
  - description: The code relies on `observe({ type: "long-animation-frame" })` throwing to fall back to `longtask`. Per the Performance Timeline spec, an unsupported `type` is ignored with a console warning and no exception. The catch branch is reached only when PerformanceObserver itself is missing.
  - scenario: Chromium older than 123 (including older Android WebViews), which supports `longtask` but not `long-animation-frame`: the first observer is silently inert, the fallback is skipped, and recorded traces have an empty 'browser: long animation frames' track.
  - fix: Choose by `PerformanceObserver.supportedEntryTypes?.includes("long-animation-frame")`, then `includes("longtask")`, instead of try/catch.
- [ ] **[low/async] L124: measureView restores settings blindly across awaits and not at all on failure**
  - description: Each row changes scene state (material weights, transmission resolution, shading model, TAA/samples, FXAA, visibility), awaits `time()` (which awaits whenReady and a 50 ms timeout while the page stays interactive), then writes back a value captured earlier. There is no try/finally: the `.catch` at line 112 only stops the trace and re-enables the buttons. The settings panel is not disabled while this runs.
  - scenario: During the 'no TAA, no MSAA' row the user picks 'FXAA' in the Antialiasing select. The row then finishes with `setSamples(samples); setTaa(true)`, so the scene runs TAA while the UI shows FXAA. Similarly, if any awaited call rejects mid-row, the scene is left as that row set it (model hidden, shading model switched, weights at 0) with the UI still showing the old values.
  - fix: Wrap each change/restore pair in try/finally, and disable the panel (or re-read the UI and re-apply its values) while measuring.

## `npm/README.md`

- [ ] **[high/leaks] L273: README says free()/GC frees a Scene or Model, but only destroy() frees their GPU objects**
  - description: The README's memory section says objects (`Scene`, `Model`, ...) are freed by the garbage collector and that `free()` 'matters for large ones such as a Model or a Scene'. It never mentions `scene.destroy()` or `model.destroy(ctx)`. Neither `Scene` nor `Model` implements `Drop` (the only Drop impls are DefaultFramebufferInner, TexturePacker, TextureCompressor, Undo, PendingRead, Core, LossEvents and loader internals). `free()` or a GC finalizer (`--weak-refs`) therefore drops only the Rust struct: every buffer, texture, program, framebuffer and param group stays in the Context's pools. `Scene::destroy` (packages/luxel/src/js/mod.rs:1236) is also the only caller of `TextureCompressor::release_shared`, and the thread-local `SHARED` slot (packages/luxel-gl/src/texture_compressor.rs:440) holds a `Context` clone, so the context itself stays alive after the app drops it. The `alive` flag is never cleared either, so reloads in flight are not freed.
  - scenario: An SPA unmounts its viewer and calls `scene.free()` as the README advises, or just drops the scene, then builds a new Scene on the same ctx. `ctx.resourceStats()` keeps growing by the whole scene (models, HDR targets, shadow maps, environment cubes) on every mount. The same happens for `const m = await loadModel(ctx, url); m.free()` without `m.destroy(ctx)`.
  - fix: Document `scene.destroy()` and `model.destroy(ctx)` as the way to release GPU memory and correct the free() sentence; or implement Drop for Scene and Model (handles are generation-checked, so a destroy from Drop is safe) so free() and GC release them.
- [ ] **[low/api] L265: README lists isLost() among the scene's state queries; Scene has no such method**
  - description: The paragraph on `isX` / `hasX` names `isAlive(e)`, `isShown(e)`, `hasEnvironment()` and `isLost()` in a section that otherwise lists Scene getters. `Scene` exports no `isLost`: it exists on `Context` (`ctx.isLost()`) and on `Model` (`model.isLost(ctx)`).
  - scenario: `if (scene.isLost()) …` written from the README throws a TypeError at run time and is a TypeScript error.
  - fix: Write `ctx.isLost()` in the README, or add a `Scene.isLost()` forward.

## `npm/orbit.js`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L70: OrbitControls never places the camera until the first input; writes to target/distance/yaw/pitch are ignored**
  - description: `update()` returns early when no velocity is pending, autoRotate is 0 and `force` is false. The constructor does not call lookAt either. So the documented loop `orbit.update(dt)` leaves the camera wherever the entity was, unless `frame()` was called. The public, writable fields `target`, `distance`, `yaw`, `pitch` (orbit.d.ts lines 34-40) have no effect when assigned until the user next drags or scrolls, at which point the camera jumps. The header comment justifies the early return with 'a still orbit leaves the scene unchanged', but `Scene.lookAt` already uses `set_if_neq` (packages/luxel/src/js/mod.rs:1317), so an unconditional lookAt would not trigger needsRender.
  - scenario: `const orbit = new OrbitControls(scene, camera, canvas); loop: orbit.update(dt); scene.renderFrame(camera)` without `frame()`: the camera stays at the spawn transform (the origin, usually inside the model) and snaps to yaw 0 / pitch 0.2 / distance 3 on the first pointer move. Likewise `orbit.target = [0, 1, 0]; orbit.distance = 5;` followed by `update(dt)` returns false and nothing moves.
  - fix: Place the camera once in the constructor, and in `update` compare the current yaw/pitch/distance/target against the last placed values (or always call lookAt, which is a no-op when unchanged) and return whether they differ.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L113: dispose() leaves `touch-action: none` on the element**
  - description: `_listen` overwrites `el.style.touchAction = "none"`; `dispose` only removes the listeners. The previous inline value is not saved or restored.
  - scenario: An app swaps OrbitControls for another control scheme, or disposes it and keeps the canvas in a scrollable page: touch scrolling and pinch-zoom over the canvas stay disabled although nothing handles the pointers any more.
  - fix: Remember the previous `style.touchAction` in `_listen` and restore it in `dispose`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L158: onTap fires for right-click and shift-click, which are the pan gestures**
  - description: `up` reports a tap for any single pointer released with little movement; it does not look at `e.button` or at the `pan` flag stored for the pointer in `down`. The `contextmenu` handler suppresses the menu at the same time.
  - scenario: With `orbit.onTap = (x, y) => scene.pick(camera, x, y).then(select)`, a right-click on the canvas (no drag) runs a pick and selects a part, though right-click is documented as pan.
  - fix: In `up`, require `e.button === 0` (or a touch/pen pointer) and that the removed pointer was not a pan pointer before calling onTap.

## `packages/luxel-core/src/column.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L112: Column sizes are multiplied in u32 without a check; an oversized resize wraps silently instead of failing**
  - description: `resize` computes `(len * self.stride) as usize`, and `reserve`, `get`, `get_mut` (lines 126, 133, 140) and `element_bytes` do the same u32 multiply. The release profile has no overflow-checks, so a product >= 2^32 wraps: `data` is resized to the wrapped (small) length while `dirty` is resized to the requested `len` and `len()` reports a third value (data.len / stride). The same pattern is in dirty.rs Runs::next (`self.chunk * d.chunk`, line 150) for a tracked length within one chunk of u32::MAX. Column has no production user today (kept for the planned zero-copy views), so this is latent.
  - scenario: `Column::<f32>::new(16, 64).resize(1 << 28)` (2^28 mat4 elements): 2^28 * 16 wraps to 0, so the column silently becomes empty with layout_version bumped and 2^28 elements marked dirty; take_dirty() then returns `0..268435456` for a zero-length buffer and an upload slicing bytes() by it panics. In a debug build the same call panics on overflow instead.
  - fix: Use checked_mul (or do the multiply in usize/u64) in resize/reserve and panic or return an error with a clear message when the scalar count does not fit; index math in get/get_mut can be done in usize.

## `packages/luxel-core/src/layout.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L414: Array / struct-array counts are unvalidated: a bad count from JS hangs or OOMs the tab before any size check**
  - description: LayoutBuilder::array / struct_array (lines 188, 200) accept any u32 count, and build() -> emit() materialises one Field plus a heap-allocated path string per element (loops at lines 414 and 432) before anyone looks at the resulting size. The only guard, validate_block_size in packages/luxel-gl/src/uniform_buffer.rs (create_uniform_buffer), runs after layout.builder.build(). The offset math (`off + i * stride`, `stride * count`, `ssize * count`, lines 417/421/433/435) is plain u32 arithmetic and the release profile has no overflow-checks, so it would also wrap silently instead of failing. A count of 0 is accepted as well (GLSL has no zero-length arrays), giving a field with no addressable elements.
  - scenario: JS: `new UboLayout().array('offsets', UniformType.Vec4, -1)` (wasm-bindgen converts -1 to 4294967295), or a count read from a wrong variable such as a byte length. ctx.createUniformBuffer(layout) then tries to push ~4 billion Fields with formatted names: the tab freezes and the wasm instance aborts on out-of-memory, instead of returning the 'UBO block is N bytes, over MAX_UNIFORM_BLOCK_SIZE' error. `structArray('s', new UboLayout(), 4e9)` (empty struct) spins for 4 billion iterations without even allocating.
  - fix: Compute the size first with checked arithmetic (measure without emitting leaves) and make build() fallible, or have the builder reject count == 0 and counts whose running size exceeds a cap (e.g. u32 checked_mul / a max byte size passed by the caller) before emitting any field; map the failure to an Error in create_uniform_buffer.
- [ ] **[low/api] L205: Duplicate or colliding field paths are accepted silently**
  - description: LayoutBuilder::push never checks names. build() sorts leaves by path and field_id() binary-searches them, so two leaves with the same path both get laid out but only one (whichever the binary search lands on) is reachable; the other can never be written. Names containing '.' or '[' collide with struct/array paths the same way (field 'a.b' vs struct 'a' with member 'b'; field 'v[0]' vs array 'v').
  - scenario: `UboLayout().field('u_color', Vec4).field('u_scale', F32).field('u_color', Vec3)`: createUniformBuffer succeeds, the block is 48 bytes, and set_vec4('u_color') / set_vec3('u_color') resolve to an arbitrary one of the two entries - one setter warns about a type mismatch (or writes the wrong offset) with no hint that the layout itself is wrong.
  - fix: Reject (or warn once on) an empty name, a name containing '.', '[' or ']', and a name already present among the builder's fields - in push(), or as an error from build().
- [ ] **[low/perf] L371: measure() fully emits (and throws away) every nested leaf, repeated per struct-array element and per nesting level**
  - description: measure() computes a struct's size by calling emit() into a temporary Vec, which allocates a Field and a path String for every leaf (array elements included) only to drop them. emit() calls measure() for each Struct/StructArray member, and member_align() calls measure() again, so each nesting level re-walks its whole subtree about three times; and because StructArray calls emit(sf, ...) once per element (line 433), any nested struct inside the element is re-measured `count` times.
  - scenario: A lights block: struct_array('lights', light, 64) where `light` has a nested struct with a 4-element array: the nested struct is measured (4 Fields + 4 Strings allocated and freed, twice) for each of the 64 elements, on top of the real emission. Only at layout creation, so it is start-up / per-createUniformBuffer waste, not per-frame.
  - fix: Give emit() an Option<&mut Vec<Field>> (or a separate size-only walk) so measuring allocates nothing, and compute (align, size) once per struct member before the per-element loop (it is already computed once at line 430 for the outer struct - pass nested results down or cache them on FieldDef).

## `packages/luxel-core/src/lib.rs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L6: Stale 'ECS components' claim for packed layouts; the Packed rule and the storage 'tracker clearing' comment describe things that no longer exist**
  - description: The crate doc says packed layouts are for 'vertex/instance data, ECS components'. docs/review-style.md lists the 'layouts "for ECS components"' comment as fixed, but this copy remains, and ECS components are plain Rust types in Vec<T> (docs/design/ecs.md, E1 notes) - nothing lays them out through Layout. In fact LayoutRule::Packed has no user outside layout.rs's own tests (the only build() call in the workspace is LayoutRule::Std140 in luxel-gl/src/uniform_buffer.rs), so the packed branches of placement/block_align/aggregate_align are exercised only by tests. Similarly packages/luxel-ecs/src/storage.rs:269 describes AnyStorage as used 'for despawn and tracker clearing', but clear_trackers was removed in phase 2 item 5.3 (it is now trim_removed and changed_since).
  - scenario: A reader looking for how instance data or ECS components are laid out follows the lib.rs doc to Layout/Packed and finds no caller; someone extending AnyStorage looks for the tracker-clearing path that the comment names and it is gone.
  - fix: Drop 'ECS components' from the lib.rs doc line (and either note that Packed is not used yet, like `column`, or remove it until vertex/instance layouts use it); reword the AnyStorage comment to 'despawn, trimming the removal log and the any-change check'.

## `packages/luxel-ecs/src/storage.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L72: Storage's public write paths take any tick: no clamp to the world tick, and a stamp can lower a row's tick below `newest`**
  - description: World::insert_at / get_mut_at clamp the tick to world.tick() ('it would read as changed for good'), but the route the crate docs actually recommend - `world.storages_mut()` / `storage_mut()` then `Storage::get_mut(e, now)` (line 72), `iter_mut(now)` (line 180), `mark_changed(row, tick)` (line 136) - stores whatever tick is passed. In addition `Mut::stamp` (line 237), `insert` on replace (line 87) and `mark_changed` assign the row tick instead of taking the max, while `newest` only ever rises, so after an older stamp `changed_since(t)` can be true while `iter_changed(t)` yields nothing for that row.
  - scenario: A system passes a frame counter or `world.tick() + 1` (off by one from the tick rule) to `storage_mut::<Transform>().get_mut(e, tick)`: `newest` and the row tick are now ahead of the world, so `Storage::changed_since(last_run)`, `iter_changed(last_run)` and `World::changed_since(observed)` keep reporting a change on every later frame until the world tick catches up - the transform system recomputes that subtree every frame and on-demand rendering never goes idle. Nothing in Storage can detect it, since it does not know the world tick.
  - fix: Either keep Storage's tick-taking mutators pub(crate) and hand systems a view created by World that carries the clamped `now`, or document the precondition on each and make the row stamps `max(old, tick)` so a row tick and `newest` cannot disagree.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L244: Mut::set_if_neq marks a component changed on every call once it holds a NaN**
  - description: set_if_neq compares with PartialEq (`*self.value != value`). For a component with float fields that contains a NaN, the comparison is always 'different', so each call writes and stamps even when the caller passes the same bits. The method exists precisely so that per-frame re-sets (camera aspect in renderFrame, framing, animation weights) are not changes.
  - scenario: A Transform or Camera gets a NaN field through the Rust API (world.insert with a degenerate matrix decomposition, a zero-length look direction, etc.). From then on every per-frame `set_if_neq` on it (packages/luxel/src/framing.rs:98/101, js/lights.rs:81, animation.rs:179/196) stamps the row: World::changed_since stays true, the transform system recomputes that subtree each frame, and on-demand rendering keeps drawing a static scene.
  - fix: Document that T's PartialEq must be reflexive for the 'unchanged' guarantee, or give the float-bearing scene components a bitwise PartialEq (compare to_bits) so identical NaN payloads count as equal.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/buffer.rs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L48: Comment claims index buffers can bind to no other target; WebGL2 allows COPY_READ/COPY_WRITE, so the VAO unbind on index uploads is unnecessary**
  - description: The comment at lines 48-52 and 75-79 says a buffer once bound to ELEMENT_ARRAY_BUFFER "can never be bound to any other target", and bind_for_upload therefore routes index uploads through ELEMENT_ARRAY_BUFFER after `bindVertexArray(null)`, with `forget_vao()` in create_index_buffer / buffer_write. Per the WebGL 2 spec (5.1 Buffer Object Binding) COPY_READ_BUFFER and COPY_WRITE_BUFFER do not set or conflict with the buffer's type: a buffer of type "element array" may be bound there (only copyBufferSubData between an element-array and an other-data buffer is INVALID_OPERATION). So index data can be created and rewritten through COPY_WRITE_BUFFER exactly like generic buffers. The wrong premise is repeated by callers: copy.rs:296/317 refuses index-to-index copies and readback.rs:391/441 refuses reading an index buffer, both legal in WebGL2.
  - scenario: Every createIndexBuffer / bufferWrite on an index buffer (one per glTF primitive at load, each write of dynamic indices) unbinds the current VAO and invalidates the renderer's VAO cache, forcing a rebind on the next draw; reading an index buffer back or copying one index buffer to another returns an error although WebGL permits it.
  - fix: Use COPY_WRITE_BUFFER as the scratch target for both kinds (a fresh buffer's type stays undefined until it is first bound to ELEMENT_ARRAY_BUFFER by vertex_array_set_index_buffer), drop the VAO unbind and forget_vao calls, and correct the comments; then relax copy/readback to reject only mixed index/generic pairs.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L119: create_empty_buffer records a size the GL buffer does not have for sizes above i32::MAX (and when bufferData fails)**
  - description: `Init::Size(size)` calls `buffer_data_with_i32(t, size as i32, …)`: a u32 above 2^31-1 becomes negative, GL raises INVALID_VALUE and the buffer has no data store, yet `BufferObj.size` is set to the requested value and Ok is returned. The same happens for any size when bufferData fails with OUT_OF_MEMORY. `size` then feeds bufferSize(), copy_buffer / read range validation and resourceStats' bufferBytes.
  - scenario: ctx.createEmptyBuffer(StaticDraw, 3_000_000_000) returns a valid id; bufferSize() reports 3e9, resourceStats().bufferBytes jumps by 3 GB, copy_buffer's own range check passes and the GL call then fails with a bare INVALID_VALUE.
  - fix: Reject size > i32::MAX with an Err before the GL call; optionally query getError (or BUFFER_SIZE via getBufferParameter) after large allocations and return an Err instead of recording the requested size.
- [ ] **[low/api] L186: buffer_write does not check offset/length against the buffer size**
  - description: `buffer_write(buffer, offset: i32, data)` knows the buffer size (`b.size`) but passes any offset and length to bufferSubData. A negative offset or offset + data.len() > size produces a raw GL INVALID_VALUE and nothing is written; the caller gets no luxel message naming the buffer, unlike copy_buffer (copy.rs:316-325) which validates the same ranges and reports them.
  - scenario: const b = ctx.createEmptyBuffer(DynamicDraw, 64); ctx.bufferWrite(b, 60, new Uint8Array(16)) -> only "WebGL: INVALID_VALUE: bufferSubData: buffer overflow"; the write is dropped whole (not clamped) and instance data stays stale. Same with offset = -4.
  - fix: Check `offset >= 0 && offset as u64 + data.len() as u64 <= b.size as u64`; otherwise console::error with the handle, offset, length and size, and return (or return a Result as create_* do).

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/compress/bc6h.frag`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L213: BC6H: zero-range blocks and regions divide by zero; NaN disables the pattern search and feeds undefined index conversions**
  - description: EncodeP1 computes `blockDir / (blockDir.x + blockDir.y + blockDir.z)` (line 213), EncodeP2Pattern does the same per region (lines 327-328), and EvaluateP2Pattern calls `normalize(p0BlockMax - p0BlockMin)` (lines 294-295). When a block, or one region of a two-region pattern, holds a single colour, the range is the zero vector and all three produce NaN. Two consequences: 1. Deterministic on any IEEE GPU: EvaluateP2Pattern returns NaN for every pattern with a flat region, and `score < bestScore` in main() (line 544) is false for NaN. Such a pattern can never be selected, even when it is the exact fit (half the block constant, half gradient). If pattern 0 itself has a flat region, bestScore starts as NaN and no other pattern can replace it, so the 32-pattern search is switched off for that block. 2. ComputeIndex3/ComputeIndex4 then evaluate `uint(clamp(NaN, 0.0, 7.0 or 15.0))`. GLSL ES 3.00 leaves clamp/min/max of NaN and float-to-uint of NaN undefined. D3D and Metal return the non-NaN operand, giving index 0, which is harmless. An implementation that propagates NaN yields an out-of-range index, and `indices[i] << n` (lines 255-270, 475-525) then ORs stray bits over the neighbouring texels' indices. The same code is in upstream Godot/Narkowicz; the port inherits it.
  - scenario: An HDR environment cube with black (0,0,0) or clamped areas next to a light edge, compressed with CompressKind::Hdr. A block whose left two columns are all zero and right two columns are a gradient: pattern 0 scores NaN, the search is stuck on pattern 0, and the block is encoded from NaN direction vectors or falls back to the one-region mode 11. Edge blocks come out visibly worse than the encoder can do. On a driver whose clamp propagates NaN, the non-flat region's indices are corrupted.
  - fix: Guard the zero range. In EvaluateP2Pattern, use a zero direction when the squared length of (max - min) is 0, so the distance is finite. In EncodeP1 and EncodeP2Pattern, skip the division when the component sum is 0 and force that region's indices to 0. Optionally return 0 from ComputeIndex3/4 when endPoint1Pos == endPoint0Pos.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/console.rs`

- [ ] **[low/leaks] L47: `*_once` dedup set grows without bound, and messages are formatted before the dedup check**
  - description: `REPORTED` is a `HashSet<String>` that is only cleared by the test hook `forget_reported`. Many `error_once` / `warn_once` messages embed a handle: `report_stale` (context.rs:237) formats `{kind} {handle:?}`, so every distinct stale handle adds a permanent entry. Callers also build the full `String` with `format!` before `first_time` is consulted, so an already-reported problem still allocates and hashes a string on every occurrence.
  - scenario: An app that keeps using handles after `destroy_*` on a stream of fresh objects (per-frame temporary textures or buffers destroyed too early) adds one string per handle per operation for the lifetime of the page. Separately, a persistent problem on the render path (a draw with a stale texture, an unset uniform) allocates one formatted string per draw per frame just to be discarded by the dedup.
  - fix: Cap the set (clear or evict past a few thousand entries), and key dedup on something cheap (op + kind, or a hash computed without allocating) so the hot path does not format a message that will not be shown.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/context.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L352: Context.fromGl leaves the canvas framebuffer at a 0×0 viewport**
  - description: `from_gl` builds the `DefaultFramebuffer` with `Viewport::new(0, 0, 0, 0)` and no canvas. `width()` and `height()` return the viewport's size, and the renderer uses `default_fb.viewport()` for every pass to the canvas (renderer.rs:214). `enableAutoResize` errors without a canvas. So a context made from a raw gl draws every canvas pass into a zero-sized viewport, and `Scene.renderFrame` sizes its HDR targets from `fb.width()/height()` = 0, until the app calls `setViewport` itself. Nothing documents that. `fromCanvas` initializes the viewport from the canvas size.
  - scenario: A worker with an OffscreenCanvas (which `fromCanvas` cannot take, since it wants an HTMLCanvasElement) or a context shared with another engine: `const ctx = Context.fromGl(gl); new Scene(ctx).renderFrame(cam)` shows nothing. HDR target creation at 0×0 fails or is degenerate, and the direct path draws into a 0×0 viewport.
  - fix: Initialize the viewport from `gl.drawing_buffer_width()/height()` in `from_gl` and have `width()/height()` follow the drawing buffer when there is no canvas, or at least document the required `setViewport` call.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L353: `fromGl` leaves the default framebuffer with a 0x0 viewport and no canvas**
  - description: `from_gl` builds `DefaultFramebuffer::new(gl, Viewport::new(0, 0, 0, 0), None)`. `from_canvas` seeds the viewport from the canvas size, but `from_gl` uses neither `gl.drawing_buffer_width()/height()` nor `gl.canvas()`. A pass targeting the canvas uses `default_fb.viewport()` when it has no override (renderer.rs:211-214), and `DefaultFramebuffer::width()/height()` (documented as the current drawing-buffer size) return 0. The function has no doc comment saying a viewport must be set first.
  - scenario: `const ctx = Context.fromGl(canvas.getContext('webgl2'))`, then render a pass to `FramebufferId.NULL`: the viewport is 0x0, so nothing is drawn and nothing is reported. `ctx.defaultFramebuffer().width()` returns 0, so code sizing targets or aspect ratio from it gets 0 / NaN. `defaultFramebuffer().canvas()` is undefined even though the gl has an HTMLCanvasElement, so auto-resize cannot be used either.
  - fix: Seed the viewport from `gl.drawing_buffer_width()/height()` and, when `gl.canvas()` is an `HtmlCanvasElement`, pass it as the canvas; document what an OffscreenCanvas caller still has to do (`setViewport` after resizing).
- [ ] **[low/api] L364: `fromCanvas` reports every creation failure as "WebGL2 not supported" / "get_context failed"**
  - description: `get_context_with_context_options` returning `Ok(None)` is mapped to "WebGL2 not supported" and an exception to "get_context failed" with the JS error dropped. `getContext('webgl2')` also returns null when the canvas already has a context of another type, when `failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat` rejects the device, and when creation fails for resource reasons (too many contexts, blocklisted GPU).
  - scenario: A canvas that earlier had `getContext('2d')` called on it is passed to `Context.fromCanvas`: the error says WebGL2 is not supported on a device that supports it. Likewise `failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat: true` on a software renderer gives the same message, so the app cannot tell the cases apart.
  - fix: Word the null case neutrally ("could not create a WebGL2 context: unsupported, refused by the options, or the canvas already has another context type"), include the caught exception's message, and optionally capture `webglcontextcreationerror`'s `statusMessage`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L395: ctx.setOnLost / setOnRestored cannot be cleared and swallow exceptions, unlike the scene's**
  - description: `Context.setOnLost(f)` and `setOnRestored(f)` require a function (typed `() => void`), replace a single slot with no way to remove it, and discard a thrown exception (`let _ = f.call0(..)`). `Scene.setOnLost` and `setOnRestored` (js/restore.rs:423, 463) accept `undefined` to remove the callback and log 'onLost threw: …'.
  - scenario: An exception inside the app's `ctx.setOnRestored` rebuild code vanishes without a console message and the app is left half rebuilt. `ctx.setOnLost(undefined)` to unregister throws a type error at the binding.
  - fix: Take `Option<Function>` on the context too and log callback errors as the scene does.
- [ ] **[low/api] L400: Exceptions thrown by JS `setOnLost` / `setOnRestored` callbacks are silently discarded**
  - description: Both wrappers call `let _ = f.call0(&JsValue::NULL);`, dropping the `Err(JsValue)` that carries a JS exception. `setOnRestored` is documented as the place to "recreate every resource", which is where `create*` calls throw (`ShaderError`, limits, a second loss).
  - scenario: After a restore the app's `onRestored` throws halfway (a shader fails to compile on the new backend, or a typo). Nothing reaches the console or `window.onerror`; the handler just stops, the scene stays half rebuilt, and `lost_until_restored` is cleared as if it had succeeded.
  - fix: On `Err(e)`, report it (`console::error` with the exception, or rethrow asynchronously with `queueMicrotask` / `reportError`) instead of dropping it.
- [ ] **[low/api] L440: `simulateContextRestore` returns true and discards the extension even when the restore cannot happen yet**
  - description: `simulate_context_restore` takes `lose_ext` out of its slot, calls `restore_context()` and returns true unconditionally. `restoreContext()` raises INVALID_OPERATION and does nothing unless the `webglcontextlost` event has already been dispatched with its default prevented, and that event is dispatched asynchronously after `loseContext()`. The extension object is gone from the slot by then, and `getExtension` returns null while lost, so it cannot be fetched again.
  - scenario: `ctx.simulateContextLoss(); ctx.simulateContextRestore();` in the same task: the second call returns true, the browser rejects the restore, and the context stays lost for good. A later `simulateContextRestore()` returns false and `simulateContextLoss()` returns false.
  - fix: Keep the extension in the slot until the restored event fires (clear it in the restored handler), and refuse (return false) or defer the restore while the lost event has not been delivered yet (`lost_until_restored` is still false).
- [ ] **[low/api] L440: simulateContextRestore returns true and throws the extension away even when the restore cannot happen yet**
  - description: simulate_context_restore takes lose_ext out of its slot, calls restoreContext() and returns true unconditionally. restoreContext() raises INVALID_OPERATION and does nothing until the webglcontextlost event has been dispatched (asynchronously after loseContext()) with its default prevented. The extension object is gone from the slot by then and getExtension returns null while lost, so it cannot be fetched again.
  - scenario: `ctx.simulateContextLoss(); ctx.simulateContextRestore();` in the same task (a natural way to write a test): the second call returns true, the browser rejects the restore and the context stays lost for good; a later simulateContextRestore() returns false.
  - fix: Keep the extension until the restored event fires (clear it in the restored handler) and return false, or defer the call, while the lost event has not been delivered (lost_until_restored still false).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L532: `Context::new` assumes a pristine, exclusively owned GL context**
  - description: `new` starts from `GlState::default()` (every unit `Empty`, nothing bound) and `reverse_z = false` without issuing any GL call or marking the state unknown. `getContext('webgl2')` returns the same object on a second call, so `from_canvas` on an already-used canvas, or `from_gl` on a context other code has used, creates a second independent `Core` (own pools, own state cache) over the same GL state. Neither function documents or detects this.
  - scenario: (a) Context A on a canvas has `setReverseZ(true)` and is freed; Context B is created on the same canvas (remount / hot reload). B reports `reverseZ() == false`, but GL clip control is still ZERO_TO_ONE because `depth_range::apply` is never run for B, so with a standard projection the near half of clip space is clipped and depth is wrong. (b) `fromCanvas` called twice without freeing the first: each context's cache believes its own program / VAO / texture-unit bindings are current and skips re-binding, so draws use the other context's bindings. (c) `fromGl` on a gl another engine has used: units assumed `Empty` still hold foreign textures, unlike after `resetGlState`, which marks them `Unknown`.
  - fix: In `new`, treat the state as unknown (`state_stale = true`, i.e. the `resetGlState` path, including `depth_range::apply`); document that only one live `Context` per GL context is supported, or reject / reuse on a second creation.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/context_loss.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L108: Reverse-Z stays flagged on after a restore that no longer offers EXT_clip_control**
  - description: The restored handler drops extensions that did not come back (lines 98-107) and then runs `if core.reverse_z.get() { depth_range::apply(&core) }`. `apply` returns silently when `getExtension('EXT_clip_control')` is null, and `core.reverse_z` is left true. The renderer copies that flag every `render` (renderer.rs:137) and keeps flipping depth comparisons and depth clear values, and `ctx.reverseZ()` keeps returning true.
  - scenario: Reverse-Z is on; the context is lost and restored on a backend without EXT_clip_control (software fallback after GPU crashes). Clip space is back to -1..1, but depth funcs are still flipped and clears use `1 - d`, so depth testing is wrong for every pass. The only report is the generic "EXT_clip_control is gone" warning.
  - fix: In the restored handler, if `ExtClipControl` was dropped from the list, set `core.reverse_z` to false and report it, so the app can switch its projection back.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L108: Reverse-Z stays flagged on after a restore that no longer offers EXT_clip_control**
  - description: The restored handler drops extensions that did not come back from loss.extensions, then calls depth_range::apply if core.reverse_z is set. apply returns silently when getExtension gives null, and core.reverse_z stays true. ctx.reverse_z() keeps answering true, so the renderer keeps flipping depth funcs and clears and the scene's cameras keep building zero-to-one reversed projections while the clip range is back at -1..1.
  - scenario: setReverseZ(true); the GPU process crashes repeatedly and the context is restored on a fallback adapter (software) without EXT_clip_control. Every frame after the restore has wrong depth testing (half the depth range clipped, near geometry lost).
  - fix: In the restored handler clear core.reverse_z (and invalidate state, warn) when ExtClipControl is not among the extensions that came back.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L125: Loss/restore listeners are never removed; every created context leaks two closures and its Core allocation**
  - description: `install` adds `webglcontextlost` / `webglcontextrestored` listeners and then calls `lost.forget()` / `restored.forget()`. Nothing calls `removeEventListener`, and `Drop for Core` (context.rs:278) only deletes GL objects. A forgotten wasm-bindgen closure is never freed on the Rust side even if the canvas is collected, and each one holds a `Weak<Core>`, which keeps the `Rc` allocation (the size of `Core`) from being released after the context is dropped. The comment "live as long as the canvas" understates this.
  - scenario: An SPA, hot reload or a remounting component that creates and frees a `Context` repeatedly leaks two closures plus one Core-sized block per cycle. When the same canvas is reused, listeners pile up: after N cycles one context loss calls `preventDefault` N times and logs "[luxel] WebGL context lost" N times (each also pushed into `console::recent`), including for contexts freed long ago.
  - fix: Store the two `Closure`s (and the `EventTarget`) in `Core`/`LossState` instead of forgetting them, and remove the listeners in `Drop for Core`.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L125: Loss / restore listeners are never removed and their closures are forgotten**
  - description: install adds two listeners to the canvas and calls Closure::forget on both. Dropping the Context (Core::drop deletes the GL objects) does not remove them: the Rust closure allocations are never freed and the canvas keeps calling them; they still call preventDefault for a context nobody owns. Context::from_gl / from_canvas on the same canvas again adds another pair.
  - scenario: A single-page app that mounts and unmounts a viewer (new Context per mount on a kept canvas, or new canvases) leaks two closures per mount; after N mounts a context loss runs N lost handlers and N restored handlers.
  - fix: Keep the two Closures (and the EventTarget) in Core / LossState and remove the listeners in Core::drop, as LossEvents does for the scene's listener.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L125: Loss / restore listeners are forgotten, never removed: each Context leaks two closures and its Core allocation, and they pile up on a reused canvas**
  - description: `install` adds `webglcontextlost` / `webglcontextrestored` listeners and calls `lost.forget()` / `restored.forget()`. `Drop for Core` (context.rs:278) deletes GL objects but removes no listener. A forgotten closure is never freed, and each holds a `Weak<Core>`, which keeps the Rc's allocation from being released after the last Context handle is gone. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: A component that creates a Context on mount and frees it on unmount, on the same canvas (getContext returns the same GL context): after N mounts the canvas carries 2N listeners; one context loss logs '[luxel] WebGL context lost' N times and calls preventDefault N times, N-1 of them for contexts freed long ago.
  - fix: Keep the two closures and the EventTarget in `LossState` and call `removeEventListener` in `Drop for Core`.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/copy.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L206: blit_framebuffer skips the format and same-image checks, so rejected blits end as silent GL errors**
  - description: The module header promises that a rejected copy is reported and does nothing. blit_framebuffer validates sample counts and rectangles but never compares formats. blit_framebuffer_attachment (line 265) and the pass resolve (renderer.rs, color and depth) do. Unchecked cases that GL rejects with INVALID_OPERATION: (a) multisampled source whose color format differs from the destination's; (b) depth/stencil mask with differing depth formats, or one side lacking the attachment in a way GL rejects; (c) Linear filter with an integer color source, or integer vs non-integer / signed vs unsigned color; (d) src == dst (WebGL2 forbids reading and writing the same image). blit_framebuffer_attachment has gaps too: it skips the format check when dst is the canvas (line 265 needs both formats), never checks Linear on integer formats, and ignores an antialiased canvas source in its same-rectangle rule (line 259) while blit_framebuffer applies it (line 195).
  - scenario: msaa = framebuffer with an RGBA16F 4x renderbuffer, out = framebuffer with an RGBA8 texture, same size. ctx.blitFramebuffer(msaa, rect, out, rect, color, Nearest) raises GL INVALID_OPERATION, `out` stays unchanged, and luxel prints nothing. Same with a color+depth mask where src has DEPTH24_STENCIL8 and dst DEPTH_COMPONENT24.
  - fix: Factor the format checks of the pass resolve / blit_framebuffer_attachment into one helper (color: same format when the source is multisampled, integer-class match, no Linear on integer; depth/stencil: equal formats; src != dst) and call it from both public blits. Treat the canvas as RGBA8 single- or multi-sampled as elsewhere.
- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L374: copy_texture_sub_image_2d refuses an antialiased canvas source, which WebGL allows**
  - description: For src = NULL (the canvas) the function calls canvas_multisampled() and rejects the copy with "the source is multisampled (resolve it with a blit first)". The GLES 3.0 restriction on copyTexSubImage2D (SAMPLE_BUFFERS > 0 is INVALID_OPERATION) applies only when READ_FRAMEBUFFER_BINDING is non-zero. For the default framebuffer the browser resolves implicitly, exactly as for readPixels; readback.rs:236 says so itself ("an antialiased one is resolved by the browser for the read") and read_pixels accepts the canvas with samples = 0. ContextOptions.antialias defaults to true (context.rs:103), so on a default context this API never works for the canvas. tests/copy.rs only covers it with antialias = false (tests/common/mod.rs:55). Not run in a browser here; based on the spec wording and the readback.rs comment.
  - scenario: Create a context with default options (antialias: true), render, then call ctx.copyTextureSubImage2D(tex, 0, 0, 0, CANVAS, 0, 0, 0, w, h) to grab the frame into a texture. Result: console.error "the source is multisampled", nothing copied, although the raw GL call would succeed.
  - fix: Drop the canvas branch of the multisample check (treat the canvas as single-sampled for reads, as check_read_format does); keep the check only for user framebuffers.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L50: Every blit issues synchronous GL getters (isEnabled, getContextAttributes), including the per-frame pass resolve**
  - description: blit_unscissored calls gl.isEnabled(SCISSOR_TEST) on every blit. canvas_multisampled (line 118) calls getContextAttributes() through Reflect on every call, which allocates a fresh attributes object each time. Both run on the per-frame path: the renderer's resolve calls canvas_multisampled for each pass resolving into the canvas and blit/blit_attachment for each resolved attachment plus depth. MDN's WebGL best practices advise against getters in the frame loop. The renderer already owns a state cache that knows the scissor state, and the antialias attribute cannot change during a context's life.
  - scenario: A scene with MSAA resolve of 2 color attachments + depth into a target, every frame: 3 isEnabled calls per frame; with resolve into the canvas, an additional getContextAttributes object allocation per frame.
  - fix: Cache `antialias` once per context (and refresh on restore). Pass the scissor state from GlState (or unconditionally disable scissor and mark the render state forgotten) instead of querying isEnabled.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L50: Every blit queries GL (isEnabled, getContextAttributes) on the per-frame path**
  - description: blit_unscissored calls gl.isEnabled(SCISSOR_TEST) for each blit, and blit_framebuffer / the pass resolve call canvas_multisampled, which runs getContextAttributes() through Reflect and allocates an attributes object. The scene blits several times a frame: the MSAA resolve of the opaque and late passes (two attachments with the key output), HdrOutput::grab (color, plus depth for see-through), screen_depth for AO, resolve_again, keep_opaque_depth and one peel_layer blit per layer. The renderer already knows the scissor state from its cached render state, and the antialias attribute cannot change during a context's life. Listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed.
  - scenario: HDR with 4x MSAA, AO and one glass mesh: about six blits a frame, each with a synchronous isEnabled; blits that go through blit_framebuffer with the canvas as an end also allocate a context-attributes object each time.
  - fix: Cache antialias per context (refresh on restore); pass the scissor state from GlState into the blit, or disable the scissor unconditionally and forget the render state's scissor.
- [ ] **[low/api] L396: copy_texture_sub_image_2d forwards level, offsets and format to GL unvalidated**
  - description: Only the source region and the 2D target are checked. `level`, `x_offset`/`y_offset` + size against the texture's level size, missing storage, a format incompatible with the source (e.g. RGBA8 source into an RGBA16F or integer texture), and `tex` being attached to `src` itself (feedback, INVALID_OPERATION) all reach copyTexSubImage2D and fail as GL errors. This contradicts the module header ("a rejected copy is reported (console.error) and does nothing").
  - scenario: tex is 64x64 RGBA8; ctx.copyTextureSubImage2D(tex, 0, 60, 0, fb, 0, 0, 0, 16, 16) raises GL INVALID_VALUE with no luxel message. The same happens with level = 3 on a single-level texture.
  - fix: Check level and destination rectangle against the TextureObj's stored size/levels and the color-class compatibility of obj.internal with the source format before the GL call; report through the existing console.error path.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/draw.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L176: instanceCount is silently ignored on rangeElements commands and the check for it is Rust-only**
  - description: DrawCommand.instance_count is a public JS-settable field on every command, but execute issues drawRangeElements for RANGE_ELEMENTS and drops it. is_instanceable sits in a non-wasm_bindgen impl block, so JS cannot ask. Counts are also unvalidated: instanceCount = 0 draws nothing without a message, and a negative count or instanceCount is only a GL INVALID_VALUE.
  - scenario: JS: const c = DrawCommand.rangeElements(Triangles, 0, 99, 300, UnsignedShort, 0); c.instanceCount = 50; with a VAO that has per-instance attributes. One instance is drawn, with no warning.
  - fix: Export isInstanceable, and warn once in execute (or when the command is pushed) if a range-elements command has instance_count != 1. Alternatively fall back to drawElementsInstanced when instance_count > 1.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/feedback.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L352: Transform feedback binding accepts sizes that are not a multiple of 4; GL rejects them and luxel still records the binding**
  - description: set_feedback_binding validates only `offset % 4`. It then always calls bindBufferRange(TRANSFORM_FEEDBACK_BUFFER, index, buf, offset, size) (line 378), including for the whole-buffer call transform_feedback_set_buffer, where size = b.size. GLES 3.0 / WebGL2 generate INVALID_VALUE for bindBufferRange on TRANSFORM_FEEDBACK_BUFFER when size (or offset) is not a multiple of 4. The GL binding is then not made, but luxel stores the Binding and retains the buffer, so Recording::check believes the buffer is bound.
  - scenario: createEmptyBuffer(usage, 1002) (or createBuffer with 1002 bytes), then transformFeedbackSetBuffer(tf, 0, buf); or transformFeedbackSetBufferRange(tf, 0, buf, 0, 10). bindBufferRange raises INVALID_VALUE. At render, Recording::check passes, beginTransformFeedback raises INVALID_OPERATION (no buffer at index 0), the draws run unrecorded, endTransformFeedback raises INVALID_OPERATION. Nothing is captured and luxel reports no reason.
  - fix: Use bindBufferBase for the whole-buffer case (no multiple-of-4 rule), and reject `size % 4 != 0` for the range case with the same console.error path as the offset check.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L170: Recording into a buffer the same draw reads from is not checked, contrary to the "each rule is checked per draw" claim**
  - description: The header says every WebGL2 transform feedback rule is checked per draw with a reason instead of a GL error. Recording::check does not check that the feedback buffers are not also bound elsewhere for the draw (vertex attribute source in the draw's VAO, or a uniform buffer range). WebGL2 rejects any draw using a buffer bound both to the bound transform feedback object and to another binding point with INVALID_OPERATION.
  - scenario: Ping-pong simulation where the swap is forgotten (or an in-place update is attempted): the draw's VAO reads buffer A and the pass's feedback object writes buffer A. Every drawArrays raises INVALID_OPERATION and nothing is written; luxel counts the draw in stats.draws and prints no reason.
  - fix: In check(), compare the binding's buffer ids against the draw's VAO buffers (the VAO already tracks the buffers it retains) and the draw's uniform buffers, and skip with a reason; or remove the claim from the header.
- [ ] **[low/api] L231: The transform feedback primitives query is not run when no draw is recorded, so it keeps the previous frame's count**
  - description: Recording::start begins the TransformFeedbackPrimitivesWritten query only when the first admitted draw is issued. If a pass records nothing (no items, or every draw skipped by Recording::check, e.g. "buffer N would get X bytes but holds Y"), query::begin is never called. The query stays Ready with the previous run's value and its run number is unchanged, so query_result and query_result_async return the old count as if it were current. A pass timer, by contrast, always runs.
  - scenario: Frame 1 records 4 points, query result 4. Frame 2 submits a draw that would overflow the buffer; it is skipped (error_once, which may already have been shown and deduplicated). ctx.queryResult(written) still returns 4 although 0 primitives were written this frame.
  - fix: When the pass has a feedback query and the recording never became active, mark the query's run as finished with 0 (bump run, state Ready(0.0)) in Recording::finish, or document that the query only runs when a draw is recorded.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/float_targets.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L108: canRenderTo answers true for formats that are never color-renderable**
  - description: can_render_to is `float_target_problem(..).is_none()`, and float_need returns None for everything that is not one of seven float formats, including formats WebGL2 never renders to (RGB16F and RGB32F, named in float_need's own comment, SRGB8 from TextureFormat::srgb8(), RGB9_E5, snorm and compressed formats). The same gap means set_color_slot's up-front check lets them through.
  - scenario: An app picks an HDR target with `ctx.canRenderTo(TextureFormat.rgb16f()) ? rgb16f : rgba8` (or srgb8): it gets true, attaches the texture, and the framebuffer is FRAMEBUFFER_INCOMPLETE_ATTACHMENT on every device: exactly the silent failure the function is documented to prevent.
  - fix: Answer from a table of color-renderable sized formats (the WebGL2 list plus the float ones gated by the extensions) and return false for the rest; reuse it in set_color_slot to report 'format is not color-renderable'.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/framebuffer.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L290: Auto-resize sets canvas.width/height inside the ResizeObserver callback, so resized frames are presented blank**
  - description: resize_to_display is called straight from the ResizeObserver callback (line 467) and assigns canvas.width/height there. ResizeObserver callbacks run after requestAnimationFrame callbacks and before paint, and assigning the size clears the drawing buffer. The frame the app rendered in rAF is therefore wiped before it is composited, and nothing redraws it: there is no resize callback for the app, and the scene only notices the new size on the next rAF (on_demand.rs observe).
  - scenario: enableAutoResize(); the user drags the window edge. Each frame: rAF renders at the old size, the observer fires, the canvas is cleared and shown empty (page background or black). The canvas flickers for the whole drag, and one blank frame is shown after every single resize or devicePixelRatio change.
  - fix: In the observer callback only record the device-pixel size (device_size is already stored) and apply it at the start of the next render (e.g. from Context::render / render_passes when the stored size differs from the canvas), or expose a resize callback and document that the app must redraw synchronously in it.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L419: DefaultFramebuffer.setViewport takes its Viewport by value and consumes the JS object**
  - description: `set_viewport(&self, v: Viewport)` takes the struct by value, so the generated glue calls `v.__destroy_into_raw()` (visible in examples/npm-consumer/out/main.js:4272) and the caller's `Viewport` becomes a null-pointer wrapper. The sibling setters take `&Viewport` (`framebufferSetViewport`, `Pass.setViewport`, `Pass.setClearRect`, `blitFramebuffer`) and leave it usable.
  - scenario: `const vp = new Viewport(0, 0, w, h); ctx.defaultFramebuffer().setViewport(vp); pass.setViewport(vp);` throws 'null pointer passed to rust' on the second call. Keeping one `vp` and passing it to `setViewport` on every resize throws the same way from the second resize on.
  - fix: Take `v: &Viewport` (Viewport is Copy), as the other viewport setters do.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L447: Auto-resize on a canvas without a CSS size grows the canvas without bound on HiDPI screens**
  - description: enable_auto_resize sizes the drawing buffer to content box x devicePixelRatio. When the canvas has no CSS width/height, its content box is its width/height attributes, so writing canvas.width changes the observed box, which fires the observer again. Neither the code nor the docs (README.md line 58, npm/README.md line 20, the method's doc comment) require a CSS size or check for one.
  - scenario: `<canvas>` with no CSS size, devicePixelRatio = 2, `ctx.defaultFramebuffer().enableAutoResize()` as in the README: 300x150 -> 600x300 -> 1200x600 ... every frame until the browser clamps the drawing buffer, with 'ResizeObserver loop' errors and the page layout blown up. With setResolutionScale(0.5) at dpr 1 it shrinks to 1x1 instead.
  - fix: Detect the feedback (the observed CSS size equals the previous backing-store size after our own write, or the computed style has width/height auto) and either refuse with an error or pin style.width/height to the current CSS size before the first resize; state the CSS-size requirement in the doc comment and READMEs.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L1065: Invalidating only depth (or only stencil) on a depth-stencil attachment discards both**
  - description: collect_invalidate_attachments pushes DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT whenever the attachment is a combined one and `mask.depth || mask.stencil` is set. invalidateFramebuffer(DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT) makes both planes undefined, so the half the caller did not select is thrown away too. The default-framebuffer path in renderer.rs (lines 519-528) pushes DEPTH and STENCIL separately, so the two targets behave differently for the same mask.
  - scenario: FBO with a Depth24Stencil8 attachment; pass 1 writes a stencil mask and sets InvalidateMask::depth() (stencil is needed by pass 2). On tiled GPUs the stencil contents are undefined in pass 2: the stencil test passes or fails at random, or reads zeros.
  - fix: For a combined attachment push DEPTH_ATTACHMENT when only mask.depth is set, STENCIL_ATTACHMENT when only mask.stencil is set, and DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT only when both are set (all three are legal for invalidateFramebuffer in WebGL2).
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L1065: Invalidating depth alone on a depth-stencil attachment discards the stencil too (and the reverse)**
  - description: collect_invalidate_attachments pushes DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT when the attachment is combined and mask.depth || mask.stencil. InvalidateMask::depth() therefore also throws away the stencil buffer, and InvalidateMask::stencil() the depth, although GL accepts DEPTH_ATTACHMENT or STENCIL_ATTACHMENT alone on a packed attachment. The canvas branch in renderer.rs:519-527 pushes DEPTH and STENCIL separately, so the two targets behave differently for the same mask. Listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed in the code.
  - scenario: Pass 1 into a DEPTH24_STENCIL8 framebuffer writes a stencil mask and sets setInvalidate(InvalidateMask.depth()) because depth is not needed again; pass 2 into the same framebuffer (no clear) tests against that stencil. On a tiled GPU the stencil is undefined after pass 1 and pass 2 draws garbage or nothing; on desktop it happens to work.
  - fix: Push DEPTH_ATTACHMENT when only mask.depth is set, STENCIL_ATTACHMENT when only mask.stencil, and DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT only when both are.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L1128: Attachments are recorded and the previous one released before knowing whether GL accepted the attach**
  - description: set_color_slot / set_depth_slot retain the new source, overwrite the slot, issue framebufferTexture2D / framebufferTextureLayer / framebufferRenderbuffer, and release the previous source, without checking that the attachment kind fits the texture (TextureObj has `target`, which is never looked at here). If GL rejects the call with INVALID_OPERATION, the GL framebuffer keeps the old attachment while the pool says the new one is attached and the old one has been released.
  - scenario: framebufferAttachColorTexture2D(fb, 0, cubeTex, 0), or framebufferAttachColorLayer with a 2D texture, on a framebuffer that already had texture A at slot 0: GL raises INVALID_OPERATION and keeps A. framebufferColorTexture(fb,0) now returns cubeTex, color_format / clear / resolve / readback checks use cubeTex's format (e.g. an integer clear is issued against a float A -> another GL error), passes still draw into A, and if A had been destroyed while attached it is deleted now although the FBO still uses it.
  - fix: Validate before touching state: Texture2D needs a 2D texture, CubeFace a cube, Layer / depth layer a 3D or 2D-array texture (and level/layer in range); on mismatch log and return, as is already done for the float-target check.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L212: Auto-resize observer outlives the Context while any DefaultFramebuffer handle is alive**
  - description: The observer, the media-query listener and both closures are only torn down by disableAutoResize or when the last Rc of DefaultFramebufferInner drops. Context::default_framebuffer() hands out Rc clones (line 360), and Core's Drop (context.rs:278) only deletes GL objects. In JS each `ctx.defaultFramebuffer()` call creates a wrapper holding such a clone, and the README's `ctx.defaultFramebuffer().enableAutoResize()` never frees it, so after `ctx.free()` the observer keeps running until the wrapper is garbage collected (never, where FinalizationRegistry support is off). The inner also keeps the canvas and the gl object alive.
  - scenario: Viewer teardown and re-init on the same canvas (SPA route change, HMR): ctx.free(); Context.fromCanvas(canvas); enableAutoResize(); setResolutionScale(0.5). The old observer still resizes the canvas to scale 1 on every resize event while the new one resizes to 0.5: two reallocations and clears per event, and the new context's viewport/width() can be left describing a size the other observer has just overwritten.
  - fix: In Core::drop (and any explicit destroy) call stop_observing on the default framebuffer's inner and drop both closures, so handles that outlive the context are inert.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L245: Fallback size uses clientWidth/clientHeight (padding included, integer CSS px), not the content box**
  - description: Where the observer entry has no devicePixelContentBoxSize (Safari) and always in the devicePixelRatio-change closure (line 487), device_size uses canvas.clientWidth/Height x dpr. clientWidth includes CSS padding and is rounded to whole CSS pixels, while a canvas draws its buffer into its content box; the entry's contentBoxSize / contentRect, which is available there, is ignored.
  - scenario: Safari, a canvas styled `width:100%; padding:8px` at dpr 2: the buffer is 32 device px wider and taller than the box it is drawn into, so the image is scaled down non-uniformly (slightly blurred, wrong pixel aspect). With a fractional CSS width (e.g. 333.5px at dpr 2) the buffer is off by one device pixel from the box.
  - fix: In the fallback use entry.contentBoxSize[0] (or contentRect) x dpr when an entry is present, and getBoundingClientRect minus padding/border (or the last observed content box) in the dpr closure.
- [ ] **[low/api] L409: DefaultFramebuffer width()/height() are documented as the drawing-buffer size but return the viewport**
  - description: Both getters return `viewport.width/height`, which setViewport overwrites and which is only refreshed by auto-resize. They are used as the canvas size by readback bounds (readback.rs:192), the resolve size check (renderer.rs:386) and the scene's target sizing (js/mod.rs:769, 807, output.rs:1053). For a context from fromGl the viewport starts at 0x0.
  - scenario: (a) No auto-resize, the app sets canvas.width = 1920: width() still returns the size at context creation, the scene keeps rendering into the old-size corner, and reads of the rest of the canvas are rejected as 'outside the target'. (b) setViewport(0,0,w/2,h) for a split view: a resolve into the canvas is skipped with 'sizes must match', and reads from the right half are rejected.
  - fix: Either return gl.drawingBufferWidth/Height from width()/height() (and use those for readback / resolve bounds) and keep the viewport separate, or correct the doc comments to say 'viewport size; call setViewport after resizing the canvas yourself'.
- [ ] **[low/api] L419: DefaultFramebuffer.setViewport takes the Viewport by value and consumes the JS object**
  - description: `set_viewport(&self, v: Viewport)` is exported through wasm_bindgen with a by-value struct parameter, so the generated JS glue calls `__destroy_into_raw()` on the argument. Context::framebuffer_set_viewport (line 1311) and Pass::set_viewport (pass.rs:283) take `&Viewport` and leave the object usable.
  - scenario: JS: `const vp = new Viewport(0,0,w,h); ctx.defaultFramebuffer().setViewport(vp); pass.setViewport(vp);` -> the second call throws 'null pointer passed to rust' (also reading vp.width afterwards), while the same sequence with framebufferSetViewport first works.
  - fix: Change the signature to `v: &Viewport` and store `*v`, like the other two setters (Rust callers in capture.rs and tests pass a reference then).
- [ ] **[low/api] L1260: Framebuffer size and default viewport are creation-time metadata, never reconciled with what is attached**
  - description: create_framebuffer stores width/height and a full-size viewport; attach calls accept any level / texture size without comparing or updating them. size() then bounds readbacks (readback.rs:205) and gates resolves (renderer.rs:385), and `viewport` is what a pass uses by default. (The dropped item in review-style.md is about validating the numbers passed to createFramebuffer, not about this mismatch.)
  - scenario: createFramebuffer(256,256) and framebufferAttachColorTexture2D(fb, 0, tex, 1) to render mip level 1 (128x128): a pass without its own viewport draws with a 256x256 viewport, so only the lower-left quarter of the image lands in the level; a read of region 0,0,256,256 passes the bounds check and returns zeros outside the real 128x128 area instead of the 'outside the target' error.
  - fix: At attach time compute the attachment's size (texture extent >> level, or the renderbuffer size) and either update width/height/viewport from it or report a mismatch with the framebuffer's declared size.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/gpu_info.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L53: ctx.limits() reports the first context's limits, not this context's**
  - description: `limits_js` is an instance method documented as 'What this GPU and browser allow', but it reads the process-wide `OnceLock` in limits.rs, filled once from the first context created. `ctx.gpuInfo()` on the same object does query its own gl.
  - scenario: A page creates a low-power or software context first (a thumbnail canvas, `failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat: false`), then a high-performance one. `second.limits().maxTextureSize` and `maxSamples` report the first GPU's values, and the library's own checks use them too.
  - fix: Keep the limits per Core, or state in the doc and .d.ts that they are those of the first context created.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/interop.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L26: resetGlState does not restore the unpack parameters, and the cube-face upload relies on them**
  - description: reset_gl_state says it puts back 'the state the library expects at its default' and resets the PACK_* parameters and the pixel buffers, but none of UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL, UNPACK_PREMULTIPLY_ALPHA_WEBGL, UNPACK_COLORSPACE_CONVERSION_WEBGL, UNPACK_ALIGNMENT, UNPACK_ROW_LENGTH, UNPACK_SKIP_*, UNPACK_IMAGE_HEIGHT. Every other upload applies a full PixelUnpack and resets it, but texture_sub_image_cube_face (texture.rs:869-895) calls sub_image_2d with whatever is set. That upload is used by Environment::constant (environment.rs:79), i.e. by every SceneRenderer::new and rebuild.
  - scenario: A page shares the context with another engine that leaves UNPACK_ROW_LENGTH or UNPACK_SKIP_PIXELS non-zero (or UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL true), calls ctx.resetGlState() as documented, then creates a Scene or uploads cube faces with textureSubImageCubeFace: the upload fails with INVALID_OPERATION (data too small) or lands flipped / skewed; the scene's default environment stays black.
  - fix: Call PixelUnpack::reset(gl) in reset_gl_state, and apply / reset a default PixelUnpack in texture_sub_image_cube_face like the other raw uploads.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L43: `resetGlState` does an extension lookup and a reflective call every time, even when reverse-Z was never used**
  - description: `reset_gl_state` ends with `depth_range::apply(&self.core)` unconditionally. `apply` calls `gl.getExtension('EXT_clip_control')`, then `Reflect::get(ext, 'clipControlEXT')`, then a dynamic `call2`. `resetGlState` is documented for use after every block of foreign GL calls, which for a shared context means once or more per frame. As a side effect it also enables EXT_clip_control on the GL context for apps that never asked for it, without recording it in the context's extension list.
  - scenario: An app interleaving another engine with luxel calls `resetGlState()` each frame: every frame pays a string-marshalled `getExtension`, a property lookup and a reflective call on top of the nine direct GL calls, with reverse-Z off.
  - fix: Skip the call when `ExtClipControl` is not in the enabled-extension list (GL's clip control is then at its default unless foreign code changed it), and cache the extension object and function in `Core` for the case where it is.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/limits.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L49: Limits are frozen from the first context ever created and never re-queried**
  - description: `LIMITS` is a process-wide `OnceLock` filled by the first `Context::new` (context.rs:533) and never refreshed: not for a second context, and not in the `webglcontextrestored` handler (context_loss.rs:92-117). The header comment's guarantee, "a check can only be stricter than the hardware, never looser", only holds for that first context in its first generation. `ctx.limits()` (gpu_info.rs:53) reads the same global, so it also reports another context's values while its TS doc says "as WebGL reports them".
  - scenario: (a) After GPU-process crashes the browser restores the context on a software backend with lower MAX_SAMPLES / MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE / MAX_UNIFORM_BLOCK_SIZE; the library keeps validating against the old GPU's values, so e.g. a multisampled renderbuffer or large texture passes the up-front check and fails with an opaque GL error instead of the promised Result / console error. (b) Two contexts with different `powerPreference` on a dual-GPU machine: the second is validated with the first one's limits. (c) The first context is already lost when created: every `getParameter` returns null, so the spec minimums (2048 textures, 4 samples, 4 attachments) stick for every later context on the page, and `ctx.limits()` reports them.
  - fix: Keep the limits per `Core` (query in `Context::new`, re-query in the restored handler), or at least re-run the queries on restore and when a new context is created; make `limits_js` read the context's own values.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/param.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L552: paramSetBlockRange stores any offset/size unvalidated; a bad range fails at draw and is cached as bound**
  - description: `param_set_block_range` only warns on a misaligned offset and then stores `(offset, size)` regardless. Negative offset, size <= 0, a misaligned offset, or offset+size past the buffer are passed straight to `bindBufferRange` in `GlBindings::bind_block` (renderer.rs:1056), which raises INVALID_VALUE and leaves the binding point unchanged, while the cache records `(id, range)` as bound, so it is not retried and nothing is reported by luxel. `block_point` (program.rs:570) also does `s as u32` on the size (renderer.rs:882), so a negative size wraps to ~4 billion and passes the 'buffer too small' debug check.
  - scenario: `ctx.paramSetBlockRange(group, field, ubo, 0, 0)` or an offset of 100 on a device with alignment 256 (only a warn_once), or `offset + size > buffer size`. At the first draw `bindBufferRange` raises INVALID_VALUE; the point keeps whatever buffer was there (the shader reads another object's block) or nothing (INVALID_OPERATION on every draw). The cache believes the range is bound, so the same group never re-issues the bind.
  - fix: Validate in `param_set_block_range`: reject (error_once and leave the field unchanged) when offset < 0, size <= 0, offset is misaligned, or offset + size exceeds the buffer's byte size (the buffer is resolvable there); or validate at draw in `apply_group` and treat a bad range as an incomplete draw.
- [ ] **[low/api] L83: ParamLayoutBuilder and UboLayout consume themselves on each call; the other builders mutate in place**
  - description: `ParamLayoutBuilder.uniform/uniformArray/texture/block` take `mut self` and `UboLayout.field/array/structField/structArray` (uniform_buffer.rs:114) take `self`. In JS each call destroys the receiver and returns a new object. `VaoBuilder`, `RenderState`, `Batch`, `PackSpec` and `Pass` use `&mut self` and return nothing. Written in statement style, the consuming ones fail.
  - scenario: `const b = new ParamLayoutBuilder(); b.uniform('u_color', UniformType.Vec4); ctx.createParamLayout(b);` throws 'null pointer passed to rust', because `b` was consumed by the first call. The same pattern with VaoBuilder works.
  - fix: Use `&mut self` for the wasm-exported builder methods (keep the chaining forms for Rust), or document that the result must be chained or reassigned.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L141: uniformArray count is unbounded: `components * count` overflows u32 or allocates gigabytes per group**
  - description: `uniform_array` clamps the count only from below (`count.max(1)`). `ParamLayoutObj::new` computes `(ty.components() * count) as usize` in u32: this panics in debug builds and wraps in release, giving a field whose `words` disagrees with `count` (possibly 0). Without wrap, a large count makes every `create_param_group` call `vec![0; l.words]` with a huge size, aborting the wasm module.
  - scenario: `new ParamLayoutBuilder().uniformArray("u_m", UniformType.Mat4, 0x10000000)`: 16 * 2^28 wraps to 0 words in release (every write then warns 'takes 0 values'), and panics in debug. With count 2^27 the layout is created and `createParamGroup` tries to allocate 8 GiB and traps.
  - fix: Return an error from `create_param_layout` when a count exceeds a sane bound (GL caps a program's uniform components at MAX_*_UNIFORM_VECTORS * 4), and do the multiplication with `checked_mul`.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/pass.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L64: defaultKey returns a BigInt while pushWithKey takes two 32-bit numbers, and KEY_LAST is not exported**
  - description: default_key is exported to JS returning u64, which is a BigInt. push_with_key deliberately avoids BigInt and takes key_hi and key_lo. KEY_LAST is a plain pub const and so is not exported by wasm_bindgen at all. A JS caller who wants the default key with a layer bit has to do BigInt arithmetic and split it per draw.
  - scenario: JS: pass.pushWithKey(defaultKey(p, s, m, v), ...) is a type error (bigint where a number is expected), and there are two parameters to fill. The user ends up writing Number(k >> 32n) and Number(k & 0xffffffffn) for every draw, which is the allocation the hi/lo form was meant to avoid.
  - fix: Export a hi/lo form of the default key (two functions, or one returning the pair written into a reused typed array) and a KEY_LAST_HI constant or getter; or give pushWithKey a flag meaning default key plus last.
- [ ] **[low/api] L65: defaultKey returns a bigint while pushWithKey takes the key as two u32 numbers**
  - description: `defaultKey(...)` returns `u64`, which is `bigint` in the .d.ts. `Pass.pushWithKey(key_hi, key_lo, ...)` deliberately takes two 32-bit numbers ('no BigInt per draw'). The helper that produces the default sort key cannot be fed to the method that consumes keys without BigInt shifting and `Number()` conversions per draw. `KEY_LAST` is not exported.
  - scenario: `pass.pushWithKey(defaultKey(p, s, m, v), ...)` is a type error, and at run time a BigInt-to-u32 conversion throws. A layer bit ORed onto the default key needs `Number(k >> 32n)` and `Number(k & 0xffffffffn)` for every draw.
  - fix: Export the default key as hi/lo numbers (or add `pushWithDefaultKeyLayer`), matching `pushWithKey`'s representation.
- [ ] **[low/api] L283: A reused Pass cannot drop its viewport override or clear rect**
  - description: Pass is documented as reusable across frames and has clearResolve, clearTimer and clearTransformFeedback, but setViewport and setClearRect store Some(..) with no way back to None from JS or Rust (the fields are pub(crate)).
  - scenario: A pass kept across frames renders to a sub-rectangle for split view, then the app returns to full screen. It must track the target size itself and call setViewport on every resize, or build a new Pass and lose its allocations. A clear rect, once set, scissors every later clear of that pass.
  - fix: Add clearViewport and clearClearRect (or make the setters accept an optional rect).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L317: Pass::set_resolve keeps an earlier attachment list; the scene's reused frame pass resolves attachment 1 that no longer exists**
  - description: set_resolve only sets the target; resolve_color and resolve_depth are reset by clear_resolve alone. The scene reuses frame_pass across frames: with contact shadows' key output and MSAA, HdrOutput::prepare_split calls set_resolve_attachments(&[0, 1]) (output.rs:697). When the key output goes away, finish_pass (output.rs:716-717) only calls pass.set_resolve(self.resolve_fb), so the list [0, 1] survives.
