The same stale list reaches DirectTarget::prepare (direct.rs) after setHdr(false) on a canvas without depth. run_pass then takes the per-attachment path (readBuffer / drawBuffers juggling) and reports attachment 1 as missing. Listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed.
  - scenario: samples = 4, no TAA: setContactShadows({}), renderFrame, setContactShadows(null) (or add an opaque mesh with a custom shader so pbr_only is false), renderFrame -> console error '[luxel] resolve: color attachment 1 is missing on the target or the resolve target; skipped', and every later frame resolves color through blit_attachment instead of the single blit.
  - fix: Make Pass::set_resolve reset resolve_color / resolve_depth (attachments are then set after it), or call pass.clear_resolve() before set_resolve in finish_pass and DirectTarget::prepare.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/poller.rs`

- [ ] **[low/async] L70: The no-`setTimeout` fallback fires rounds on the microtask queue, which can never let GPU results arrive**
  - description: When `setTimeout` is missing or the call fails, `schedule` does `spawn_local(async move { me.fire() })`. `fire` wakes every waiter in a microtask; each waiter re-polls, finds its fence / query / link still pending, awaits `tick()` again, and `register` schedules the next round, again as a microtask. Fence, query and link status only change after control returns to the event loop, so the conditions can never become true inside this chain.
  - scenario: In a global without a usable `setTimeout` (the case the comment calls "an odd global"), any `readPixelsAsync`, `queryResultAsync` or `programsReady` that is not ready on first poll spins the microtask queue forever and the thread hangs.
  - fix: Fall back to something that yields a task (`MessageChannel`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `scheduler.postTask`), or fail the waiters with an error rather than busy-looping on microtasks.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/profiler.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L79: time() leaves the pass's previous timer query attached when no query is free**
  - description: `time` only calls `pass.set_timer(q)` when `next_query` returns a query. When it returns None (all `RING_MAX` slots in flight, or `create_query` failed), a pass that lives across frames keeps the query an earlier frame attached. The module header says a full ring "skips timing a frame"; instead `execute_pass` begins that still-pending query again, discarding its outstanding result and bumping `run`, so the slot can only complete with a later run's result and stays in flight longer.
  - scenario: Scene's `frame_pass` (label `opaque`) is reused every frame. On a device where timer results take more than 8 frames, frame 9 gets None from `next_query`, the pass re-runs frame 8's query, and it keeps restarting that same query each frame until another slot frees. The frame is not skipped as documented and one slot's measurement is repeatedly thrown away.
  - fix: In `time`, call `pass.clear_timer()` when `next_query` returns None.
- [ ] **[low/api] L139: Timings of labels that are no longer timed are reported for ever**
  - description: A `Timer` is created on a label's first `time` call and never removed or aged: `milliseconds`, `lastMilliseconds` and `labels` keep returning its last average although no pass has been timed under it since, and its query slots stay allocated until `destroy`. `Scene.gpuTimings()` iterates `labels()` and so presents these as current costs ("GPU milliseconds per pass of recent frames").
  - scenario: With `setGpuProfiling(true)`, enable FXAA or TAA (labels `fxaa`, `motion`) or render glass (`glass`), then turn the feature off or remove the glass meshes. `gpuTimings()` continues to list e.g. `fxaa: 0.4` indefinitely, so a sum of the entries overstates the frame's GPU time.
  - fix: Track the last `update` in which each timer was attached or collected; drop the average (and free the queries) after a few updates without a `time` call for that label.
- [ ] **[low/async] L156: destroy() after a context restore reports every old query as a stale handle**
  - description: `destroy` does not call `resync`, so if the context was lost and restored since the last `update`/`time`, it calls `ctx.destroy_query` on handles the loss already drained. `lost_until_restored` is false again by then, so each goes through `report_stale` as a distinct `error_once` message (and a permanent entry in the dedupe set).
  - scenario: GPU profiling on; the context is lost and restored; before the next `renderFrame` the app calls `scene.setGpuProfiling(false)` or `scene.destroy()`. The console shows one "[luxel] destroy_query: query ... is destroyed or invalid; ignored" error per query slot (labels x slots).
  - fix: In `destroy`, if `self.generation != self.ctx.generation()` just clear `timers` without calling `destroy_query`.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/program.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L181: All parked samplers share one texture unit, so two unsupported sampler arrays of different types fail every draw**
  - description: Unsupported sampler arrays (and samplers past the unit limit) are all parked on unit `max_combined_texture_units() - 1` (lines 181 and 195). The comment says parking avoids 'two sampler types on one unit fail every draw', but parked samplers of different types now meet on that one unit, which is the same GL rule (INVALID_OPERATION at draw when two active samplers of different types point at the same unit). In addition, `unit = samplers.len()` (line 202) counts parked entries, so a real sampler can be assigned unit max-1 itself and collide with the parked ones, and array elements eat units that named samplers could use.
  - scenario: A custom shader declares `uniform sampler2D u_maps[4]; uniform samplerCube u_cubes[2];` (or `sampler2D a[2]` plus `sampler2DShadow b[2]`). Reflection warns 'sampler arrays aren't supported yet' and parks all six elements on unit 31. Every draw with this program then raises INVALID_OPERATION and draws nothing, instead of the arrays just sampling black.
  - fix: Park per sampler type: give each distinct GL sampler type its own parked unit counting down from the top (max-1, max-2, ...), and assign named samplers from a separate counter that excludes parked entries and stops below the lowest parked unit.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L444: Shaders are deleted but never detached, so they live as long as the program**
  - description: `finalize` calls `delete_shader` on both stages and its comment says it 'frees the shader objects'. A shader that is still attached is only flagged for deletion; GL keeps it (source, translated source, compiled binary) until it is detached or the program is deleted. `detach_shader` is never called anywhere in the crate, so every live program holds both of its shader objects for its whole lifetime.
  - scenario: A scene with many PBR variants (each program assembled from the prelude and library chunks, tens of kilobytes of source per stage) keeps two shader objects per program in driver/ANGLE memory until the program is destroyed, although nothing needs them after the link and the info logs have been read.
  - fix: After reading the status and logs in `finalize`, call `gl.detach_shader(&self.raw, shader)` before `delete_shader` for each stage (safe once the link has completed), and correct the comment.
- [ ] **[low/api] L488: Bool compatibility rule ignores component count: an int vector field is silently accepted for a scalar bool uniform**
  - description: `mapping` treats a field as compatible when the shader uniform is `Bool` and the field's scalar kind is Bool/I32/U32, without comparing shape. A layout field declared `IVec3` (or `UVec4`) against `uniform bool u_on` is accepted with no warning; `upload_field` then sends `min(f.words, u.words)` = 1 word via `uniform1iv`, so only the first component reaches the shader. Every other type mismatch gets the 'not uploaded' warning.
  - scenario: Layout: `.uniform("u_flags", UniformType.IVec3)`; shader: `uniform bool u_flags;` (a typo for bvec3). No warning; `paramSetInts(group, f, [0, 1, 1])` uploads only 0 and the shader sees false.
  - fix: Require `f.ty.components() == 1` in the Bool branch (i.e. accept only Bool/I32/U32 scalars for a `bool` uniform); let other shapes fall through to the mismatch warning.
- [ ] **[low/api] L612: Uniform-coverage check warns about parked samplers with an empty name, one warning per array element**
  - description: `reported_unset` and the coverage bits span every entry of `refl.samplers`, including parked ones whose name is "". No layout field can cover them (the lookup at line 506 matches by name), so `check_covered` reports each one as `sampler "" is in none of the draw's parameter groups ... Add it to a group's layout`. `active_names` already filters out the empty names; `check_covered` does not.
  - scenario: Uniform validation is on and a program declares `uniform sampler2D u_maps[4]`. The first draw logs four warnings naming sampler "" and advising to add it to a layout, which is impossible, in addition to the 'sampler arrays aren't supported yet' warning.
  - fix: In `check_covered`, skip samplers with an empty name (mark their bits reported at reflection time), or keep parked samplers in a separate list that takes no coverage bits.
- [ ] **[low/async] L866: programsReady resolves successfully when the context was lost and restored while it waited**
  - description: programs_ready stops waiting as soon as program_link_pending returns false, which it does for a lost context and for stale handles. programs_ready_js then only checks ctx.is_lost() at that moment; unlike readback (PendingRead::alive) and query_result_async it does not compare the epoch. If the poll timer first fires after the restore (timers are throttled to 1 s or more in background tabs), is_lost() is false, every handle is stale, program_error returns None and the promise resolves as 'all linked'.
  - scenario: `await ctx.programsReady([p1, p2])` in a hidden tab; the context is lost and restored before the throttled timer fires. The promise resolves; the app starts drawing with p1 / p2, which are dead handles.
  - fix: Capture core.epoch when the promise is created and reject with ContextLostError if it differs afterwards (as query_result_async does).
- [ ] **[low/async] L872: programsReady resolves successfully when the context was lost and restored while it waited**
  - description: `programs_ready_js` only checks `ctx.is_lost()` after the wait. If the context was lost and restored meanwhile, `is_lost()` is false, every id is stale, `program_error` returns None for each, and the promise resolves as if all programs had linked. The comment says 'Lost meanwhile: nothing linked (every handle is gone)'. Readbacks and queries guard this case with the epoch (readback.rs:462, query.rs:283); this path and `TextureCompressor::when_ready` do not.
  - scenario: `await ctx.programsReady([p])` with parallel compile; a loss and restore happen during the wait. The promise resolves; the app draws with `p`, which is a dead handle, and gets stale-handle errors instead of the documented ContextLostError.
  - fix: Capture `ctx.generation()` before the wait and reject with `Error::context_lost` when it differs afterwards, as `Scene::when_ready` does.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/readback.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L192: Canvas read region is validated against the default framebuffer's viewport, not the drawing buffer**
  - description: bind_read_source takes the canvas size from DefaultFramebuffer::width()/height(), which return `viewport.width/height` (framebuffer.rs:409-416). That viewport is user-settable through DefaultFramebuffer::set_viewport and its x/y are ignored here. With a sub-rectangle viewport, valid pixels are refused. With a viewport larger than the drawing buffer (or a canvas resized without auto-resize), out-of-buffer regions pass validation and readPixels returns untouched zeros with Ok. The same function serves copy_texture_sub_image_2d.
  - scenario: Canvas 800x600; ctx.defaultFramebuffer.setViewport(new Viewport(400, 0, 400, 600)) for a split view; ctx.readPixels(CANVAS, 0, 500, 10, 1, 1, Rgba8) returns Err "region 1x1 at (500, 10) is outside the 400x600 target" although the pixel exists.
  - fix: Validate against gl.drawingBufferWidth/Height (or keep a separate drawing-buffer size in DefaultFramebuffer) instead of the viewport.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L276: Async reads allocate a new GPU buffer per call and copy the bytes twice for JS**
  - description: start_readback / start_buffer_readback run createBuffer + bufferData for every read and delete the buffer afterwards. A per-frame read (hover picking in luxel/src/gpu_pick.rs issues two 1x1 reads per pick) churns two buffers and two fences per frame. On the JS path (read_pixels_promise line 143, buffer_read_promise line 410), the data goes getBufferSubData -> zero-initialised Vec in wasm memory -> Uint8Array::from copy. The sync read_pixels does ArrayBuffer -> to_vec -> a wasm-bindgen copy back to JS (three buffers).
  - scenario: Reading a 1920x1080 RgbaF32 target through readPixelsAsync allocates 33 MB in wasm memory (which never shrinks) plus a 33 MB JS copy; hover picking every pointer move creates and deletes 2 buffers + 2 sync objects per frame.
  - fix: Keep a small pool of pack buffers keyed by size (reuse when len fits) and let one fence cover reads issued in the same call sequence. For the JS promise variants, call getBufferSubData straight into a JS Uint8Array of the final size instead of going through a Vec.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/render_state.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L687: Interned render states are never freed and are found by a linear scan, so per-frame-varying state grows without bound**
  - description: Context::render_state interns by scanning the whole pool with a full RenderState == compare (line 711) and never removes an entry. Scissor rect, stencil ref and polygon offset are part of the state, so every distinct value is a new permanent entry. The doc comment assumes 'an app has a handful'; the style review dropped only the pool-full case as unreachable, not the growth or the scan cost. A NaN polygon offset never equals itself, so each call with one inserts a new state. default_key keeps only 12 bits of the state index, so beyond 4096 states the sort keys alias as well.
  - scenario: A core user clips UI panels with scissor_test and an animated rect (or animates stencil_ref), calling ctx.renderState(state) each frame: 60 new states per second. After 10 minutes there are ~36k entries and every renderState() call compares against all of them. Memory never comes back, and after hours the pool fills and NULL ids are returned. Smaller in-repo case: packages/luxel/src/lighting/maps.rs:315 interns one state per distinct shadow slope/constant bias, so dragging a bias slider leaves one state per value.
  - fix: Take scissor rect (and ideally stencil ref and polygon offset values) out of the interned state and set them per draw or pass, or look states up through a HashMap on a hashable key (f32 as bits) and add a release/refcount. At minimum reject non-finite polygon offsets.
- [ ] **[low/api] L61: Constant blend factors are exposed but the blend colour can never be set**
  - description: BlendFactor offers ConstantColor, OneMinusConstantColor, ConstantAlpha and OneMinusConstantAlpha, but RenderState has no blend colour field and no code in the repo calls gl.blendColor. The constant is therefore always GL's default (0,0,0,0). WebGL also rejects constant-colour and constant-alpha factors mixed as src/dst with INVALID_OPERATION, and nothing validates that.
  - scenario: state.blendSrcRgb = BlendFactor.ConstantAlpha; state.blendDstRgb = BlendFactor.OneMinusConstantAlpha for a cross-fade. The constant alpha is 0, so the source contributes nothing, and the only way to change it is rawGl().blendColor plus resetGlState.
  - fix: Add blend colour fields to RenderState (applied and diffed with the blend state), or remove the four factors. Report the constant-colour/constant-alpha mix at intern time.
- [ ] **[low/api] L446: Polygon offset is not flipped under reverse-Z although the renderer claims to be transparent to render states**
  - description: depth_range.rs says that under reverse-Z 'the renderer makes it transparent to render states' and that states 'keep meaning nearer and far'. Depth funcs and clears are flipped, but apply/apply_diff pass polygon_offset_factor/units through unchanged (lines 446 and 665). Under reverse-Z a positive offset therefore moves fragments toward the camera. The scene crate works around it by hand (lighting/maps.rs:323, sign = -1 under reverse-Z) and bakes the sign into the interned state.
  - scenario: A core user has a decal or shadow-caster state with polygonOffsetFactor = 2 that works, then calls ctx.setReverseZ(true). Depth tests still behave, but the offset now pulls geometry nearer: z-fighting or acne returns, or decals sink. In the scene, a caster state interned before a setReverseZ toggle keeps the old sign.
  - fix: Negate factor and units in apply/apply_diff when reverse_z is set (as as_gl_for does for depth funcs) and drop the manual sign in maps.rs; or document that polygon offset is the caller's to flip.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L476: Per-target blend is applied through getExtension and Reflect on every state change**
  - description: apply_targets calls gl.getExtension by name, then for each override does four Reflect.get lookups by string and four Function.apply calls with freshly collected JS arrays. It runs on every switch to or from a state with overrides (apply, and apply_diff whenever blend_differs).
  - scenario: A weighted-blended OIT or G-buffer decal pass alternating between two states with two per-target overrides each: every state switch costs one getExtension, 8 Reflect.get and 8 apply calls with 8 array allocations, on top of the GL calls themselves, per switch per frame.
  - fix: Fetch the extension object and its four functions once (when the extension is enabled, again on restore) and store them in the Core or GlState; call them with call2/call3 and a small bound helper instead of building arrays.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/renderbuffer.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L36: RenderbufferFormat::Stencil8 is exposed but there is no way to attach it**
  - description: DepthAttachment::attachment_point (framebuffer.rs:695) only yields DEPTH_ATTACHMENT or DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT; nothing ever uses STENCIL_ATTACHMENT (no occurrence in the crate). A STENCIL_INDEX8 renderbuffer is valid only at STENCIL_ATTACHMENT.
  - scenario: createRenderbuffer(Stencil8, w, h) succeeds; framebufferAttachDepthRenderbuffer or framebufferAttachDepthStencilRenderbuffer with it leaves the framebuffer FRAMEBUFFER_INCOMPLETE_ATTACHMENT, and every pass into it fails. There is no call that makes it work.
  - fix: Add a stencil-only attach (STENCIL_ATTACHMENT) with matching clear / invalidate handling, or remove Stencil8 from the enum. Checking the renderbuffer format against the attach kind (depth vs depth-stencil) at attach time would also catch e.g. Depth24 passed to the depth-stencil call.
- [ ] **[low/api] L189: Multisample renderbuffers record the requested sample count; 0 is silently turned into 1**
  - description: create_renderbuffer_multisample passes `samples.max(1)` and stores that number. renderbufferStorageMultisample rounds a request up to the next supported count, so the stored value can differ from the real one (RENDERBUFFER_SAMPLES is never queried), and a request for 0 or 1 becomes a renderbuffer the library treats as multisampled because every check is `samples > 0`.
  - scenario: createRenderbufferMultisample(Rgba8, 0, w, h) (e.g. an app's 'MSAA off' setting passed straight through): renderbufferSamples() returns 1, readPixels on it is refused with 'multisampled (1x); resolve it first', and the resolve path applies the same-format rule. createRenderbufferMultisample(Rgba8, 3, ...) on a device offering 2/4/8: GL allocates 4 samples, renderbufferSamples() says 3 and resourceStats undercounts by a quarter.
  - fix: Treat samples <= 1 as a plain renderbufferStorage with samples = 0 (or return an error for 0), and store the actual count read back with getRenderbufferParameter(RENDERBUFFER_SAMPLES) after allocation.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/renderer.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L241: Every pass with a clear rect forgets the render state, forcing a full state re-apply per shadow tile**
  - description: When clear_rect is set, the pass enables the scissor, sets the rect, and then forgets the render state even when the current state is known (the match arm at 241-244). The first draw of the pass then goes through RenderState::apply: about 25 GL calls plus per-target calls. The masks and the scissor could be put back from cur, as the non-rect path does.
  - scenario: The scene's shadow atlas (lighting/maps.rs:309 sets a clear rect on every tile pass): with 4 cascades plus a few spot or point faces, that is 10-20 passes per frame, each re-issuing the complete caster state although it is identical from pass to pass. Several hundred redundant GL calls per frame; frameStats.states counts them.
  - fix: After a rect clear with a known cur, call opened.restore and re-issue cur's scissor enable and box (2 calls) instead of forget_render_state.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L241: A rect clear always forgets the render state: every shadow tile pass re-applies the full state**
  - description: After a clear with clear_rect the match at 241-244 calls forget_render_state even when the current state is known, so the pass's first draw goes through RenderState::apply (about 25 GL calls) instead of a diff. lighting/maps.rs:309 sets a clear rect on every shadow view's pass, and all of a light's views share one caster state, so the state that is already applied is issued again in full for each tile. Listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed.
  - scenario: A directional light with 4 cascades plus a point light (6 faces): 10 shadow passes a frame while casters move, each issuing the complete depth / blend / cull / stencil / scissor / polygon-offset state although it equals the previous pass's; frameStats().states counts them.
  - fix: With a known `cur`, restore the masks (opened.restore) and put back cur's scissor enable and box after the rect clear instead of forgetting the state.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L286: Float-blend guard skips valid draws when per-target overrides turn blending off on the float32 attachment**
  - description: Without EXT_float_blend, a draw is skipped when any colour attachment is 32-bit float and RenderState::blends() is true. blends() (render_state.rs:321) is self.blend || any target blends; it does not look at which attachment blends. GL only raises the error when blending is enabled for a draw buffer that is itself 32F.
  - scenario: MRT target with RGBA8 at 0 and R32F at 1 on a device with OES_draw_buffers_indexed but no EXT_float_blend. State has blend = true plus setTargetBlend(1, {blend: false}). GL accepts this draw, but the renderer skips it and warns 'blending into a 32-bit float target needs EXT_float_blend'.
  - fix: Evaluate per attachment: for each colour attachment that is float32, take the effective blend flag (the override for that index, else self.blend) and skip only if one of those is on.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L372: FrameStats counters use plain += and overflow if resetFrameStats is never called**
  - description: Every stats field is a u32 incremented with += (draws, uniforms, textures, skipped += items.len() and so on). The counters are reset only by Context::reset_frame_stats, which nothing in the scene crate calls. The dev profile keeps overflow checks on, so a wrap panics inside run_pass while the res and gl_state RefCells are borrowed. In release it wraps silently.
  - scenario: A dev-build scene app left running: at about 100k uniform uploads or draws per second the counter passes 2^32 after roughly 12 hours, giving 'attempt to add with overflow', a wasm trap in the middle of render, and every later call failing. In release, frameStats() jumps back to small numbers.
  - fix: Use wrapping_add or saturating_add for the counters, or reset them at the start of each render call and expose per-render stats.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L395: Resolve and store ops call GL getters and allocate JS objects every frame**
  - description: For a pass that resolves into the canvas, the match guard calls copy::canvas_multisampled, which is getContextAttributes() via Reflect and allocates a fresh attributes object on every render. Each blit goes through copy.rs:50 blit_unscissored, which calls gl.isEnabled(SCISSOR_TEST) although the renderer already knows the scissor state from its cached render state. The invalidate step allocates a js_sys::Array per pass (line 515), and the attachments path allocates one per blit for drawBuffers (copy.rs:94) and again in sync_draw_buffers.
  - scenario: The common MSAA setup (render into a multisampled framebuffer, pass.setResolve(NULL), setInvalidate): every frame does one getContextAttributes call with an object allocation, one or two isEnabled queries and one array allocation. A G-buffer pass with 4 resolved attachments and depth does 5 isEnabled calls and about 9 array allocations per frame.
  - fix: Cache the context's antialias attribute once (it cannot change, and can be re-read on restore). Pass the known scissor state into the blit or disable from the cached render state. Keep the invalidate and drawBuffers arrays on the FramebufferObj and rebuild them only when attachments change.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L411: Default resolve path (no setResolveAttachments) skips the checks the other blit paths make**
  - description: With resolve_color empty, run_pass calls copy::blit directly after only a size check. The listed-attachments branch (lines 440-456) checks that a multisampled source and the destination have the same format, and Context::blit_framebuffer checks that the destination is not multisampled. The default path checks neither, nor that dst differs from the pass target. GL then raises INVALID_OPERATION with no luxel message, yet blitted is set, so the invalidate goes through READ and the cache is moved to dst as if the resolve had happened.
  - scenario: pass = new Pass(msaaFb) with an RGBA16F multisampled renderbuffer; pass.setResolve(fbWithRGBA8Texture); pass.setInvalidate(color). Each frame blitFramebuffer fails with INVALID_OPERATION, the MSAA colour is then invalidated, and the resolve texture stays empty with only a raw GL error in the console. Same for a multisampled dst, or dst equal to the pass target.
  - fix: Run the same format, destination-samples and dst != target checks before the default blit (share one helper with blit_framebuffer and the attachments branch), and set blitted only when a blit was actually issued.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L411: The default resolve path blits unchecked, then invalidates the source and moves the cache as if it had worked**
  - description: With an empty resolve_color, run_pass sets blitted = true and calls copy::blit after a size check only. The attachment-list branch (lines 440-456) checks that a multisampled source and the destination share a format, and Context::blit_framebuffer refuses a multisampled destination; this path checks neither, nor dst == target. When GL rejects the blit (INVALID_OPERATION), the store op still invalidates the multisampled color through READ_FRAMEBUFFER (line 530) and st.target is set to the resolve target, so the frame's color is gone with no luxel message. Listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed.
  - scenario: pass = new Pass(msaaFb) where msaaFb holds an RGBA16F 4x renderbuffer; pass.setResolve(fbWithRgba8Texture); pass.setInvalidate(InvalidateMask.color()). Every frame: a raw GL error only, the resolve texture stays empty, and the MSAA color is discarded.
  - fix: Share one check (same format for a multisampled source, single-sampled destination, dst != target) between this path, the attachment-list path and blit_framebuffer; report with console::error_once and leave blitted false when it fails.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L786: Every draw re-diffs every field of every group, including groups shared by consecutive draws**
  - description: apply_group walks all plain fields (a slice compare against the program's shadow), all textures and all blocks of each of up to four groups for every draw. There is no per-(program, slot) memo of the group id and a change counter, so a frame group and a material group shared by a run of sorted draws are compared in full each time, although sorting exists to make those runs.
  - scenario: 2000 sorted draws sharing one program, one frame group (about 20 fields) and a few materials (about 40 fields, 8 textures): around 120k slice compares, plus per-texture pool lookups and binding checks, each frame, with nearly all of them finding nothing to do.
  - fix: Give ParamGroupObj a change counter bumped by its setters; remember per program and slot the (group id, counter) last applied and skip the plain-field loop when both match. The texture and UBO cache checks can stay, or be skipped the same way when no upload or foreign bind happened in between.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L837: Once-only warnings on the draw path format their message for every draw of every frame**
  - description: The float32 with linear sampler warning builds its message with format! (a heap String) before console::warn_once looks it up in the dedup HashSet, so the cost is paid per affected texture per draw indefinitely, plus a second textures.get for the same handle. The stale-handle reports in apply_group and in the draw loop do the same (lines 812-816, 847-851, 871-875). Because those messages embed the handle, the dedup set in console.rs also gains an entry per distinct stale handle and never shrinks.
  - scenario: A phone without OES_texture_float_linear where a shader samples an RGBA32F texture through a linear sampler in 500 draws: 500 string formats and hash lookups per frame for a warning shown once. Likewise a destroyed texture left in a material group used by many draws.
  - fix: Check a cheap flag first (a bool on the sampler/texture pair or the group, or a 'reported' bit) and only build the string on first sight; resolve the texture object once and reuse it for the float32 test.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/shader_source.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L80: Include-once ignores preprocessor conditionals: a chunk first included inside a false #if is dropped everywhere else**
  - description: `expand` pastes a chunk at its first `#include` in text order and drops every later `#include` of the same name (`self.files` lookup). It works on raw lines, with no knowledge of `#if/#ifdef` blocks or block comments. If the first textual include sits in a conditional that evaluates false (or inside `/* ... */`), the chunk body is compiled out and later unconditional includes are silently removed. The module doc promises 'chunks needn't guard themselves' without stating this limit. No shader in the repo does this today, but `shader_sources`/`createProgram`-style user code with defines can.
  - scenario: User code: `#ifdef USE_FOG\n#include "fog.glsl"\n#endif\n...\n#include "fog.glsl"` (or chunk A includes `common.glsl` under `#ifdef X` and chunk B includes it unconditionally). With USE_FOG undefined the only copy of fog.glsl is inside the dead branch; the second include is replaced by a `#line`, and the compile fails with 'undeclared identifier' errors pointing at code that visibly includes the chunk.
  - fix: Either track conditional depth and only dedupe includes seen at depth 0 (inside a conditional, wrap the pasted chunk in its own `#ifndef LUXEL_CHUNK_n / #define` guard and do not mark it as included), or emit every chunk with a generated `#ifndef` guard and let the GLSL preprocessor do the once-only; at minimum document the limit and reject `#include` inside a conditional.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L80: A chunk whose name equals the main file's name is silently never included**
  - description: The include-once test is `self.files.iter().any(|f| f == name)`, and `files[0]` is the main file's display name (`name` passed to `assemble`, e.g. "vertex", "fragment", "present.frag"). An `#include` of a chunk with that same name is treated as already included and replaced by a `#line`, with no error.
  - scenario: `ShaderSource::assemble(prefix, &[], "common.glsl", code, chunk)` where `code` contains `#include "common.glsl"` (an app naming its main source after the file it came from while also registering a chunk under that name): the chunk is dropped and the compile fails with undeclared identifiers.
  - fix: Dedupe against `self.files[1..]` only (track included chunk names separately from the main file's label), or return an error when a chunk name equals the main name.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/texture.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L1679: Image load with generate_mipmaps allocates a full mip chain before checking the format can have mipmaps generated**
  - description: Both load paths (drive_image_load at 1674-1691 and fill_from_bitmap at 1605-1624) compute levels = mip_levels(w, h), allocate immutable storage with that many levels, fill level 0, then call texture_generate_mipmaps. That function refuses (mipmap_problem, line 1460) for any format that is not colour-renderable and filterable: SRGB8 (0x8C41 is deliberately absent from the list at 1464), RGB16F, RGB32F, 16F/32F/R11F on a device without the colour-buffer-float extensions, 32F without OES_texture_float_linear. The texture is then left with levels 1..n allocated but zero-filled, and on_load still reports (w, h) as success. In-repo callers only pass rgba8 / srgb8Alpha8 so they are not affected; the public textureLoadImage / textureLoadBytes / textureLoadBlob are.
  - scenario: ctx.textureLoadImage(tex, url, TextureFormat.srgb8(), true, false, cb) on a texture created with LinearMipmapLinear: one console error 'format 0x8C41 can't have mipmaps generated', cb(w, h) signals success, and the texture fades to black as soon as it is minified (levels >= 1 are zeros). Same for rgb16f(), or rgba32f() on a phone without OES_texture_float_linear.
  - fix: Call mipmap_problem(format.internal) before allocate_2d in both paths; when it reports a problem, log it once and allocate a single level (or fail the load with (0, 0)) instead of allocating a chain that will never be filled.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L397: Storage is recorded as allocated when GL rejects texStorage (wrong target, non-square cube, compressed format without its extension)**
  - description: storage_fits checks sizes and level count only, then every storage function sets obj.extent and obj.internal unconditionally (lines 461-463, 664-666, 684-686, 704-706). It does not check that the call matches the texture's target (texStorage2D on a Texture3D/Texture2DArray texture, texStorage3D with depth 1 on a Texture2D texture: INVALID_ENUM / INVALID_OPERATION), that a cube map is square (INVALID_VALUE), or that a compressed format's extension is enabled or legal for the target (S3TC/ETC2/ASTC on TEXTURE_3D is INVALID_OPERATION although the doc at 691 offers TEXTURE_3D). After the GL error the object still claims storage: a corrected retry is refused with 'is already allocated (immutable storage is one-shot)', region_fits lets sub-image uploads through to more GL errors, stats.rs:167 counts memory that does not exist, allocate_2d returns true so an image load reports success, and texture_compressor reads the bogus extent/internal. This is not the dropped 'textureStorage* can't report failure' item: the problem here is the library's own bookkeeping going wrong, not the return type. texture_sub_image_cube_face (881) likewise never checks the texture is a cube map.
  - scenario: const t = ctx.createTexture(TextureTarget.Texture2DArray, ...); ctx.textureStorage2D(t, 1, TextureFormat.rgba8(), 64, 64) (wrong call for the target) -> GL INVALID_ENUM; the corrected ctx.textureStorage3D(t, 1, fmt, 64, 64, 4) is now refused as 'already allocated' and the handle is unusable. Or textureCompressedStorage2D with an ASTC format on a desktop without the extension: INVALID_ENUM, but internal = 0x93B0 is recorded and stats report the texture's size.
  - fix: In storage_fits take which call it is (2D vs 3D) and reject a target mismatch and a non-square cube with a message; for compressed storage check the format's extension is enabled (and reject TEXTURE_3D for the 2D-array-only families); or simply only set extent/internal when gl.getError() is NO_ERROR for the compressed/extension-dependent case. Fix the doc at line 691.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L514: 32F linear-filter warning does two synchronous getTexParameter reads and only looks at the filters at allocation time**
  - description: storage_allocated queries TEXTURE_MIN_FILTER and TEXTURE_MAG_FILTER back from GL (a synchronous round trip each) for every 32-bit float texture allocated on a device without OES_texture_float_linear, although the library set both values itself in create_texture / texture_set_*_filter and could keep them in TextureObj. Because the check runs only inside texStorage, the common order 'allocate, then set filter' is never checked: texture_set_min_filter / texture_set_mag_filter to Linear after storage gives no warning and the texture samples as black.
  - scenario: On a phone without OES_texture_float_linear: createTexture(Texture2D, Nearest, Nearest); textureStorage2D(t, 1, rgba32f, ...); textureSetMagFilter(t, Linear) -> incomplete texture, black samples, no warning. Conversely each 32F allocation there stalls on two getTexParameter calls.
  - fix: Store min/mag filter in TextureObj (set in create_texture and the two setters) and run the same check from both storage_allocated and the filter setters, without reading GL back.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L760: Every upload issues 18 pixelStorei calls, also when the unpack settings are the defaults**
  - description: Each raw / DOM upload does unpack.apply(gl) (9 pixelStorei) and PixelUnpack::reset(gl) (9 more) around one texSubImage call (lines 760/764, 825/862, 996/1008, 1032/1044, 1062/1074, 1092/1104, 1122/1134), on top of a bind and unbind. The library's invariant is that unpack state is at its defaults between calls (texture_sub_image_cube_face and the compressed uploads already rely on it and call neither), so for PixelUnpack::default() all 18 calls are redundant, and for other settings only the differing fields need setting and restoring.
  - scenario: A video texture updated with textureSubImage2DFromVideo every frame: 18 redundant GL state calls per frame per video. A KTX2 uncompressed texture with 12 mip levels (luxel-gltf ktx2.rs:155): 216 pixelStorei calls for 12 uploads.
  - fix: In apply/reset set only the parameters that differ from PixelUnpack::default() (compare field by field), so a default unpack costs zero calls and reset undoes only what apply changed.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L760: Default uploads issue 18 pixelStorei calls and leave the active unit Unknown; the scene pays this every frame**
  - description: texture_sub_image_2d goes through texture_sub_image_2d_with, which applies a PixelUnpack (9 pixelStorei) and resets it (9 more) even when it is the default and nothing changed. with_texture also marks the active unit Unknown (forget_texture_unit, line 485), so the next bind on that unit first unbinds the three other targets (renderer.rs:1008-1013). The scene uploads its instance texture on every frame whose matrices changed (render.rs:987-989 -> instances.rs:214), and HdrOutput::grab adds four texParameter round trips through with_texture per copy (output.rs:769-780).
  - scenario: Any animated scene: each renderFrame issues 18 redundant pixelStorei calls for the instance upload plus 3 bindTexture(null) on the unit that was active, and with glass another handful of unit resets from the base / max level changes.
  - fix: Track the unpack state in the context (or skip apply + reset when the PixelUnpack is the default and the tracked state is default); in with_texture, since the texture is unbound again at the end, record the unit as 'this target empty, others unchanged' instead of Unknown.
- [ ] **[low/api] L825: textureSubImage3DWith accepts flipY / premultiplyAlpha, which WebGL2 rejects for 3D uploads from an ArrayBufferView**
  - description: texture_sub_image_3d_with applies the whole PixelUnpack, including UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL and UNPACK_PREMULTIPLY_ALPHA_WEBGL, and then calls texSubImage3D with an ArrayBufferView. WebGL 2 generates INVALID_OPERATION for texImage3D/texSubImage3D from an ArrayBufferView or PBO when either flag is set (Chrome: 'FLIP_Y or PREMULTIPLY_ALPHA isn't allowed for uploading 3D textures'). The web-sys call returns Ok, so luxel prints nothing and the region is simply not written. The doc ('with explicit pixel-unpack settings') and PixelUnpack.flipped() suggest it works as in the 2D variant.
  - scenario: ctx.textureSubImage3DWith(arr, 0, 0, 0, layer, w, h, 1, TextureFormat.rgba8(), bytes, PixelUnpack.flipped()) to fill an array layer from flipped rows: GL INVALID_OPERATION, layer stays black, no luxel message.
  - fix: Reject (console error, skip) a PixelUnpack with flip_y or premultiply_alpha in texture_sub_image_3d_with, or flip the rows on the CPU before the upload; say so in the doc.
- [ ] **[low/api] L881: Cube-face upload is the only raw upload with no PixelUnpack and it neither applies nor resets unpack state**
  - description: texture_sub_image_cube_face calls sub_image_2d directly with whatever unpack state GL currently has. There is no ...With variant, so UNPACK_ALIGNMENT is always 4: R8/RG8/RGB8 faces whose row size is not a multiple of 4 cannot be uploaded from tightly packed bytes (GL INVALID_OPERATION for a too-short buffer, silent in luxel), unlike textureSubImage2DWith. And since it does not apply defaults, unpack state left by the user's own rawGl calls leaks into it; resetGlState (interop.rs:28-43) restores the pack parameters but none of the unpack ones.
  - scenario: A 6x6 RGB8 cube face with 108 tightly packed bytes: GL needs 6 rows of 20 bytes (minus padding on the last) and raises INVALID_OPERATION; the face stays black with no luxel message and no way to pass alignment 1. Or: user code sets gl.pixelStorei(UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL, true) on rawGl, calls resetGlState(), then uploads cube faces: they arrive flipped.
  - fix: Give the cube-face upload an unpack parameter (or a With variant) and wrap it in apply/reset like the 2D path; have reset_gl_state also call PixelUnpack::reset.
- [ ] **[low/async] L1536: createImageBitmap path has no fallback to the <img> path when fetch or decode is refused**
  - description: When createImageBitmap exists (every current browser), texture_load_image goes through fetch() + createImageBitmap(blob, options) and never uses drive_image_load. Two environment differences turn into a hard load failure although the <img> path kept in the file would succeed: (1) fetch is governed by CSP connect-src, an <img> by img-src, so a page whose policy allows the image host only under img-src loses every URL texture; (2) decode_bitmap always passes imageOrientation: 'from-image' (line 1536); engines that predate that enum value reject the call with a TypeError for an invalid enum member (they only know 'none' | 'flipY'), so every load fails there even with flip_y = false, where the option is not needed at all. The failure is only a console error and on_load(0, 0).
  - scenario: Page served with Content-Security-Policy: img-src https://cdn.example; connect-src 'self'. scene.loadTexture('https://cdn.example/a.png') -> fetch blocked, '[luxel] image failed to load', texture stays empty; the same URL in an <img crossOrigin> loads.
  - fix: Only set imageOrientation when flip_y is true (the default is already the wanted behaviour), and when the fetch or the decode rejects for a URL source, fall back to drive_image_load with an HtmlImageElement (where one exists) before reporting failure.
- [ ] **[low/api] L1591: Exceptions thrown by the on_load callback are silently discarded**
  - description: All three places that invoke the user's callback do 'let _ = on_load.call2(...)' (lines 1591, 1707, 1806). A JS exception thrown inside the callback is caught by wasm-bindgen's catch wrapper, returned as Err and dropped: nothing reaches the console or window.onerror, and the caller's code after the throwing statement just does not run.
  - scenario: ctx.textureLoadImage(tex, url, fmt, true, false, (w, h) => { materal.ready = true; }) with a typo: the ReferenceError never appears anywhere; the app sits waiting for a texture that loaded fine.
  - fix: On Err(e) report it (console::error with the exception, or rethrow asynchronously via queueMicrotask/setTimeout so it reaches window.onerror) in one shared helper used by the three call sites.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L1785: Non-UNSIGNED_BYTE uploads copy the whole pixel buffer a second time**
  - description: pixel_view builds the typed array with js_sys::Uint8Array::from(data).buffer(), a full copy into a new JS ArrayBuffer, although the data was already copied from JS into wasm memory by wasm-bindgen for the &[u8] parameter. The UNSIGNED_BYTE and compressed paths use a zero-copy view of wasm memory; float, half-float, depth and packed formats pay an extra allocation and memcpy of the full image per call.
  - scenario: Uploading a 2048x1024 RGBA32F environment or a per-frame RGBA16F data texture via textureSubImage2D: 32 MB (or the per-frame buffer) is allocated and copied once more in JS before texSubImage2D, then left to the GC.
  - fix: When data.as_ptr() is aligned to the element size (wasm allocations normally are), create the typed array as a view over wasm memory (Float32Array / Uint16Array / Uint32Array ::view on a slice cast, or new_with_byte_offset_and_length on the wasm memory buffer) for the duration of the call; keep the copy only as the unaligned fallback.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/texture_compressor.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L440: The shared texture compressor pins its Context for the life of the page; only Scene::destroy lets go, and a later load pins it again**
  - description: `SHARED` is a thread_local `Option<(Context, u32, Rc<TextureCompressor>)>`, and the compressor itself also holds a `Context` clone. `Core::drop` (context.rs:278, which deletes every GL object when the last handle goes) can therefore never run once `TextureCompressor::shared(ctx)` has been called. The only release is `release_shared`, which sits in a plain `impl` block (not exported to JS) and is called from exactly one place, `Scene::destroy` (luxel/src/js/mod.rs:1240). luxel-gltf calls `shared` for every textured model: `compress::after_load` does `kind.zip(compressor(ctx))` (luxel-gltf/src/compress.rs:139), which evaluates `compressor(ctx)` eagerly even when `kind` is None, so any model with one texture builds and pins the compressor while texture compression is on (the default). Because `release_shared` is tied to one Scene's teardown, it also drops the compressor another Scene on the same context still uses. (The single slot is mentioned in docs/review-night-raw.md as a low perf item; the pinning and the re-pin after destroy are confirmed here against the code.)
  - scenario: (a) Core + loader without a Scene (the crate doc's and examples/gltf's use): `const m = await loadModel(ctx, url); ... m.destroy(ctx); ctx.free()` -> the Core is still referenced from SHARED, so `delete_all` never runs: the encoder programs, the table texture, the sampler and the WebGL context itself stay alive until the page unloads, and JS has no call to release them. (b) With a Scene: `scene.destroy()` while a `loadModel` is still downloading; when it finishes, the build calls `compressor(ctx)` -> `shared` makes a new compressor and stores a new Context clone; the app destroys the model and drops ctx, but nothing ever calls `release_shared` again.
  - fix: Keep the compressor on the context instead of beside it (e.g. an `Option<Rc<TextureCompressor>>` slot in `Core`, with the compressor holding a `Weak<Core>`), or hold the context weakly in SHARED and key by context; at minimum export a release to JS and make `compress::after_load` call `compressor(ctx)` only when `kind` is Some.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L266: The 2D BC6H encoder is never precompiled and whenReady() does not cover it, so the first 2D Hdr compress blocks on compile and link**
  - description: The constructor precompiles the Hdr encoder only with `cube = true` (lines 266-268). A 2D HDR texture uses the key `(0x8E8F, Hdr, false)`, a different program (`#define BC6H` instead of `CUBE`). That program is created lazily inside `encode()` (line 648) and immediately forced with `ctx.program_linked` (line 653), which blocks on the link. `when_ready()` (line 301) only lists encoders that already exist, so awaiting it does not help. Its doc says "Resolves once the encoders have compiled; compress before then waits", which implies that after it resolves compress no longer blocks.
  - scenario: `await compressor.whenReady(); compressor.compress(hdr2dTexture, CompressKind.Hdr)` on a desktop with BPTC. The 550-line BC6H shader is compiled and linked synchronously inside compress(), a main-thread stall of the kind whenReady exists to avoid.
  - fix: Precompile the 2D BC6H encoder in `new` as well when BPTC is present. Or have whenReady take the kind/target so it can create and await that encoder. Or correct the doc to say only the 2D LDR encoders and the cube HDR encoder are covered.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L440: The 'one compressor per context' cache is a single slot: two contexts loading models evict each other's encoders**
  - description: `SHARED` holds one `(Context, generation, Rc<TextureCompressor>)`. `shared(ctx)` for another context replaces it (line 457). The evicted compressor stays alive only as long as in-flight `compress_when_ready` tasks hold their `Rc`, then `Drop` destroys its encoder programs. The next model built on the first context calls `TextureCompressor::new` again, which creates the tables texture and compiles every encoder program (the loops at lines 258-268). `luxel-gltf/src/compress.rs::compressor` calls `shared` for each texture of each model build, and `Scene::destroy` calls `release_shared`.
  - scenario: A page with two viewers (two canvases, two contexts) loading models alternately, such as a product grid: each model load on one canvas throws away the other's compiled encoders and compiles its own set again, several program links per load, and several compressors exist at once while their compress tasks are pending.
  - fix: Key the cache by context (a small Vec/map of `(Context, generation, Rc)`, pruned on `release_shared` and on generation change), or hang the compressor off the context's `Core`.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L447: SHARED holds one compressor for the whole thread, so two contexts evict each other and recompile all encoders**
  - description: `SHARED` is a single `Option<(Context, generation, Rc<TextureCompressor>)>`. `shared(ctx)` for a context other than the stored one builds a new TextureCompressor (lines 456-457) and overwrites the slot. `TextureCompressor::new` compiles every encoder the device has: up to five BC or ETC programs plus the BC6H cube encoder. The comment says "One compressor per context", but it is one per thread. The evicted compressor is also dropped inside `slot.borrow_mut()` (the assignment on line 457), while `release_shared` deliberately drops outside the borrow (lines 473-474). The two paths disagree. Separately, the slot is only cleared by `release_shared`, which is not exported to JS and is called only from Scene::destroy (packages/luxel/src/js/mod.rs:1240). A page that uses luxel-gltf's loader without a Scene keeps the Context clone, the encoder programs and the table texture alive for the life of the page.
  - scenario: Two canvases, each with its own Context, loading glTF models alternately (A1, B1, A2, B2). Each model's first `compress::compressor(ctx)` call (packages/luxel-gltf/src/compress.rs:51) sees the other context in the slot, discards its compressor and recompiles all encoder programs. Every load pays the full encoder compile again, and `whenReady` waits on it each time.
  - fix: Key the shared compressors by context, for example a small Vec or HashMap of (Context, generation, Rc), pruned on generation mismatch and by release_shared. Or store the Rc on the Context itself. Take the old value out of the slot and drop it after the borrow ends, as release_shared does. Export a JS-visible release, or release when the context is destroyed, for users without a Scene.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L457: One shared compressor slot for the thread: two contexts (or two scenes) evict each other and recompile every encoder**
  - description: `shared(ctx)` overwrites the single slot when `ctx` is another context, dropping the previous compressor (its Drop destroys its programs once in-flight tasks let go). `TextureCompressor::new` compiles an encoder program per format the device has (up to six LDR ones plus the BC6H cube encoder). `Scene::destroy` calls `release_shared(&self.ctx)` unconditionally, so destroying one of two scenes on a context has the same effect for the other. The comment says 'One compressor per context'.
  - scenario: A page with two viewers (two canvases, two contexts) that load models alternately: every `loadModel` on A after one on B builds a new compressor and links all encoders again, and the textures of that load wait for them (`compress_when_ready` awaits `when_ready`). Same after destroying scene 1 while scene 2 (same ctx) keeps loading models.
  - fix: Store one compressor per context (on `Core`, or a small list keyed by context and generation) and release it when the context goes, not when a scene does.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L764: compress() issues 6-7 synchronous getTexParameter calls after queuing the encode draws**
  - description: After all levels' encode draws and readPixels-to-PBO are queued, try_compress reads the old texture's MIN/MAG filter, WRAP_S/T, BASE/MAX_LEVEL and anisotropy with `gl.get_tex_parameter` (lines 764-778). Each is a synchronous query, the kind of get* call MDN's WebGL best practices say to keep out of non-debug paths. They sit right behind the heaviest work the module does, so the main thread blocks until the GPU process has consumed the whole encode command stream. `scratch()` adds a synchronous checkFramebufferStatus per compress (line 630). This undercuts the module's stated design that "the data never comes back to the CPU".
  - scenario: A glTF model with 20-30 textures: every `comp.compress(tex, kind)` call makes 7-8 synchronous round trips placed after a full mip chain of encoder draws. The per-texture time recorded by textureCompressionStats is mostly this wait, and it lands on the main thread during loading.
  - fix: Do not query GL for the parameters. Either track filter/wrap/level/anisotropy in TextureObj when the setters run and replay them on the new texture, or at least read them before the encode draws are issued. Skip check_framebuffer for the fixed RGBA32UI scratch target outside debug builds.
- [ ] **[low/api] L766: compress() drops TEXTURE_MIN_LOD / TEXTURE_MAX_LOD although it promises to keep the sampler settings**
  - description: The doc on `compress` says the same handle then samples the compressed texture "with its sampler settings". The carried-over list (lines 766-776) covers MIN/MAG filter, WRAP_S/T, BASE/MAX_LEVEL and anisotropy only. texture.rs exposes setters for TEXTURE_MIN_LOD and TEXTURE_MAX_LOD (texture.rs:1327-1344), and those values are not copied to the replacement texture, which gets the GL defaults (-1000 / 1000).
  - scenario: `ctx.textureSetMaxLod(tex, 3.0)` (or setMinLod) to limit the mips sampled, then `compressor.compress(tex, CompressKind::Rgb)`. The handle now samples the full mip chain again; the LOD clamp is gone with no warning.
  - fix: Add TEXTURE_MIN_LOD and TEXTURE_MAX_LOD to the copied parameters and set them with tex_parameterf, or track the parameters in TextureObj and replay them all.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/texture_packer.rs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L118: PackSpec::set_source: the "fifth source" error branch is unreachable and its documented behaviour is not what the code would do**
  - description: set_source first sets `self.mode[out] = 0` and calls `compact()` (lines 113-114). After that at most the three other output channels can reference a source, so `self.sources.len()` is at most 3 and the `>= 4` check on line 118 can never be true. A spec has four output channels, each reading one texture, so a fifth distinct source cannot exist by construction. The doc comment (lines 107-109) still describes the rejection and says "the channel is left at its previous setting". Even if the branch were reachable, the channel has already been switched to a constant and its old source compacted away before the check, so that statement would be false.
  - scenario: Any sequence of setSource calls: the console error "a pack can reference at most 4 distinct source textures" can never fire. A reader of the docs or the TypeScript API expects a failure mode and a keep-previous guarantee that do not exist.
  - fix: Remove the dead branch and the sentence about a fifth source. A debug_assert!(self.sources.len() < 4) is enough to document the invariant.
- [ ] **[low/api] L436: pack()/packInto() return Ok with a blank texture when the draw is skipped for a stale source**
  - description: `run_pass` builds the pass, calls `self.ctx.render(&pass)` and returns `Ok(())` unconditionally (lines 436-437). The renderer skips a draw whose param group references a destroyed texture: `apply_group` reports the stale handle and marks the draw incomplete (renderer.rs:812-818, 353-355). In that case nothing is written to the freshly allocated output, which stays at texStorage's zero fill. pack_into then generates mipmaps from it and returns Ok. PackSpec::set_source does not validate the handle, and neither does pack_into, although it does check the output handle and the program's link status for exactly this "would draw nothing" reason (lines 377-391).
  - scenario: `spec.setSource(Channel.R, ao, Channel.R); ctx.destroyTexture(ao); const orm = packer.pack(spec, 1024, 1024, false, true)`. This returns a valid TextureId whose content is (0,0,0,0) in every channel, including the channels set to constants such as A = 1. The only sign is a console stale-handle line; the Result says success. A material using it renders fully occluded and non-metallic.
  - fix: Before allocating storage in pack_into, check that every `spec.sources` entry is a live texture and return Err naming the stale source. Alternatively compare the renderer's skipped-draw count around the pass and return Err if the draw was skipped.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/trace.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L298: start() keeps fences from the previous recording, giving a GPU span with a stale timestamp and negative duration**
  - description: `Fence::set` is microseconds relative to `State::origin`. `start()` resets `origin` and clears events, stack and names, but not `s.fences`. Fences set under the old origin are still in flight when the next `gpu_fence` runs, which emits `ts: set` (old timebase) and `dur: now - set` (new minus old). `stop()` does not clear them either; they only go away if a `gpu_fence` call happens while not recording.
  - scenario: `Trace.start()`, render for 30 s, then `Trace.start()` again to restart (or `Trace.stop(); Trace.start()` with no frame in between). The first `renderFrame` of the new recording finds the old fence signalled and pushes `gpu frame` with `ts` of about 30,000,000 µs and `dur` of about -30,000,000 µs into a recording that is 16 ms old. The GPU track gets a bogus slice far past the end and a negative `dur`.
  - fix: Store `Fence::set` as absolute `performance.now()` ms and convert at push time, or in `start()` mark existing fences as belonging to no recording (e.g. a recording serial on `Fence`; mismatching fences are deleted in `gpu_fence` without emitting).
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L376: GPU fence spans partially overlap on one track exactly when the GPU falls behind**
  - description: Each passed fence becomes a `ph:"X"` event `[set, time seen passed]` on tid `TRACK_GPU`. When a fence is not yet passed at the next `gpu_fence` call, the next fence is set while the first is still open, so the two events overlap without nesting: A = [t0, t2], B = [t1, t3] with t0 < t1 < t2 < t3. The Chrome trace format requires complete events on one tid to nest (the module header says so itself: "Spans must nest"); viewers either stack B as a child of A running past its parent or flag and drop it. With two contexts recording, both write to the same tid and the shared `gpu frames in flight` counter name, so they also interleave.
  - scenario: A GPU-bound phone with more than one frame in flight (the case this probe exists for): `gpu frames in flight` is 1 or more after the check, so every `gpu frame` span partially overlaps the next one and the GPU row is mis-stacked or has slices missing.
  - fix: Emit the fence spans as async events (`ph:"b"`/`"e"` with an id), or put each in-flight depth on its own tid, or clip each span's start to the previous span's end. Key the counter name and track by context when more than one records.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L666: Trace.complete with a non-finite time makes the whole report invalid JSON**
  - description: `Trace::complete` stores `ts = (start_ms - origin) * 1000` unchecked, and `write_json` prints `ts` and `dur` with `{ts:.1}` / `{dur:.1}` (lines 482, 488, 494) instead of `json_num`. A NaN prints as `NaN` and an infinity as `inf`, neither of which is JSON. Only `duration_ms` is partly protected (`NaN.max(0.0)` is 0, but `Infinity` survives). A start before `Trace.start()` also yields a negative `ts`.
  - scenario: `Trace.start(); Trace.complete('laf', entry.startTime, entry.duration, 3)` where a field is `undefined` (wasm-bindgen turns it into NaN for an f64 argument), or `Trace.complete('x', NaN, 1, 1)`. `Trace.report()` then contains `"ts":NaN`; `JSON.parse` and ui.perfetto.dev reject the file, so the whole recording is lost to one bad event.
  - fix: In `complete`, return early (or warn) when `start_ms` or `duration_ms` is not finite; additionally write `ts`/`dur` through a finite-checked formatter in `write_json`.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L310: A finished recording can never be released; the event buffer keeps its capacity**
  - description: `start()` uses `events.clear()`, which keeps the allocation, and there is no API to drop a recording after `report()`. With the default limit of 1,000,000 events the `Vec<Rec>` is roughly 80 MB (plus per-event arg vectors and interned names), and `report()` builds a second string of about 100 MB or more in wasm memory.
  - scenario: On a phone (the stated use), record until full, call `Trace.report()`, upload it, and carry on using the app. The ~80 MB event buffer stays allocated in `STATE` for the rest of the page's life, and wasm linear memory, grown for the report string, never shrinks.
  - fix: Add `Trace.clear()` (or have `stop`/`report` take an option) that replaces `events`, `names`, `name_index` with fresh empty collections; in `start()` use `self.events = Vec::new()` when the previous capacity is far above the new `max_events`.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L353: gpu_fence does JS calls every frame when nothing is recording and no fences exist**
  - description: The doc says it "does nothing while not recording", but the not-recording path always runs `retain`, drains and partitions the (empty) fence list and then calls `gl.is_context_lost()` (a JS call) before returning. `Scene.renderFrame` and `presentFrame` call it every frame.
  - scenario: Any app that never calls `Trace.start()` still pays a `STATE` borrow and an `isContextLost()` round trip per rendered frame.
  - fix: Return at the top when `!s.recording && s.fences.is_empty()`.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L396: Fences and their WebGL context stay in the thread-local for ever once a context stops rendering**
  - description: `State::fences` holds a `WebGlSync` plus a clone of the `WebGl2RenderingContext` per fence. They are only removed by a later `gpu_fence` call for the same context (line 350-359). `stop()`, `start()`, `Scene.destroy` and the context's `delete_all` never touch them, and `stop()` cannot, having no context.
  - scenario: `Trace.start()`, render a few frames, then `scene.destroy()` / destroy the context (or simply stop rendering that canvas) and call `Trace.stop()`. The last in-flight fences stay in `STATE` for the life of the page, keeping the sync objects and the WebGL context (and so its canvas and GPU memory) reachable. Repeating this with viewers that come and go accumulates dead contexts.
  - fix: Give trace a `forget_context(&Context)` called from the context's destroy path and from `Scene::destroy`, and drop fences whose context reports `is_context_lost()` or belongs to an older recording when any `gpu_fence` or `start()` runs.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L396: Trace fences keep a clone of the WebGL context and its sync objects after the Context is dropped**
  - description: `gpu_fence` stores `Fence { sync, gl: gl.clone(), .. }` in the thread-local `STATE`. A context's fences are only removed by a later `gpu_fence` call for that same context (lines 350-359). `Core::drop` and `Scene::destroy` do not touch them, so the fences in flight at teardown stay in STATE with a JS reference to the `WebGL2RenderingContext`, which keeps the browser from collecting it.
  - scenario: `Trace.start()`, a few `renderFrame`s (each calls `gpu_fence`), then `scene.destroy()` and the last `ctx` handle dropped: the last one or two fences and the gl object they clone stay referenced for the life of the page (or until another recording drains them for that same gl, which can no longer happen).
  - fix: Drop a context's fences when its Core drops (a `trace::forget(gl)` called from `Drop for Core`), or hold the gl weakly / key fences by a context id and clear them in `Trace.stop()`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L656: JS docs name TRACK_MAIN / TRACK_GPU, which are not exported to JS**
  - description: The doc comment of `Trace.complete` (shipped in `luxel.d.ts`) tells callers to pass `TRACK_MAIN`, `TRACK_GPU`, or a value of 3 or more. Both are plain Rust `pub const`s (lines 51, 53) with no `#[wasm_bindgen]` export, so nothing by those names exists in the JS module.
  - scenario: A JS user writes `Trace.complete('task', t, d, TRACK_MAIN)` or `Trace.TRACK_MAIN` as the typings suggest and gets a ReferenceError or `undefined` (which becomes track 0, an unnamed row).
  - fix: Export them (e.g. static getters `Trace.TRACK_MAIN` / `Trace.TRACK_GPU`, or a `#[wasm_bindgen]` enum), or state the numeric values 1 and 2 in the doc.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/uniform_buffer.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L114: UboLayout builder methods consume the JS object; the exported docs show Rust syntax and names**
  - description: `field`, `array`, `struct_field`, `struct_array` take `self` by value. Through wasm-bindgen that moves the JS object: the receiver's pointer is zeroed and a new object is returned. Only the chained form works in JS; the natural imperative form fails at run time. The doc comment that reaches luxel.d.ts (dist/npm/luxel.d.ts:3811-3843) is the Rust example (`UboLayout::new()`, `.struct_array("lights", &light, 4)`) and refers to `set_vec4("offsets[3]", …)` / `set_vec3("light.pos", …)`, names that exist neither in JS (`uniformBufferSetVec4`, `structArray`) nor any longer in Rust (`uniform_buffer_set_vec4`).
  - scenario: JS: `const l = new UboLayout(); l.field("u_view", UniformType.Mat4); l.field("u_count", UniformType.I32); ctx.createUniformBuffer(l, …)` -> the second call throws "null pointer passed to rust" because the first call consumed `l`. A loop that adds fields conditionally hits the same unless it reassigns `l = l.field(…)`.
  - fix: Make the wasm-facing methods take `&mut self` (returning nothing, or keep a separate chaining Rust API), or state the reassign rule with a JS example in the doc comment; update the path examples to the current setter names.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L215: Uniform-buffer fields can only be written by path string: a string build/transfer and binary search per setter call in the per-frame path**
  - description: Every `uniform_buffer_set_*` takes `path: &str`, resolves it with `Layout::field_id` (binary search comparing strings) and only then writes. luxel-core's layout is designed for the opposite ("resolves once to a FieldId; writes go by id"), but the Context API exposes no id. From JS each call additionally encodes the string into wasm memory (malloc + free); from Rust, callers build the path with format! on every call (packages/luxel/src/lighting/pack.rs:152, 168, 175: one String per light member, per cascade matrix, per frame).
  - scenario: A frame with 8 lights and 4 shadow matrices: ~40 heap-allocated path strings plus ~40 string binary searches per frame just to address fields whose offsets never change; a JS app writing a 64-element array does 64 string encodes per frame.
  - fix: Add `uniform_buffer_field(ubo, path) -> u32` (FieldId index, NULL/-1 when missing) and id-based setters (or a bulk `uniform_buffer_set_floats(ubo, field, &[f32])`), and let the path setters call them; cache the ids in pack.rs/render.rs.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L232: UBO setters mark the field dirty even when the bytes did not change, so unchanged blocks are re-uploaded every frame**
  - description: `put` writes the value into the staging bytes and unconditionally marks the range dirty (`Ok(range) => self.dirty.mark(range)`). The dirty tracking exists to upload only what changed, but a caller that sets the same values each frame (the scene's frame/lighting blocks do this: packages/luxel/src/lighting/pack.rs:152-175, render.rs) dirties the whole block every frame; flush then issues bindBuffer + bufferSubData + bindBuffer per run on each draw-time bind although the GPU copy is already identical. The comment at MERGE_GAP notes that a bufferSubData call costs about as much as 1 KB of upload on the measured Mali device.
  - scenario: A static scene: lights and shadow matrices are re-set each frame with identical values -> one or more bufferSubData uploads of the full lighting block per frame for nothing (the plain-uniform path, by contrast, compares against what the program holds).
  - fix: In `put` (or in Layout::write's caller) compare the destination bytes with the new components before writing and skip the mark when equal; the compare is a few words per field.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L266: Block size is validated only after the whole layout is expanded: a large array count allocates one Field (with a path String) per element or overflows u32**
  - description: create_uniform_buffer calls `layout.builder.build(Std140)` first and validate_block_size second. `build` emits one `Field { path: format!("{name}[{i}]") … }` per array element (and per member per struct-array element) and computes `stride * count` / `off + i * stride` in u32. `count` comes straight from JS (`array(name, ty, count)`, `structArray`).
  - scenario: new UboLayout().array("a", UniformType.F32, 200_000_000): build tries to create 2e8 path strings -> wasm memory exhaustion and an abort (the whole module is dead afterwards) instead of the "UBO block is N bytes, over MAX_UNIFORM_BLOCK_SIZE" error. With count = 0x1000_0000 and a 16-byte stride `stride * count` wraps to 0 in release builds (panics in debug).
  - fix: Compute the size first with checked arithmetic (a measure-only pass that multiplies instead of iterating) and return the MAX_UNIFORM_BLOCK_SIZE error before emitting fields; also reject count == 0 arrays, which GLSL does not allow.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/vao.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L223: vertex_array_attr does not validate size/stride/offset and records the attribute even when GL rejected it**
  - description: VertexAttr constructors take any `size: u8`, and `stride` / `offset` are public i32 fields. vertex_array_attr forwards them to vertexAttrib(I)Pointer without checks: size outside 1..=4, stride < 0 or > 255, offset < 0, or stride/offset not a multiple of the component size all raise INVALID_VALUE / INVALID_OPERATION in WebGL. The function then still calls enableVertexAttribArray, retains the buffer and stores `(buffer, layout)` in `attribs`, so luxel's bookkeeping (vertexArrayAttrBuffer / vertexArrayAttrLayout) claims a binding that GL does not have. Other entry points in the crate (attribute index, copy ranges) validate and report with a luxel message.
  - scenario: ctx.vertexArrayAttr(vao, 0, buf, VertexAttr.float(5)) or an attr with `stride = 300` or `offset = 2` for a float attribute: the browser logs a bare GL error, attribute 0 ends up enabled with no buffer (first use) or still pointing at the previous buffer (replacement, whose use count luxel already released -> if that buffer was destroyed it is now deleted while GL still reads it through the VAO), and every draw with this VAO fails or shows old data. vertexArrayAttrBuffer(vao, 0) returns `buf`.
  - fix: Validate before touching GL: size in 1..=4, 0 <= stride <= 255, offset >= 0, stride and offset multiples of the component byte size; console::error and return without changing the vertex array.

## `packages/luxel-gl/src/vao_builder.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L378: buildInto on an already configured vertex array keeps the old buffers and stale attributes until the VAO is destroyed**
  - description: build_into creates a new VBO per divisor group (line 451) and an optional index buffer (line 469) and pushes each into the vertex array's `owned` list (own_buffer, line 541). Nothing ever removes an entry from `owned` or clears the previous configuration. When the vertex array already had attributes from an earlier build, vertex_array_attr only drops the use count of the replaced buffer (vao.rs:253); since that buffer is Live and not doomed, it stays in the pool and in GL. Attributes and the index buffer of the earlier build that the new build does not set remain enabled/bound and keep reading the old buffers.
  - scenario: const vao = ctx.createVertexArray(); every time the geometry changes: `b = new VaoBuilder(ctx); b.addF32(0, F32x3, positions); b.addIndicesU16(idx); b.buildInto(vao)`. Each call adds one VBO + one IBO that are never freed: GPU memory and `owned` grow without bound (resourceStats buffers/bufferBytes climbs) until destroyVertexArray. Second variant: first build has attrs 0,1 + indices, second build only attr 0 and no indices -> attr 1 stays enabled on the old (possibly shorter) buffer and the old index buffer stays bound, so draws use mixed old/new data.
  - fix: At the start of build_into (after validation) take the vertex array's previous `owned` list, and after the new attributes are set, disable attributes/index buffer not covered by the new build and destroy the previously owned buffers (or document buildInto as valid only on a fresh vertex array and reject one that already has attributes/owned buffers).
- [ ] **[low/perf] L233: VaoBuilder holds three copies of the vertex data at build time, and always interleaves vertex by vertex**
  - description: Each `add*` receives a slice wasm-bindgen already copied into wasm memory and copies it again into an owned Vec (`bytes_of(..).to_vec()` / `data.to_vec()`, lines 233-237, 307, 345). `interleave` then allocates a third, zero-filled buffer of stride * count and copies attribute by attribute, vertex by vertex with a bounds-checked slice copy, even when a group has a single attribute (stride == bytes per vertex), where the stored bytes could be uploaded as they are. Index data is likewise copied once more before upload. wasm linear memory never shrinks, so the transient peak becomes permanent heap size.
  - scenario: A mesh with 2M vertices of position/normal/uv (64 MB of attribute data) built from JS: ~64 MB (bindgen copies, sequential) + 64 MB (builder Vecs) + 64 MB (interleaved) live at build(); the wasm heap grows by well over 128 MB and stays there. A single-attribute per-instance group still goes through the per-instance copy loop.
  - fix: Upload a single-attribute group straight from `e.data`; for multi-attribute groups free each source Vec as it is consumed (build takes `self`), or interleave in chunks into a reusable scratch buffer and upload with bufferSubData.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L252: setDivisor doc contradicts itself and a divisor for an index that is never added (or a matrix's later slot) is silently dropped**
  - description: The doc says "Makes the attribute at `index` (added already) per instance" and, three lines later, "Before or after its `add*`". The code implements the second (divisors are looked up at build time). build_into only looks up divisors by an entry's first index (`divisor_of`, line 430), so a `(index, divisor)` pair that matches no entry is ignored without any message.
  - scenario: b.addF32(4, AttrKind.Mat4, m); b.setDivisor(5, 1) (a slot inside the matrix) or b.setDivisor(3, 1) with a typo in the index: build succeeds, the attribute stays per vertex in the per-vertex buffer, and the instanced draw shows every instance with the first transform; if the instance count differs from the vertex count the error message blames a vertex-count mismatch instead.
  - fix: Drop "(added already)" from the doc, and in build_into return an Err (or warn) for any divisor entry whose index is not the first slot of an added attribute.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L458: VaoBuilder never checks the WebGL stride limit of 255 bytes; build succeeds with every pointer call rejected**
  - description: WebGL rejects vertexAttribPointer / vertexAttribIPointer with stride > 255 (INVALID_VALUE). `interleave` computes the stride as the sum of all attributes of a divisor group with no upper bound, and build_into passes it on. vertex_array_attr does not notice the GL error, enables the arrays and records the layout, and build returns Ok.
  - scenario: Four per-instance Mat4 attributes at indices 0, 4, 8, 12 with setDivisor(…, 1) (or 16 slots of F32x4 / I32x4 in one group): stride = 256. Every vertexAttribPointer raises INVALID_VALUE, the attributes are enabled with no buffer attached, `build()` returns a valid id, and each draw then fails with INVALID_OPERATION (enabled attribute without buffer) or renders nothing. With MAX_VERTEX_ATTRIBS > 16 any layout above 255 bytes hits it.
  - fix: In build_into, after `interleave`, return an Err when stride > 255 (naming the group), or split such a group into several buffers.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/accessor.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L81: read_floats allocates and zero-fills count x components before checking the data can back it**
  - description: The output vector is reserved and `resize`d to `total` floats (lines 81-82) before `view_bytes` and the `end > data.len()` range check (lines 84-93). The comment says a count the file cannot back is an error rather than an allocation abort, which holds only when the reservation itself fails; a large but allocatable count is fully allocated and zeroed and then thrown away. Wasm linear memory never shrinks, so the growth is permanent.
  - scenario: A malformed or hostile file with an accessor `count: 100000000`, type VEC3, over a 12-byte bufferView: each `read_vecs::<3>` call (bounds, tangents, morph targets) grows the wasm heap by 1.2 GB, zero-fills it, then returns None. The model loads with a 'primitive skipped' warning, but the page keeps a multi-GB wasm heap or later allocations fail.
