  - fix: Do the bufferView lookup and the `end` range check first (and for the view-less sparse case, check `sparse.count()` views), and only then reserve and fill the output.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/animation.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L66: Animation and instancing accessors are read with the gltf crate's unchecked iterators: byteStride smaller than the element panics, a huge sparse count aborts on allocation**
  - description: animation.rs (lines 65-99) and scene.rs gpu_instances (lines 238, 244) read through `gltf::accessor::Iter` rather than the crate's own overflow- and range-checked `accessor::read_floats` / `read_vecs`. Two holes follow. (1) `Iter::new` takes `view.stride()` as is; the stride >= size check is only a debug_assert. `ItemIter::next` then does `split_at(stride)` and `T::from_slice(val)`, which for [f32;3] / [f32;4] indexes `slice[4..]`, `slice[8..]` and panics when byteStride is 4 or 8 (both pass gltf-json's 4..=252 validation). (2) For a sparse accessor without a bufferView, the iterator yields `accessor.count()` zero-filled items with no data backing them, and `.collect()` allocates count elements. accessor.rs guards exactly this ("A count the file can't back ... is an error, not an allocation abort"), but these two files bypass it.
  - scenario: (1) A translation sampler output (VEC3 float) whose bufferView has `byteStride: 4` -> panic in release wasm on the first keyframe read. Same for an EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing TRANSLATION accessor on such a view. (2) A sampler input accessor `{count: 4294967295, componentType: 5126, type: "SCALAR", sparse: {count: 1, ...}}` with no bufferView -> `collect::<Vec<f32>>()` tries to allocate ~16 GB -> capacity overflow / allocation abort in wasm32. A kilobyte-sized file kills the page's wasm instance.
  - fix: Read times, outputs and instancing attributes with `crate::accessor::read_floats` / `read_vecs` (already overflow-checked, range-checked, sparse-aware and normalisation-aware), and reject a byteStride smaller than the element size.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L81: Animation output accessor with a spec-forbidden component type traps the loader (gltf crate unreachable!)**
  - description: read_animations calls the gltf crate's `reader.read_outputs()` with no type check. In gltf 1.4.1 (animation/util/mod.rs:168 and :184) a rotation or weights output whose componentType is UNSIGNED_INT hits `_ => unreachable!()`. The crate's JSON validation (run in lib.rs `document`) does not cross-check sampler accessor types, so the file passes validation and then panics. geometry.rs already guards the same class of trap for primitives with `spec_types` ("Types the spec forbids would trap the gltf crate's readers"), but animation.rs has no equivalent. Related: `read_inputs` and the Translation/Scale branches reinterpret any accessor as f32 / [f32;3] without checking data_type or dimensions (only a debug_assert in the crate), so a non-float or wrong-dimension accessor yields garbage keyframes silently.
  - scenario: A .gltf with `animations[0].channels[0].target.path = "rotation"` and a sampler output accessor `{componentType: 5125, type: "VEC4"}` -> loadModel panics inside read_outputs (wasm `unreachable` RuntimeError) instead of rejecting the file or skipping the channel; the whole load is lost and the wasm instance is left after a panic.
  - fix: Before `read_outputs`, check the output accessor (rotation: VEC4 with F32/I8/U8/I16/U16; weights: SCALAR with the same types; translation/scale: VEC3 F32) and the input accessor (SCALAR F32); on mismatch `fetch::warn` and `continue`. Or read through `crate::accessor::read_floats`, which handles every component type without panicking.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L102: Keyframe times are not validated (NaN / non-increasing) although the API promises "increasing"; a NaN first key panics the sampler every frame**
  - description: `AnimationChannel::times` is documented "Keyframe times in seconds, increasing", and luxel's `animation::sample` relies on it. read_animations pushes whatever the binary buffer contains: only emptiness and the value count are checked. With `times[0]` NaN, `sample` (packages/luxel/src/animation.rs:120-130) passes both range checks (`t <= NaN` is false), `partition_point(|x| x <= t)` returns 0, and `k = 0 - 1` underflows (panic with overflow checks, otherwise an out-of-bounds index panic on `times[k]`). `duration` is taken from `times.last()` folded with `f32::max`, so an unsorted channel also reports a duration shorter than its real last key and the tail is never played; a NaN last time is silently ignored.
  - scenario: A glb whose sampler input buffer starts with a NaN float (times = [NaN, 1.0]) loads fine; `playAnimation` then panics inside `sample` on the first frame with 0 < t < 1 (a RefCell-poisoning wasm trap in the render loop). With times = [0, 2, 1], duration is 1 and the keys are sampled in the wrong order.
  - fix: In read_animations, drop (with `fetch::warn`) a channel whose times are not all finite and non-decreasing (`times.windows(2).all(|w| w[0] <= w[1])` and `times.iter().all(|t| t.is_finite())`), so the documented invariant actually holds for consumers.
- [ ] **[low/api] L106: Dropped animation channels are silent: nothing reaches Model::warnings(), and empty clips stay in the list**
  - description: Channels are skipped with a bare `continue` at lines 67, 100 and 106 (unreadable input, unreadable output, value count mismatch, no keys). No `fetch::warn` is issued, unlike skipped primitives and the instancing fallback (review-style.md made build-time skips report into the model's warnings). An animation whose channels are all dropped is still returned as a clip with `channels: []` and `duration: 0`.
  - scenario: A file whose sampler output has one keyframe more than its input (a common exporter slip that three.js tolerates): the channel vanishes, the clip is listed by name, `playAnimation` on it does nothing or finishes immediately (duration 0), and `model.warnings()` is empty, so the user has no clue why.
  - fix: `crate::fetch::warn(format!("animation {a} channel {c}: ...; skipped"))` at each `continue`. Consider truncating to `min(times, values)` for a count mismatch instead of dropping the channel.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/buffers.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L36: Buffer byteLength is trusted: unchecked allocation for meshopt fallback buffers, u64 truncation, and no length check on loaded data**
  - description: `let length = buffer["byteLength"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0) as usize;` truncates on wasm32 (`as usize`), and for a meshopt fallback buffer the value goes straight into `vec![0; length]`, an infallible allocation. `accessor.rs` deliberately uses `try_reserve_exact` to avoid allocation aborts from file-controlled sizes, but this path does not. For non-fallback buffers `length` is never compared with the bytes actually received, so a truncated `.bin` is accepted silently and only shows up later as unrelated 'primitive skipped' warnings.
  - scenario: A file with `"buffers":[{"byteLength":4294967295,"extensions":{"EXT_meshopt_compression":{"fallback":true}}}]` makes `vec![0; 0xFFFF_FFFF]` fail: the allocation error handler aborts and the wasm module traps (every later call into luxel fails), instead of `loadModel` rejecting. A `byteLength` of 2^32+16 silently becomes 16. A `.bin` cut short by a proxy loads with missing meshes and no message that the buffer was short.
  - fix: Convert with `usize::try_from` and return an error on overflow; allocate the fallback buffer with `try_reserve_exact` (error on failure); after resolving each non-fallback buffer return an error (or at least a warning naming the buffer) when `data.len() < byteLength`.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/decoders.js`

- [ ] **[medium/async] L68: A decoder whose wasm fails to instantiate leaves every job pending forever**
  - description: Both worker bodies build `ready` from a promise that only resolves: KTX2 at lines 68-71 (`onRuntimeInitialized: resolve`, the return value of `BASIS(Basis)` dropped) and Draco at lines 131-133 (`onModuleLoaded` only). When Emscripten's async instantiation fails (out of memory on a phone with up to 4 workers per pool, a truncated or mismatched .wasm next to a good .js), it calls abort() inside a promise callback: the factory's own promise rejects, nothing calls resolve, and no `error` event reaches `worker.onerror` (it is an unhandled rejection inside the worker). `ready` never settles, so the `(err) =>` branches at lines 81 and 146 never run and the Pool never rejects the jobs, contrary to the comment at line 188 ("none hangs"). Based on Emscripten's documented abort path; not reproduced here.
  - scenario: `setDracoDecoderPath(dir)` where draco_decoder.wasm is from another Draco version than the wrapper (or instantiation runs out of memory): `await loadModel(...)` of a Draco file never resolves or rejects. Same with KTX2: the texture's `on_load` never comes, its `Job` is never dropped, so `model.whenLoaded()` and `scene.whenReady()` hang.
  - fix: Reject `ready` on failure: pass `onAbort: reject` and chain the factory's promise (`Promise.resolve(BASIS(Basis)).catch(reject)`, same for `DracoDecoderModule`), and add `self.onunhandledrejection` posting a fatal message so the Pool can `fail(slot)`. Optionally have the worker post an 'init failed' message that the Pool turns into `fail`.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L68: A decoder wasm that fails to instantiate leaves every job, whenLoaded, whenReady and loadModel pending forever**
  - description: In both worker bodies `ready` is `new Promise((resolve) => { ...; BASIS(Basis) })` (line 68) / `DracoDecoderModule({ wasmBinary, onModuleLoaded })` (line 131): it only ever resolves. If the wasm binary does not instantiate, Emscripten aborts inside its own promise chain; that is an unhandled rejection in the worker, which does not raise the `error` event that `worker.onerror` (line 190) listens for. `ready` then never settles, so the `ready.then(ok, err)` of each job posts nothing and `Pool.jobs` keeps the entry. Across crates: `ktx2::load` never calls `on_load`, so the `Job` from `textures.rs::tracked` is never dropped, `Loading.pending` never reaches 0, and `Model.whenLoaded()` and `Scene.whenReady()` (js/mod.rs:1026 awaits every model's `when_loaded`) never resolve. For Draco, `draco::decode` awaits the job inside `load()`, so `loadModel` neither resolves nor rejects. `pool()` only checks `r.ok` for the two files, not that the wasm is a wasm. Reasoned from the code, not run.
  - scenario: setKtx2TranscoderPath('/basis/') on a dev server or SPA host that answers a missing basis_transcoder.wasm with index.html and HTTP 200 (or a truncated/corrupt download). loadModel of a KTX2 model resolves, the textures stay empty, and `await scene.whenReady()` / `await model.whenLoaded()` hang with nothing in the console from luxel. With a Draco model and a bad draco_decoder.wasm, `await loadModel(...)` hangs.
  - fix: Make `ready` reject: pass the module promise through (`ready = Promise.resolve(BASIS(Basis)).then(...)`, likewise for Draco's returned promise) and/or set `onAbort` to reject, so the existing error branch posts `{id, error}`. As a backstop add a `self.onunhandledrejection` in the worker that posts a fatal message, and have the pool `fail(slot)` on it.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L233: setDecoderPath always closes the live pool, failing jobs in flight even when the path is unchanged**
  - description: `setDecoderPath` does not compare the new base with the current one: any call closes the existing pool (`old.pool.then((p) => p.close())`), and `close()` rejects every running job with 'decoder pool closed' and terminates the workers. `setDracoDecoderPath` / `setKtx2TranscoderPath` read as idempotent configuration setters, and calling them before each load is a natural pattern. It also throws away the fetched script, the wasm and the warm workers, which are fetched again on the next job.
  - scenario: `function open(url) { setDracoDecoderPath('/draco/'); setKtx2TranscoderPath('/basis/'); return loadModel(ctx, url); }` called for model A, then for model B while A's Draco meshes or KTX2 textures are still decoding -> A rejects with "Draco: decoder pool closed", or A's KTX2 textures stay empty with a console error and a load warning.
  - fix: Return early when `decoders[kind]?.base === base`. For a real path change, let the old pool finish its jobs before closing (close once `jobs.size === 0`) instead of rejecting them.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L233: setDecoderPath closes the live pool: transcodes and Draco decodes in flight fail, even when the path is unchanged**
  - description: `setDecoderPath` always does `old.pool.then((p) => p.close())` and replaces the entry. `Pool.close()` rejects every running job with 'decoder pool closed' and terminates the workers. Jobs whose `pool(kind).then((p) => p.run(...))` was registered before the close reaction still run first and are then failed by it. There is no check that the path actually changed. The failure travels on: ktx2.rs logs 'KTX2: decoder pool closed' and calls `on_load(0, 0)`, `tracked` records 'image … didn't load', and the texture stays empty for the model's life; for Draco, `load()` returns Err and `loadModel` rejects.
  - scenario: An app (or a component mounted per model) calls `setKtx2TranscoderPath(sameUrl)` before each `loadModel`. Model A's KTX2 textures are still transcoding when the call for model B comes: A's textures fail with 'decoder pool closed' and stay blank; `model.warnings()` lists them. Same with `setDracoDecoderPath` while another Draco model loads: that `loadModel` rejects.
  - fix: Return early when `decoders[kind]?.base === base`. When the path does change, let the old pool finish its running jobs (close it once `jobs.size === 0`) instead of failing them.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L176: Decoder workers and their wasm heaps are never released**
  - description: A Pool keeps up to 4 workers per decoder kind alive for the page's life. Nothing terminates idle workers and there is no public dispose; the only path to `close()` is calling the path setter again. Each worker's Emscripten heap stays at its peak (grown by the largest texture or mesh it handled), and the Pool also keeps the fetched wasm ArrayBuffer and the script Blob URL.
  - scenario: A page loads one Draco + KTX2 model at startup and then runs for hours: up to 8 idle workers, each holding a Basis or Draco heap sized for the largest asset, stay resident.
  - fix: Terminate a worker after an idle timeout (when `load === 0` for some seconds; `run` already respawns on demand), and/or export a `disposeDecoders()` that closes both pools.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L259: Compressed inputs and the decoder wasm are copied more often than needed**
  - description: `ktx2Transcode` and `dracoDecode` (lines 259, 267) do `bytes.slice()` before transferring, but the `Uint8Array` they receive was already made by `Uint8Array::from(&[u8])` on the Rust side (ktx2.rs:90, draco.rs:127), which is a fresh JS-heap copy only this call owns, so its buffer could be transferred as it is. For an embedded KTX2 image textures.rs:206 adds one more copy (`.to_vec()` of the bufferView) before that, so each file exists four times at once. `Pool.spawn` (line 179) does `this.wasm.slice(0)` and then posts it without a transfer list, so the wasm is copied twice per worker. On the way back, ktx2.rs:152 copies every transcoded level into wasm memory with `to_vec()`, which raises the wasm heap's high-water mark for good by the largest level (64 MB for a 4096x4096 RGBA32 fallback level).
  - scenario: A GLB with twenty 10 MB embedded KTX2 images: each image is transiently held as buffer + Vec copy + JS copy + slice copy at load, all twenty submitted in the same tick -> several hundred MB of avoidable peak memory on a phone.
  - fix: Transfer `bytes.buffer` directly (drop the `.slice()`); let `ktx2::load` take `&[u8]` so the bufferView is copied once; in `spawn` either post `this.wasm` without slicing (structured clone copies it) or transfer the slice. For uploads, add a luxel-gl entry that takes the `Uint8Array` so levels go from JS to GL without passing through wasm memory.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L259: Every KTX2 file and Draco bufferView is copied twice before it is transferred to the worker**
  - description: The Rust side already builds a fresh JS copy (`Uint8Array::from(bytes.as_slice())` in ktx2.rs:90, `Uint8Array::from(data)` in draco.rs) that nothing else references. `ktx2Transcode` / `dracoDecode` then `bytes.slice()` it again (lines 259 and 267) only to transfer the second copy. The first copy is garbage as soon as the call returns.
  - scenario: A model with 40 KTX2 textures of 2 MB each allocates and copies an extra 80 MB of short-lived JS heap during load (on top of the wasm-side `Vec`), on phones where the loader's peak memory is already the concern (see the comment in `load()` about not holding the file twice).
  - fix: Transfer the buffer the Rust side made (`[bytes.buffer]`) and drop the `slice()`; the array handed in is already a private copy.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/fetch.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/perf] L52: Every fetched file is copied twice on its way into wasm (extra full-size JS copy)**
  - description: `main_file` (line 52) and `start_bytes` (line 67) do `js_sys::Uint8Array::new(&v).to_vec()`. `loader.js` already returns a `Uint8Array` from `main()`, `bytes()` and `blobBytes()`, and `new Uint8Array(typedArray)` is the copying constructor, so each call allocates a second JS buffer of the full file size before `.to_vec()` copies it a third time into wasm memory. The comment in `load()` (lib.rs:854) says the code tries not to double peak memory, but this transient copy does exactly that.
  - scenario: `loadModel(ctx, 'big.glb')` with a 400 MB GLB: the fetched ArrayBuffer (400 MB), the `new Uint8Array(view)` copy (400 MB) and the wasm `Vec` (400 MB) are alive together, then `split` copies the BIN chunk again. The same happens for every external `.bin`. On mobile this is the difference between loading and an out-of-memory tab kill; on desktop it is a wasted memcpy and GC pressure per file.
  - fix: Use the returned array without copying: `v.unchecked_into::<js_sys::Uint8Array>().to_vec()` (or `dyn_into` with an error), in both `main_file` and `start_bytes`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L24: makeFetcher can throw through a non-catch import: loadModel throws synchronously and leaks the borrowed Context**
  - description: `make_fetcher` is imported without `catch`, and `LoadRequest::from_js` calls it synchronously inside `load_model_js` (lib.rs:764) while the exported function holds its `&Context` borrow. The `Fetcher` constructor in loader.js throws for option shapes that `from_js` does not validate: a `signal` that is not an `AbortSignal` makes `AbortSignal.any([this.signal, this.stop.signal])` throw a TypeError (loader.js:36), and `files: [null]` throws in `filesMap` on `f.name` (loader.js:228). A JS exception through a non-catch import unwinds the wasm frames without running Rust destructors.
  - scenario: `loadModel(ctx, url, { signal: controller })` (the AbortController passed instead of `controller.signal`, an easy mistake): instead of a rejected promise with a `loadModel: ...` message like the other option errors, the call throws a raw TypeError synchronously, and the wasm-bindgen borrow guard for `ctx` plus the `source`/`options` values are never dropped, so the Context's reference/borrow count stays raised (it can no longer be freed, and any later exclusive borrow reports recursive use).
  - fix: Validate `signal` (instance of AbortSignal), `files`, `resolveUrl` and `onProgress` in `from_js` alongside `baseUrl` and the flags, and declare `makeFetcher` with `#[wasm_bindgen(catch)]` so a constructor failure becomes an `Error` returned from `from_js`.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/geometry.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L209: Unreadable or empty POSITION leaves an inverted infinite AABB and a primitive with no positions, giving the model a NaN centre**
  - description: When bounds are computed from data (no min/max, or normalized quantized positions) and `read_vecs::<3>` returns None or zero elements, `aabb_min` / `aabb_max` stay `[+inf; 3]` / `[-inf; 3]`. `scene::expand_world_aabb` then feeds corners mixing +inf and -inf into the model bounds (min = -inf, max = +inf), so the centre is NaN and the radius inf/NaN for the whole model; culling and camera framing use these. Separately, `bind_attributes` only `continue`s when the POSITION buffer cannot be built (line 350), so the primitive is still returned and drawn with attribute 0 disabled. The doc comment on `build_primitive` says None is returned when a GPU object cannot be created. With zero vertices and morph targets, `morph_texture` also calls `texture_storage_2d` with height 0 (logged error, texture without storage).
  - scenario: A file whose POSITION accessor has count 0, or a normalized-quantized (or min/max-less) POSITION accessor whose bufferView runs past a truncated .bin: the load succeeds, but `model.center()` returns NaN, so framing the camera on the model breaks, even though other meshes are fine.
  - fix: In `build_primitive`, skip the primitive (with a warning) when positions cannot be read, the POSITION buffer fails, or the computed bounds are not finite / count is 0, before creating the vertex array.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L514: Component count and element count of POSITION/NORMAL/TANGENT/TEXCOORD are never checked against what the packer assumes**
  - description: `vertex_buffer` hard-codes `n` per semantic (3, 3, 4, 2), but `read_floats` returns `count x accessor.dimensions().multiplicity()` floats. `spec_types` (line 851) validates only indices, COLOR, JOINTS and WEIGHTS; it falls through `_ => {}` for the rest. An accessor of another shape is silently re-chunked, so the buffer holds a different number of vertices than `vertex_count`. No attribute's `count` is compared with POSITION's either. A buffer shorter than the draw range makes every draw of that primitive fail with INVALID_OPERATION (attribs out of range) in WebGL2.
  - scenario: A primitive whose TEXCOORD_0 is a VEC3 float accessor: the UVs are re-chunked in pairs, so every vertex after the first gets wrong coordinates and the texture is scrambled, with no warning. Or NORMAL.count < POSITION.count, or POSITION declared VEC2: the buffer is too short, drawElements raises INVALID_OPERATION every frame and the mesh is invisible.
  - fix: Extend `spec_types` (or `vertex_buffer`) to require the expected dimensions per semantic and `accessor.count() >= POSITION.count`; otherwise skip the attribute or primitive with a warning.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/image_formats.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L50: WebP/AVIF support probe uses an <img> with a data: URI, which CSP img-src can block**
  - description: `decodes` sets a `data:image/...` URI on an `HtmlImageElement`. Under a CSP whose `img-src` lacks `data:`, the load is blocked and `onerror` fires, so support is cached as false for the page's life although the browser decodes both formats. The real texture path (fetch + `createImageBitmap` in luxel-gl) does not depend on `img-src` at all. `patch_image_sources` then keeps the PNG/JPEG fallback where the file has one.
  - scenario: Page with `img-src 'self'`; a glTF using EXT_texture_webp with PNG fallbacks -> every texture downloads its larger PNG fallback instead of the WebP, in a browser that supports WebP.
  - fix: Probe the way textures decode: `createImageBitmap(new Blob([bytes], { type }))` where available (resolved means supported), keeping the <img> probe only as the fallback.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/ktx2.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L146: A KTX2 texture larger than MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE is reported as loaded but has no storage**
  - description: `upload` passes the file's level-0 size straight to `texture_compressed_storage_2d` / `texture_storage_2d`. When it exceeds the device limit, luxel-gl's `storage_fits` logs and skips the allocation, every following sub-image call logs "the texture has no storage yet", and `upload` still returns `Ok((width, height))` (line 162). `on_load` therefore gets a non-zero size: `tracked` adds no warning, `note_content_changed` fires and `whenLoaded` resolves as if all were well, while the texture is unusable. The browser-decoded path reports `(0, 0)` and a warning for the same case (texture.rs doc of `texture_load_image`). The worker also transcodes every level, including those that can never be uploaded.
  - scenario: An 8192x8192 KTX2 base color map loaded on a phone with MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE 4096 -> console errors only, `model.warnings()` empty, the material samples a storage-less (black) texture.
  - fix: Send the device's max texture size in `config` and have the worker skip leading mip levels until the level fits (KTX2 files normally carry the chain), returning that level's size as width/height. When no level fits, or the file has a single level, return an error so `on_load(0, 0)` and the load warning are produced.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L80: KTX2 transcodes are not skipped or cancelled for a destroyed model or an aborted load**
  - description: `load` submits the job without checking `ctx.is_lost()` or `ctx.texture_is_valid(out)`, and the Pool has no way to drop queued jobs. All of a model's KTX2 images are posted to the workers at build time, and for external images `load` runs after the fetch, by which time the model may already be gone. Validity is only checked in `upload`, after the full transcode has run and its result has been transferred back.
  - scenario: A viewer switches from model A (30 4k KTX2 maps) to model B right after A's `loadModel` resolves and destroys A: the pool's workers still transcode all of A's textures (results discarded in `upload`) before B's textures get a turn, delaying B by seconds.
  - fix: In `ktx2::load`, return via `failed_later(on_load)` when the context is lost or `out` is no longer valid. In the Pool, keep jobs in a queue (post one per worker at a time) and let the caller cancel by id / AbortSignal so queued jobs of a destroyed model are dropped.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/lib.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L446: Destroying a model (or despawning it, or destroying the scene) does not stop its image downloads and decodes**
  - description: The requests of a load go through the load's `Fetcher`, whose `cancel()` is called only when the load itself fails (`load_request`, line 846). The Model keeps `request: Some(request.for_reload())` (line 1018), a different Fetcher (`forReload()` in loader.js builds a new one without the signal), so `Model::destroy` has nothing to cancel with and does not try. Every image fetch, `createImageBitmap` decode, KTX2 worker transcode and ORM source load of a destroyed model runs to completion and is then thrown away at the `texture_is_valid` check (texture.rs:1573, ktx2.rs:126, pack.rs:399). The same holds for the reload tasks the scene starts after a loss when the scene is destroyed (`alive` false frees the model, the downloads go on).
  - scenario: `root = scene.addModel(await loadModel(ctx, bigUrl))` (40 external 4K textures), then the user picks another model at once: `scene.despawn(root)`. All 40 downloads and decodes continue, competing with the new model's, unless the app kept and aborted its own `AbortSignal` (the viewer does; `loadModel` without `signal` has no way).
  - fix: Keep the load's own `Fetcher` (or its stop controller) in the Model's `Loading` and call `cancel()` from `Model::destroy`; have the scene do the same for reloads in flight when it is destroyed or the root despawned.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L478: Model.adopt (exported to JS) overwrites the model's GPU handles without checking that they are lost: on a live model every old object leaks**
  - description: `adopt` is documented for 'the same file loaded again after a context loss', but it checks only the four counts. It then replaces `self.textures`, `samplers`, `vertex_arrays`, `buffers`, `morph_textures` and the items' vertex arrays with `fresh`'s and never destroys the old ones, relying on the loss having killed them. Nothing verifies `self.generation != ctx.generation()` (nor that `fresh` belongs to the current generation). The scene guards its own call (`!model.is_lost` -> `fresh.destroy`, restore.rs:359), but `adopt` is in the `#[wasm_bindgen] impl Model` block, so JS and Rust users of the loader can call it directly.
  - scenario: An asset hot-reload wants to keep material handles: `const fresh = await loadModel(ctx, url); model.adopt(ctx, fresh)` with no context loss in between. It returns Ok; the old model's vertex arrays, buffers, textures, morph textures and samplers stay on the GPU with no handle left to destroy them. With a `fresh` from before a loss, the live model's handles are replaced by dead ones.
  - fix: If `self.generation == ctx.generation()`, destroy the old vertex arrays, buffers, textures, morph textures and samplers before taking over (after the remap), and refuse a `fresh` whose generation is not the context's (destroying it).
- [ ] **[low/api] L716: loadModel validates its options differently from every scene option object**
  - description: Scene option helpers throw on a non-object ('expected an options object') and on a wrong-typed key. `LoadRequest::from_js` treats a non-object `options` as absent. It throws for a non-boolean `vertexCompression` / `textureCompression` and a non-string `baseUrl`, but loader.js:37-38 silently ignores a non-function `resolveUrl` / `onProgress`, and unknown or misspelt keys are ignored at run time.
  - scenario: `loadModel(ctx, url, { resolveUrl: signedUrlMap })` passes an object instead of a function: every request goes out unsigned and fails with HTTP 403, with nothing naming the option. `loadModel(ctx, url, 'cors')` is accepted.
  - fix: Validate `options` and the function-typed keys in `from_js` with the same messages as the scene's option helpers.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L1034: A .gltf that starts with a UTF-8 BOM is rejected**
  - description: `split` passes the raw bytes to `serde_json::from_slice`, which does not skip a leading EF BB BF. The glTF spec says files should not have a BOM but allows implementations to ignore one, and three.js (which the loader otherwise follows for tolerance) loads such files because `TextDecoder` strips the BOM.
  - scenario: A `.gltf` saved by a Windows editor or produced by a script with a BOM: `loadModel` rejects with `glTF parse failed: expected value at line 1 column 1`, while the same file opens in three.js and other viewers.
  - fix: In `split`, strip a leading `\xEF\xBB\xBF` before the JSON parse (and before the `glTF` magic test is unaffected).
- [ ] **[low/perf] L1046: document() deep-clones the whole JSON although the caller never uses it again**
  - description: `document(&json, ..)` does `serde_json::from_value(json.clone())`. In `load()` the `json` value is not read after this call (variants, meshopt and Draco all run before it), so the clone is pure waste. For a `.gltf` with embedded base64 `data:` URIs the JSON strings are the whole payload, so the original `Value`, its clone and the deserialized `Root` hold the base64 text at the same time, and `json` stays alive until the end of the build.
  - scenario: A 150 MB `.gltf` with embedded base64 buffers and images: during `gltf.document` the wasm heap holds the base64 text three times (original Value, clone being consumed, Root strings) plus the decoded buffers. Wasm memory never shrinks, so the page keeps that high-water mark for its lifetime.
  - fix: Take `json` by value in `document` (`serde_json::from_value(json)`) and pass ownership from `load()`; the unit tests can pass the split result directly.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/material.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/perf] L582: Alpha-only maps are flagged linear, so a texture shared with its colour map is decoded and uploaded twice**
  - description: MAP_SLOTS marks specularTexture (line 582-588), sheenRoughnessTexture (603-609) and diffuseTransmissionTexture (631-637) as srgb=false. The shader reads only .a from all three (pbr.frag:373, 464, 511), and alpha is identical in an sRGB and a linear texture. But textures.rs:302 texture_color_spaces puts a texture into both sets when one slot is srgb and another is not, and pack.rs then builds it once per colour space (remap[i][space]). The glTF extensions recommend exactly this packing: sheenColor RGB + sheenRoughness A in one image, specularColor RGB + specular A in one image, diffuseTransmissionColor RGB + diffuseTransmission A in one image. docs/review-plan.md records 'a texture used both as colour and as data is built in both spaces' as a fix; the new evidence is that for these three slots the second copy is never needed.
  - scenario: Load a model whose KHR_materials_sheen uses one 2048x2048 RGBA texture for both sheenColorTexture and sheenRoughnessTexture (the Khronos sheen sample assets do), or KHR_materials_specular with specularTexture == specularColorTexture: the image is decoded/uploaded twice and holds two full mip chains of GPU memory (about 2 x 22 MB uncompressed), and two texture objects instead of one.
  - fix: Give MapSlot a third state (e.g. `space: Srgb | Linear | Any` or an `alpha_only` flag) for the three A-only slots; in texture_color_spaces insert such a texture into the linear set only if it is not already in the sRGB set after all materials are scanned, and in remap_textures pick whichever space exists (r[1] if >= 0 else r[0]).
- [ ] **[low/api] L260: JS setter `openPbrNative` on Material only changes a detached copy**
  - description: `set_openpbr_native` is exported with `#[wasm_bindgen(setter, js_name = openPbrNative)]`, the only JS-writable field of Material. The only way JS gets a Material is `Model::material(i)` (lib.rs:386), which returns a clone; the scene keeps its own copy in its material record and switches semantics through `setMaterialSemantics`, which also updates the shader variants. The doc comment 'Sets what its parameters mean' promises an effect the setter cannot have from JS.
  - scenario: `const m = model.material(0); m.openPbrNative = true;` succeeds silently; `scene.materialSemantics(group)` still returns 'gltf', the OPENPBR_NATIVE shader variant is never selected and rendering is unchanged.
  - fix: Drop the `#[wasm_bindgen(setter)]` attribute (keep the method as a plain Rust `pub fn` in a non-bindgen impl block for the scene), leaving the getter read-only like every other Material field.
- [ ] **[low/api] L260: Material.openPbrNative has a JS setter that only changes a throwaway copy**
  - description: `Model.material(i)` returns a clone (`self.materials.get(i).cloned()`, lib.rs:386) and the scene keeps its own `MaterialRecord` copy. The exported `openPbrNative` setter on `Material` therefore changes nothing a renderer reads. It is the only setter on the class; `scene.setMaterialSemantics` is what works.
  - scenario: `model.material(0).openPbrNative = true; scene.addModel(model)` type-checks and runs, and the material still renders with glTF semantics.
  - fix: Remove the wasm setter (keep the Rust method), or mark the property readonly in JS and point to `setMaterialSemantics`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L350: Property `iridescenceThickness` does not use the glTF name the table promises**
  - description: The PROPERTIES doc (lines 299-301) says each entry is named by 'its glTF name (as in the spec and its extensions)'. KHR_materials_iridescence calls the value `iridescenceThicknessMaximum` (and translate reads that key at line 895), but the property is registered as `iridescenceThickness`, while its sibling is registered verbatim as `iridescenceThicknessMinimum` (line 355).
  - scenario: `scene.setMaterialProperty(mat, 'iridescenceThicknessMaximum', 550)` throws 'no material property "iridescenceThicknessMaximum"', whereas 'iridescenceThicknessMinimum' works; a user following the extension spec cannot set the maximum without reading the source.
  - fix: Rename the property to `iridescenceThicknessMaximum` (update luxel/src/js/openpbr.rs:139 and :239 and tests/api.rs:1012), or accept both names in the lookup and document the alias.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L422: Reading one property clones the whole Material (heap-allocating its name) because only values_mut exists**
  - description: `property()` does `self.clone().values_mut()[i].to_vec()`: a ~500-byte struct copy plus a String allocation for `name`, to read 1-4 floats. `write_property` (line 457) goes through `property()`, and `write_properties` (line 451) clones as well. Scene::set_material_values calls set_property + write_property on every setMaterialProperty, and the OpenPBR path (openpbr::get/set) calls `property` several times per call.
  - scenario: An app animating a material each frame (`scene.setMaterialProperty(mat, 'emissiveStrength', t)` or an OpenPBR name that fans out to several glTF properties) performs one or more full Material clones with a String alloc/free per call on the per-frame path, in addition to the result Vec.
  - fix: Add a shared-reference twin of the table (`fn values(&self) -> [&[f32]; PROPERTY_COUNT]`, e.g. generated from one macro together with values_mut) and use it in property / write_property / write_properties; write_property can then pass the slice directly without the intermediate Vec.
- [ ] **[low/api] L669: After assign_uv_slots, map UV values are shader slots, but set_map and the JS getters document them as TEXCOORD sets**
  - description: assign_uv_slots (line 1056) rewrites every map's second tuple element from a TEXCOORD set to a slot index (0/1) and stores the real sets in `uv_sets`, which has no public getter. Yet `set_map` documents `uv` as 'its TEXCOORD set (0 or 1)' (line 669), `map()` says 'TEXCOORD set' (line 663), and the JS getters baseColorUv / normalUv / ... say 'TEXCOORD set index used by ... (TEXCOORD_n)' (lines 140, 161). For a material that used TEXCOORD_2+ the numbers are not sets, and the scene's setMaterialTexture(texCoord) (luxel/src/js/materials.rs:446-459) passes the user's set number straight in as a slot.
  - scenario: A material whose only map reads TEXCOORD_2: assign_uv_slots gives uv_sets = [2, 0] and the map's uv becomes 0. `model.material(i).baseColorUv` returns 0 although the file says texCoord 2. Then `scene.setMaterialTexture(mat, 'emissiveTexture', tex, { texCoord: 0 })` samples the new map with TEXCOORD_2 (slot 0) and `texCoord: 1` samples it with TEXCOORD_0 (slot 1) - the wrong UV set in both cases, with no error.
  - fix: Either translate in set_map (look the requested TEXCOORD set up in `self.uv_sets` and reject/report one that is in neither slot) and have the getters return `uv_sets[slot]`, or rename/document these values as UV slots and expose `uv_sets` so callers can map them.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/meshopt.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L702: meshopt view parameters are cast and added unchecked, unlike json_view**
  - description: `decode_views` reads `buffer`, `byteOffset`, `byteLength`, `byteStride` and `count` as u64 and casts them with `as usize` (lines 685, 701, 702, 710-711). On wasm32 that truncates silently, and `offset + length as usize` at line 702 is an unchecked add. The parent view right below goes through `buffers::json_view`, which the style review introduced as the overflow-checked path. In a build with overflow checks (dev and test profiles) the add panics; in release it wraps, so a corrupt file can address a different range than it states instead of being rejected.
  - scenario: Extension object with `byteOffset: 4294967280, byteLength: 32` -> panic 'attempt to add with overflow' in a debug or test build. `buffer: 4294967296` -> truncated to buffer 0 on wasm32 and decoded from the wrong buffer instead of "source out of range".
  - fix: Use `usize::try_from(..)` for each field and `offset.checked_add(length)`, returning the existing "source out of range" error (or share a helper with `json_view`).

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/pack.rs`

- [ ] **[high/api] L445: Empty texture callback is built with new Function(""), which a CSP without 'unsafe-eval' blocks**
  - description: Outside my file list, but it is the `on_load` that textures.rs' `build_texture` is handed for every ordinary texture (pack.rs:135, and the textures.rs:33 doc says to "pass an empty function"). `empty_callback()` is `js_sys::Function::new_no_args("")`, i.e. `new Function("")`. On a page whose CSP allows wasm ('wasm-unsafe-eval') but not 'unsafe-eval', that throws EvalError. The js-sys binding has no `catch`, so the exception unwinds through wasm in the middle of `build_texture_set`: the `WithOptions` and `Building` guards in lib.rs never drop (the global compression toggles and the BUILDING slot stay as that load set them) and textures already created are leaked. It is the only `Function::new_*` use in the workspace. Not run here; read from the code.
  - scenario: Page served with `Content-Security-Policy: script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`; `loadModel(ctx, 'chair.glb')` for any model with at least one texture -> EvalError thrown out of loadModel, every textured model fails to load.
  - fix: Make the no-op without eval: a `Closure::<dyn Fn(f64, f64)>::new(|_, _| {})` created once and kept in a thread_local (or `Closure::once_into_js(|_: f64, _: f64| {})`), or have `after_load` / `tracked` take `Option<Function>`.
- [ ] **[medium/perf] L80: Textures no material slot reads are still fetched, decoded and kept as uncompressed RGBA8 with mips**
  - description: `spaces(i)` returns `[false]` for a document texture that is in neither the sRGB nor the linear set, so step 1 builds it anyway. Its compress kind is `None` (`compress::kinds` only sees material slots), so it stays full RGBA8, with a mip chain under the default sampler. The model keeps it in `textures` until destroy, and `whenLoaded` waits for it. Nothing can sample it: no material references it. The inline comment ("linear when in none: decoded as data") states the behaviour but gives no reason for it.
  - scenario: A KHR_materials_pbrSpecularGlossiness-only file (the extension is not handled; core pbrMetallicRoughness has no textures) with five 2048x2048 maps: all five images are downloaded, decoded and uploaded as RGBA8 with mips (about 21 MB each, about 105 MB of GPU memory) for a model that renders untextured. The same happens for leftover textures or ones only an unhandled extension references.
  - fix: Skip document textures used by no handled slot (leave `remap[i] = [-1, -1]`, which the remap already supports), or at least warn and do not load them.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L445: empty_callback() uses `new Function("")`, which a CSP without 'unsafe-eval' blocks**
  - description: `empty_callback()` builds the fire-and-forget image callback with `js_sys::Function::new_no_args("")`, i.e. `new Function("")`. It is called once per non-ORM texture in `build_texture_set` (line 135). Under a Content-Security-Policy that allows wasm but not eval (`script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`), `new Function` throws EvalError. The js-sys binding is not `catch`, so the exception unwinds through the synchronous build in `load()` without running Rust destructors: the `WithOptions` and `fetch::Building` guards in lib.rs are not dropped (the global compression toggles stay overridden) and textures already created are not freed. This is the only non-test use of `Function::new*` in the workspace. Not run under a CSP here; inferred from the code.
  - scenario: A page served with `Content-Security-Policy: script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` loads any glTF that has at least one texture not consumed by an ORM pack. `new Function("")` throws, loadModel rejects with an EvalError, and the vertex/texture compression globals are left at that load's options.
  - fix: Create the no-op without eval, e.g. `Closure::once_into_js(|_: f64, _: f64| {})`, a cached `Closure` or an exported JS no-op from loader.js; or let `after_load` / `build_texture` take `Option<Function>`.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L8: Module doc says a packed material samples one texture/unit; the shader still samples two**
  - description: The header gives "a material samples one texture/one unit instead of two" as the reason for packing. pbr.frag keeps separate `u_metallic_roughness_tex` and `u_occlusion_tex` samplers and does two `texture()` fetches (lines 358 and 453), each with its own UV and sampler object; lib.rs picks samplers per document texture. The only actual gain is holding one GPU texture instead of two.
  - scenario: A reader relying on the doc expects one fetch and one unit per ORM material (for example when counting texture units against MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS or judging the value of packing); the cost is unchanged.
  - fix: Correct the comment to say the benefit is memory only, or make the shader share the fetch when both slots resolve to the same texture, UV set, transform and sampler.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L59: A new TexturePacker (program, FBO, dummy texture, sampler, layout) is built for every model load with an ORM pair**
  - description: `build_texture_set` calls `TexturePacker::new(ctx)` per load, inside the synchronous build, and drops it when the last source callback has run. `TextureCompressor` has a per-context `shared()` instance; the packer has none, so each model with separate occlusion and metallic-roughness maps compiles and links the packing program again.
  - scenario: A configurator loading 20 product models that each have one ORM pair: 20 program compiles/links and 20 sets of scratch GL objects created and destroyed, where one would do.
  - fix: Keep one packer per context (as `TextureCompressor::shared` does) and reuse it across loads.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L320: Transient ORM source textures get a full mip chain that is thrown away**
  - description: `build_packed_orm` passes `force_mips = false` for both sources, but `build_texture` computes `generate_mipmaps = mips || force_mips`, where `mips` comes from the texture's own glTF sampler. With no sampler or any mipmap min filter (the default) it is true. Each source is then uploaded with mips, sampled once at level 0 by the packer through its own linear-clamp sampler, and destroyed.
  - scenario: Every ORM pair under the default sampler: two extra generateMipmap calls and about a third more transient GPU memory per source (two 4096x4096 sources: about 43 MB of mips allocated and discarded) on top of the packed output's own chain.
  - fix: Give `build_texture` a way to suppress mips for sources (e.g. a tri-state, or build the sources with an explicit no-mips flag).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L408: When both ORM sources fail, the packed texture is left without storage and reads as fully occluded**
  - description: `on_source_loaded` substitutes the constant 1 for a single failed source so it does not read as "an empty texture (black: fully occluded, or a smooth dielectric)". When both fail (`w <= 0 || h <= 0`) it returns, leaving `output` with no storage. Both the occlusion and metallic-roughness slots still point at it, and an incomplete texture samples as (0, 0, 0, 1).
  - scenario: Occlusion and metallic-roughness are separate external images and both return 404 (or fail to decode): the material renders with AO = 0 (no ambient/IBL light at occlusionStrength 1), roughness 0 and metallic 0. That is the black result the single-failure path was written to avoid.
  - fix: In the both-failed branch, pack a 1x1 texture of constants (1, 1, 1, 1) into `output` instead of returning.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L436: ORM packing turns GPU-compressed KTX2 sources into an RGBA8 texture**
  - description: `plan_orm_pairs` does not look at the image type. For KTX2 (KHR_texture_basisu) sources, `build_texture` transcodes each to a compressed GPU format, then `pack_into` renders them into a new RGBA8 texture with mips. That texture is only re-compressed if runtime compression is on and the device supports the Rgb kind. Otherwise two block-compressed textures are replaced by one uncompressed texture; with compression on, the data goes through a second lossy encode.
  - scenario: A basisu file with separate 2048x2048 occlusion and metallic-roughness textures, loaded after `setTextureCompression(false)` (or where the runtime compressor is unavailable): about 5.6 MB of compressed textures become one RGBA8 texture of about 21 MB.
  - fix: Do not pack pairs whose sources are KTX2 (leave them unpacked), or only pack when the output will be compressed.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/scene.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L173: Node walk has no cycle or depth guard: a cyclic node graph recurses until the wasm stack overflows**
  - description: `walk` recurses over `node.children()` with no visited set. glTF requires the node graph to be a strict forest, but neither gltf-json validation (no cycle or parent check in gltf-json 1.4.1) nor lib.rs `document` enforces it. Each recursion also pushes a SceneNode and, for mesh nodes, DrawItems and GL render-state lookups before the stack gives out. A node reachable twice (two parents, or listed twice in `scene.nodes`) is also emitted twice with the same `index`: model.rs `by_index` keeps only the last entity, so the earlier duplicate's draw items attach to the wrong node entity.
  - scenario: `{"scenes":[{"nodes":[0]}],"nodes":[{"children":[0]}]}` (or 0 -> 1 -> 0) -> loadModel never returns an Err; it recurses until `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` / a wasm trap, with `nodes` and `out` growing meanwhile. A legitimately very deep chain (tens of thousands of nested nodes) hits the same limit, since each frame carries two Mat4s plus SceneNode/DrawItem temporaries.
  - fix: Keep a `visited: Vec<bool>` sized `doc.nodes().len()`; on revisiting a node, `fetch::warn` and skip it (or fail the load). Preferably walk with an explicit stack of (node, parent, parent_world) instead of recursion.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L239: gpu_instances silently substitutes defaults when an instancing attribute can't be read; the "data out of range" warning is unreachable**
  - description: `read3` and the rotation read use `.and_then(Iter::new).map_or_else(|| vec![default; count], collect)`. `Iter::new` returns None both when the attribute is absent and when it is present but its data is out of range (the buffer is shorter than the view/accessor claims, or the buffer is missing). In the latter case the code falls into the default branch and produces exactly `count` identity translations / rotations / scales. The check at line 246 (`translations.len() != count ...`) can therefore never fire: a successful Iter always yields `count` items and a failed one is replaced by `count` defaults. The doc comment ("or it can't be read — reported, and the node is drawn once") and the warning text describe behaviour the code does not have. Also only `data_type() == F32` is checked, never `dimensions()`: a VEC4 TRANSLATION or VEC3 ROTATION is reinterpreted with the wrong element size.
  - scenario: Node with EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing, TRANSLATION accessor count 1000 whose bufferView extends past the end of a truncated .bin, ROTATION/SCALE fine -> no warning; 1000 draw items are emitted with all translations (0,0,0), i.e. 1000 coincident copies at the node origin (1000x overdraw, z-fighting for blended materials), instead of "drawn once" plus a warning.
  - fix: Distinguish absent from unreadable: `match a { None => vec![default; count], Some(a) => read_vecs::<3>(a, buffers) or else warn + return None }`. Also check dimensions (VEC3 / VEC4). Cap or try_reserve the default vectors, since count comes from the file.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L139: Model bounding sphere includes meshes under KHR_node_visibility-hidden nodes**
  - description: `walk` records `visible` on the SceneNode but `expand_world_aabb` is called for every primitive regardless of whether the node or any ancestor has `visible: false`. `Model::center()/radius()` (used for camera framing in js/picking.rs frameBounds and js/mod.rs) therefore covers geometry that is not drawn by default.
  - scenario: A model with a small visible product and a large hidden helper or alternative mesh (KHR_node_visibility `visible:false`) placed far away: `frameBounds` / the viewer frames the union, so the visible part ends up tiny and off-centre.
  - fix: Pass an inherited `visible` flag down `walk` (parent_visible && node visible) and skip `expand_world_aabb` when it is false, falling back to the full bounds only if nothing visible exists.
- [ ] **[low/api] L227: EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing with normalized BYTE/SHORT rotations (allowed by the extension) collapses to a single copy**
  - description: The extension allows ROTATION as VEC4 of normalized BYTE or SHORT as well as FLOAT. The check at line 227 rejects any non-F32 accessor, so a valid file is drawn once at the node transform with only a warning, even though `crate::accessor::read_vecs` already converts normalized integers to floats (it is used for quantized vertex data). The extension is in HANDLED_EXTENSIONS and is stripped from extensionsRequired, so a file that requires it loads "successfully" with the wrong result.
  - scenario: An asset with `extensionsRequired: ["EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing"]` and a quantized instance ROTATION accessor (componentType 5122, normalized true): instead of N instances the model shows one copy at the node origin, and loadModel resolves without error.
  - fix: Read ROTATION (and the others) through `accessor::read_vecs::<4>` / `::<3>`, which handles normalized integer component types; keep the warning only for genuinely invalid types.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L246: Dead length check and stale doc comment in gpu_instances**
  - description: The condition `translations.len() != count || scales.len() != count || rotations.len() != count` cannot be true. Every vector is either `vec![default; count]` or the collection of an iterator that `Iter::new` sized to exactly `accessor.count()`, and line 227 has already forced every present accessor's count to equal `count`. The warning "data out of range; drawn once" is therefore dead code, and the function comment ("or it can't be read — reported, and the node is drawn once") describes a path that does not exist. This is what hides the silent-defaults bug reported separately.
  - scenario: Reading the code, a maintainer assumes out-of-range instancing data is reported and handled; in fact that branch never runs (see the silent-defaults finding).
  - fix: Remove the check once the reads return None on unreadable data (and warn there), and update the doc comment to match.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/textures.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L180: KTX2 detection by URI extension is case-sensitive**
  - description: `is_ktx2` tests `p.ends_with(".ktx2")` on the raw URI, while `mime_from_path` in the same file lowercases before matching. `image.mimeType` is optional for URI images, so an upper-case extension is the only signal, and it is missed: the file goes to the browser's image decoder typed image/png and fails.
  - scenario: A .gltf whose KHR_texture_basisu image is `{ "uri": "Albedo.KTX2" }` with no mimeType (assets exported on Windows) -> "image 0 \"Albedo.KTX2\" didn't load" although the transcoder path is set.
  - fix: Compare case-insensitively (lowercase the path part, as `mime_from_path` does), or sniff the 12-byte KTX2 identifier once the bytes are in.

## `packages/luxel-gltf/src/vertex_format.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L143: Float normals/tangents are always stored as SNORM16, clamping non-unit vectors per component and changing their direction**
  - description: `choose` returns `Format::SNORM16` for every float `Kind::Direction` attribute without looking at the values (unlike `Kind::Unit`, which checks the range). `encode` clamps each component to [-1, 1] independently, so a normal longer than 1 with a component above 1 changes direction, not just length; the shader's normalize cannot recover it. With `setVertexCompression(false)` the same file shades correctly. Very short normals also lose angular precision.
  - scenario: A file exported with non-normalized normals (a scale baked without renormalizing), e.g. n = (0, 1.5, 0.5): stored as (0, 1, 0.5), which is 26.6 degrees off +Y where the file says 18.4. Lighting is visibly wrong on those meshes in the default configuration only.
  - fix: For `Kind::Direction`, normalize xyz before quantizing, or fall back to F32 when any component is outside [-1, 1] (as `Unit` does).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L190: encode() clamps non-normalized signed integers to -127 / -32767, so the type's minimum value is altered**
  - description: The clamp bounds `q(127.0, -127.0, 127.0)` and `q(32767.0, -32767.0, 32767.0)` are right for normalized data (-128 and -127 both decode to -1). They are also applied when `f.normalized` is false, where the dequantized value is the integer itself. The comment in `choose` ("re-encoding the dequantized values gives the file's integers back") is therefore untrue for i8 = -128 and i16 = -32768.
  - scenario: A KHR_mesh_quantization file with non-normalized SHORT positions (or BYTE/SHORT texture coordinates) using the full signed range: vertices at -32768 are uploaded as -32767, one quantization step from where the file puts them and from what the min/max bounds declare.
  - fix: Clamp to the full range when not normalized: lower bound -128 / -32768 when `!f.normalized`.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/env_bake.frag`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L107: Mipmapped equirect lookup over-blurs the poles of the baked cube**
  - description: `texture(u_equirect, uv)` uses implicit derivatives on a trilinear, fully mipmapped texture (environment.rs: LinearMipmapLinear, mip_levels(w, h)). Near the +-Y face centres u = atan(n.z, n.x) / 2pi changes by a large fraction of the image per pixel (du/dx is about 1/(2 pi r) for a pixel r texels from the pole), so the isotropic LOD picks a very coarse level although the footprint in v is about one texel: for a 2048x1024 image and a 256 face the centre quads read LOD 7 and LOD stays above 4 out to about 20 px. That is a vertical blur of several degrees around the zenith and nadir, baked into the source cube and so into specular mip 0 (the sharp background) and the reflections.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr with an HDRI that has detail straight up or down (a ceiling light grid, a textured floor), background on, camera looking down at a mirror floor or up: a smudged disc a few degrees wide sits at the pole while the rest of the environment is sharp.
  - fix: Compute the LOD from the cube texel's solid angle instead of the uv derivatives (textureLod with 0.5*log2(texel solid angle / equirect texel solid angle at that latitude)), or use textureGrad with derivatives of the direction mapped analytically (scale du by sin(theta)).

## `packages/luxel/shaders/exposure.frag`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L46: One non-finite pixel drives auto exposure to its limit, or to NaN for good**
  - description: The BEGIN step sums texels with no sanitising, and the shrink steps average them, so a single Inf texel in the HDR target makes the 1x1 result Inf: line 66 `clamp(prev_lum + (exposure - prev_lum) * u_adjust, min, max)` jumps straight to u_max_luminance (10 000 by default, exposure.rs:121) in one frame, i.e. full_exposure = exposure * grey / 10000 and the picture goes black. With a NaN texel (what TAA leaves in the target for the same pixel) `clamp(NaN, ...)` is undefined; where it stays NaN the stored result is NaN, and every later frame computes `prev_lum + (x - prev_lum) * adjust` = NaN, so the meter never recovers until a forced set (a gap of more than 1 s between frames). present.frag and taa.frag then fall back to the fixed exposure (`!(measured > 0.0)`), silently disabling auto exposure.
  - scenario: setAutoExposure(true), a scene with a roughness-0 metal highlight under a strong punctual light (pixel overflows RGBA16F): without TAA the frame snaps to near black while the highlight is on screen; with TAA the meter becomes NaN and auto exposure stops working although it is still reported as on.
  - fix: Clamp each fetched texel to a finite maximum and drop NaN in the BEGIN loop (`c = min(c, vec3(65504.0)); if (!(c == c)) c = vec3(0.0)` per component), and in the END step ignore a non-finite prev_lum (treat as u_force).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L66: Exposure easing overshoots and oscillates when speed x dt exceeds 1**
  - description: `prev_lum + (exposure - prev_lum) * u_adjust` is a linear step with u_adjust = speed * dt (exposure.rs:174); dt may be anything up to 1 s before the force path takes over (exposure.rs:169) and speed is only clamped to >= 0 (output.rs:479). For u_adjust > 1 the result overshoots the measured value, and for u_adjust >= 2 it diverges, bouncing between u_min_luminance and u_max_luminance.
  - scenario: setAutoExposureOptions with speed 4 and a page rendering on demand or at low frame rate (frames 600 ms apart): u_adjust = 2.4, the eased luminance flips between the 0.05 and 10 000 clamps and the picture flashes white and black on alternate frames.
  - fix: Use a bounded step: `mix(prev_lum, exposure, clamp(u_adjust, 0.0, 1.0))` or the frame-rate independent `1.0 - exp(-u_adjust)`.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/grab_down.frag`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L28: Firefly suppression turns an Inf texel into NaN across the transmission mip chain**
  - description: tapReinhard computes `w = 1.0 / (1.0 + vmax(s))` and `sum += s * w`. For an Inf texel (HDR target overflow, see the TAA finding; with TAA on the copied-back resolve already holds NaN there) w is 0 and `s * w` is Inf * 0 = NaN, so the level-1 texel is NaN. HdrOutput::grab then builds every further level from level 1 with generateMipmap (output.rs:777-779), so the NaN covers a doubling footprint per level, up to the whole 1x1 top mip. The pass meant to stop a subpixel highlight from 'blooming into the rough mips' instead turns the brightest one into NaN.
  - scenario: A rough transmissive mesh (roughness around 0.5 or more, so scene_color_at reads high mips) in front of a polished metal whose highlight overflows 65504: the glass shows a black/garbage block that grows with roughness, covering the whole surface at the last mips.
  - fix: Clamp the tap before weighting (`s = min(s, vec4(65504.0))`, and drop non-finite taps: `if (!(w > 0.0)) return;` after replacing NaN), or clamp the scene's radiance at the source.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/gtao.frag`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L91: normal_from_depth unprojects sky neighbours to Inf/NaN under reverse-Z or an infinite far plane**
  - description: main() only rejects the centre pixel when it is at the far plane. normal_from_depth picks, per axis, the neighbour whose extrapolation error is smaller, but when both sides are background (dl and dr both equal c0) it still unprojects that neighbour. With reverse-Z (Mat4::perspective_infinite_reverse_rh, camera.rs:58) or zfar: Infinity (camera.rs:77-85) a far-plane depth gives w == 0 in view_position, so `v.xyz / v.w` is Inf (NaN on the centre column/row), dpdx/dpdy are non-finite and `normalize(cross(dpdx, dpdy))` is NaN. Then normal_in_slice, nx, ny and ao are NaN; `clamp(NaN, 0, 1)` and `pow` are undefined and the value written to the RG8 target is typically 0 (fully occluded). The contact-shadow early-out `dot(n, l) <= 0.0` is also false for NaN, so the ray is marched for nothing. For a feature 2 px wide the choice between the valid neighbour and the sky is a near tie (`c0 < 2*B - c0`), so about half of those pixels are hit as well.
  - scenario: setReverseZ(true) (or a camera with zfar: Infinity), setAmbientOcclusion on, a thin object 1-2 px wide against the empty background (a cable, a railing, a distant small part): its pixels get NaN normals and come out as black AO speckles, which the depth-weighted denoise keeps (the neighbours are background, weighted near zero).
  - fix: Never difference against a far-plane neighbour: treat a neighbour at the far plane as invalid (prefer the other side, and fall back to the valid side or to a view-facing normal when neither exists), or clamp the unprojected w away from 0 in view_position. Also guard the result, e.g. `if (!(dot(n, n) > 0.0)) n = view_dir`.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L42: Stale uniform documentation and dead code in the post shaders**
  - description: u_gtao is documented as 'z distance exponent, w distance falloff', but occlusion.rs:223 always sends 1.0 for both, w is never read in the shader, and z only feeds `pow(..., u_gtao.z)` with exponent 1 (36 pow calls per pixel that do nothing). The header also describes three.js' distance falloff as replaced, so the comment describes parameters that no longer exist. In taa.frag:96-99 `sample_current` is never called (the 3x3 loop fetches inline), and the two comment lines at taa.frag:61-62 ('Karis' reversible tonemap...' / 'The display's exposure...') sit above the wrong functions.
  - scenario: A reader tuning AO looks for the 'distance falloff' control the uniform comment promises and finds it does nothing; changing sample_current has no effect on the resolve.
  - fix: Drop u_gtao.z/w (or wire them to AmbientOcclusion options), remove the pow, delete sample_current and move the tonemap comment to tonemap().

## `packages/luxel/shaders/gtao_denoise.frag`

- [ ] **[low/webgl] L29: Every depth read of the denoise (and the half-res GTAO centre) lands exactly on a texel corner of a NEAREST depth texture**
  - description: The taps are `(vec2(x, y) + 0.5) * texel` from an AO texel centre, i.e. AO texel corners; the AO texture is LINEAR (occlusion.rs:292) so each tap is a 2x2 average, but its weight comes from `textureLod(u_depth, uv + o, 0.0)` on a NEAREST depth texture (output.rs:899-901, 1204) at a point that is a corner of four depth texels at full and at half resolution (even sizes). Which texel NEAREST returns there depends on the last bit of the interpolated coordinate, so the weight belongs to an arbitrary one of the four texels while the AO value mixes all four: occlusion and the contact shadow (green) bleed one AO texel across silhouettes, which the header says the weighting prevents, and the pattern differs between GPUs. At half resolution the centre `uv` is a corner too, here and in gtao.frag (line 142 `depth_at(uv)` versus line 76 `ivec2(uv * size)`, which can pick different texels, so position and normal can come from different surfaces).
  - scenario: setAmbientOcclusion({halfResolution: true}) plus contact shadows, an object in front of a lit wall: a 2 px dark fringe of the wall's occlusion/contact shadow lies on the object's outline (or the reverse), with column/row patterns that change with canvas width.
  - fix: Read depth with texelFetch at explicit texel coordinates (e.g. the min/nearest of the 2x2 block for the centre, and per-tap the same block the bilinear AO tap covers), or sample the AO at texel centres with per-texel depth weights as XeGTAO does.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/lib/lights.glsl`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L49: Lighting block comment says u_output.z is 1 with a scene copy; it is 1 + the least mip to read**
  - description: The comment on `u_output` (lines 49-52) documents z as "1 with a scene copy for transmission". environment.glsl:30-31 and `scene_color_at` (environment.glsl:78) treat it as `1 + the least mip to read`, using `u_output.z - 1.0` as a LOD floor. The prelude is the reference for custom scene shaders (shader.rs module docs), so the block comment is what an author reads.
  - scenario: A custom scene shader that follows the block comment tests `u_output.z == 1.0` or uses z as a 0/1 weight. On frames where the copy's least mip is above 0 (reduced transmission resolution), z is 2 or more and the shader misbehaves.
  - fix: Change the comment to "z: 0 without a scene copy, else 1 + the least mip to read (scene_color_available / scene_color_at)".

## `packages/luxel/shaders/lib/subsurface.glsl`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L27: Subsurface profile integration repeats 32 light-independent profile evaluations per light**
  - description: `integrate_burley_diffusion` is called once per light from the OpenPBR light loop (surface_openpbr.glsl:332). Inside its 32-sample loop, `dist`, `burley_diffusion_profile(dist, shape)` (two vec3 `exp`) and `sum_r` depend only on the pixel's radius and mean free path; only `cos(theta + x)` depends on the light. It also runs for lights whose radiance at the pixel is 0.
  - scenario: A HAS_SUBSURFACE material under N lights costs N x 32 x (6 exp + sin + cos) per pixel, about 1500 exp calls at 8 lights, where 32 profile evaluations per pixel would be enough.
  - fix: Compute the 32 profile weights and `sum_r` once per pixel, before the light loop, and accumulate only the cosine term per light. Skip the call when the light's radiance is 0.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/lib/surface.glsl`

- [ ] **[low/api] L69: Includable chunk surface.glsl depends on a varying it neither declares nor lists in its "Needs"**
  - description: `volume_ray` (line 69) and `diffuse_through_volume` (line 83) read `v_model_scale`, which only pbr.frag declares (pbr.frag:22, before its includes). The header says "Needs common.glsl, object.glsl, environment.glsl". shader.rs advertises every entry of CHUNKS as `#include`-able from user scene shaders, and surface_gltf.glsl / surface_openpbr.glsl pull the same dependency in.
  - scenario: `create_scene_program` with a fragment shader that does `#include "brdf.glsl"` and `#include "surface.glsl"` to reuse the Surface struct or the transmission helpers fails to compile with an undeclared `v_model_scale`. The error names the chunk, not the missing declaration in the user's code.
  - fix: Have surface.glsl take the model scale as a parameter or a Surface field that pbr.frag fills. Otherwise declare the varying in the chunk and state in its header that the vertex stage must write it.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/lib/surface_openpbr.glsl`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L293: Back-lit terms (punctual transmission, diffuse transmission, subsurface wrap) are cancelled by the mesh's own shadow**
  - description: Both models multiply the whole per-light result by the shadow factor sampled at the shaded point: `radiance = light_radiance(...) * light_shadow_rgb(i, light, s.position, s.ng)` here, and `intensity = ... * light_shadow_rgb(...)` in surface_gltf.glsl:125-126. That factor also scales the terms that exist only for light arriving from behind the surface: the transmitted punctual light (surface_openpbr.glsl:349-360, surface_gltf.glsl:141-155), the diffuse-transmission BTDF under `NdotL < 0` (surface_openpbr.glsl:338-345, surface_gltf.glsl:131-139) and the subsurface wrap past the terminator (surface_openpbr.glsl:332-335). For those terms the shaded point is on the far side of its own mesh from the light, and that mesh is a shadow caster (casters.rs:58 only skips blended meshes; maps.rs:320 draws both faces). The receiver bias makes it worse: shadows.glsl:59-60 clamps `cos_t` to 0 for a surface facing away, so `sin_t = 1` and shadows.glsl:21 pushes the sample a full normal bias along `ng`, away from the light and behind the caster depth. Not run here; this follows from the code.
  - scenario: A leaf or lampshade with KHR_materials_diffuse_transmission (alpha mode OPAQUE or MASK), or a glass object with KHR_materials_transmission, lit by a directional, spot or point light with castShadow on and viewed from the side away from the light. The fragment is in the mesh's own shadow, so the shadow factor is 0 and the back-lit contribution is 0. The same scene with castShadow off shows the glow (the LightsPunctualLamp shade that the comment in surface.glsl is tuned for). Subsurface likewise loses all its wrap past the terminator.
  - fix: Take a separate shadow value for the from-behind terms: sample with the offset normal flipped toward the light (`-ng` when `dot(ng, l) < 0`), so a thin sheet does not shadow itself, and apply it only to those terms. Alternatively leave the shadow off the transmitted, BTDF and subsurface-wrap terms for thin-walled materials. Keep the current factor for the reflected terms.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L293: Shadow maps are sampled for lights that contribute nothing to the pixel**
  - description: `light_radiance(...) * light_shadow_rgb(...)` (and surface_gltf.glsl:125-126) evaluates the shadow unconditionally: 4 hardware PCF taps, 8 in a cascade overlap, plus the matrix transform and the cascade or face selection. It runs even when the attenuation is 0 (outside a point light's range or a spot's cone) and when the surface faces away with no back-lit layer compiled in.
  - scenario: Eight ranged point or spot lights with castShadow, each covering a small part of the frame: every lit pixel does 32 to 64 shadow-map comparisons, almost all multiplied by an attenuation of 0.
  - fix: Compute the attenuation first and call `light_shadow_rgb` only when it is above 0 and the light can contribute (NdotL > 0, or a transmission, diffuse-transmission or subsurface layer is compiled in).

## `packages/luxel/shaders/pbr.frag`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L269: Zero-length TANGENT or NORMAL produces NaN pixels, which nothing downstream contains**
  - description: `f.t = normalize(v_tangent.xyz)` (line 269) and `f.ng = normalize(v_normal)` (line 262) have no guard, unlike the derivative frame a few lines below, which checks its lengths explicitly. A zero tangent makes t and b NaN, and with a normal map `f.n = normalize(mat3(t, b, ng) * m)` is NaN, so the shaded colour is NaN. No shader in packages/luxel/shaders tests for NaN, so on an HDR target the value survives: exposure.frag sums raw texels for metering, the transmission scene copy is mip-mapped (output.rs:763, 779), and TAA blends it into history. Reasoned from the code, not run.
  - scenario: A glTF whose exporter wrote TANGENT (0,0,0,1), or a zero NORMAL, on vertices of degenerate-UV triangles; the material has a normal map and auto exposure is on. Those triangles render NaN, the metered average becomes NaN, and the eased exposure can stay NaN after the model leaves view. Glass in front shows NaN blocks from the copy's mips.
  - fix: Fall back when the squared length is about 0: use the derivative or arbitrary perpendicular frame for t and b, and the face normal for ng. Optionally sanitise the final colour once before `scene_output` so a NaN never reaches the HDR target.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/pbr.vert`

- [ ] **[medium/perf] L37: `flat` varying v_model_scale in every PBR variant forces ANGLE's flat-shading emulation on all PBR draws**
  - description: `flat out vec3 v_model_scale` (pbr.vert:37, pbr.frag:22) is declared and written in every variant, including DEPTH_ONLY shadow programs and materials without HAS_VOLUME, which never read it. WebGL's provoking vertex is the last one. Where the native convention is the first, ANGLE has to emulate it for any program with a flat varying: from memory of ANGLE's backends (not checked in this session), D3D11 adds a geometry shader to non-point draws and Metal rewrites or generates index buffers. The library never enables WEBGL_provoking_vertex; it is only listed in luxel-gl/src/extension.rs. The value does not need `flat`: without skinning it is identical at all three vertices (it comes from the instance's model matrix), and with skinning an interpolated scale is just as usable.
  - scenario: Any scene drawn in Chrome or Edge on Windows (ANGLE on D3D11) or in Safari or Chrome on macOS/iOS (ANGLE on Metal). Every PBR draw and every shadow-caster draw links a program with a flat varying and pays the emulation cost, although only HAS_VOLUME materials read the value.
  - fix: Drop `flat` from v_model_scale in pbr.vert and pbr.frag and declare and write it only under HAS_VOLUME (never in DEPTH_ONLY). If flat varyings are kept, enable WEBGL_provoking_vertex and select FIRST_VERTEX_CONVENTION_WEBGL when it is available.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L119: `u_normal_matrix` macro re-reads the instance and inverts a 3x3 per vertex, although the model matrix was just read**
  - description: In the vertex stage `u_model` and `u_normal_matrix` are macros (object_instanced.glsl:43-44). pbr.vert:118 reads `mat4 model = u_model; // read once`, then line 119 expands `u_normal_matrix` to `transpose(inverse(mat3(object_model())))`, which fetches the same three instance texels again before inverting. It is also evaluated in variants that cannot use it: meshes without HAS_NORMAL, and DEPTH_ONLY shadow programs, where shadow_key keeps HAS_NORMAL so v_normal is still written though the fragment stage never reads it.
  - scenario: Every non-skinned vertex of every PBR and shadow-caster draw does 6 texelFetches instead of 3, plus a mat3 inverse, unless the driver happens to fold or strip them.
  - fix: Use `mat3 normal_matrix = transpose(inverse(mat3(model)));` in pbr.vert, inside `#ifdef HAS_NORMAL` and skipped under DEPTH_ONLY. Consider making object_normal_matrix take the already-read model matrix.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/skybox.frag`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L23: Environment background under an orthographic camera uses 1/P00 as a field of view**
  - description: `view_ray = vec3(v_ndc.x / u_proj[0][0], v_ndc.y / u_proj[1][1], -1.0)` is the perspective unprojection. For an orthographic projection (Camera::orthographic, camera.rs:36; gtao.frag distinguishes it with u_proj[3][3]) 1/P00 and 1/P11 are the half extents in world units, so the background is drawn as a perspective view whose half-angle is atan(half_height): it depends on the scene's unit scale and changes as the ortho size is zoomed.
  - scenario: setCamera({type: 'orthographic', ymag: 10}) with setBackground on: the environment appears as an extreme wide-angle view (+-84 degrees vertically), and zooming the orthographic camera (ymag 10 -> 1) zooms and distorts the background although an orthographic view has a single view direction.
  - fix: Branch on u_proj[3][3] > 0.5: for orthographic cameras use a fixed direction (the camera's forward axis) or a chosen constant field of view instead of the half extents.

## `packages/luxel/shaders/taa.frag`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L70: TAA turns an overflowed (Inf) HDR pixel into NaN history that never clears**
  - description: tonemap() is `c *= exposure(); return c / (max3(c) + 1.0);`. For an Inf texel in u_current this is Inf/Inf = NaN. Nothing sanitises it: the 3x3 statistics (m1/m2) become NaN so the clip is a no-op (`m > 1.0` is false), `mix(history, current, factor)` is NaN, and the NaN is written to the history and copied back into the HDR target by TAA_COPY_FRAG. Next frame history_catmull_rom multiplies every tap by its weight, and with a still camera f == 0 gives weights of exactly 0 for the outer taps: 0 * NaN = NaN, so every pixel whose 9 taps touch the NaN texel (offsets -1, 0, +2) is poisoned too. `max(r, 0.0)` either keeps the NaN (GLSL definition: the region then grows 1-2 px per frame) or returns 0 (D3D/Metal semantics: a black block around the highlight, because the neighbours' clip is disabled by the NaN statistics). The Inf source is real: the scene writes unclamped radiance to RGBA16F (scene_output in scene.frag.glsl has no cap; D_GGX floors alpha at 0.002, a peak of about 8e4, so a roughness-0 metal under a punctual light of intensity of roughly 4 or more exceeds 65504; a large emissive strength does too). env_bake.frag:46-47 and :96-100 already note that such values store as infinity and that 'TAA then smears' them, but only the environment is capped.
  - scenario: setTaa(true); a flat polished metal (roughness 0, metallic 1) lit by a directional light with intensity 10, camera still.
