The pixels at the highlight peak overflow RGBA16F to Inf; after the resolve they are NaN in the history, the highlight centre shows black, and the black/NaN area persists (and on drivers whose max() propagates NaN, grows every frame) until the next TAA reset (resize or camera switch).
  - fix: Make the resolve robust to non-finite input: clamp what it reads (e.g. `min(c, vec3(65504.0))` on u_current and u_history samples, and replace NaN with 0 via `c == c` tests) before tonemap(), and/or clamp radiance once at the source in scene_output (min(linear, 65504.0)) so no pass ever sees Inf.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L20: Late (unjittered) meshes are depth-tested against the jittered opaque depth: 1px crawl on every occluding silhouette**
  - description: The design noted here ('transmissive and transparent meshes draw after the resolve, unjittered') leaves the late pass testing against the depth the jittered opaque pass wrote: HdrOutput::prepare_late targets self.fb, whose depth attachment is taa.depth (output.rs:1205), and drop_jitter (js/mod.rs:890) removes the jitter from the projection for those draws. The opaque depth edge moves by up to +-0.5 px with the Halton index each frame, while the colour under it is the stable resolved edge. A silhouette pixel is therefore covered by the late mesh behind the opaque object on some frames and not on others; nothing accumulates the late pass, so the result is not averaged.
  - scenario: TAA on, setGroundShadow (ground.frag plane, drawn in the late pass) or a tinted glass/transparent mesh partly behind an opaque object, camera still and frames rendered continuously: the pixels along the opaque object's outline flicker between 'shadow/glass drawn over the antialiased edge pixel' and 'not drawn', with the 8-frame jitter period. With render-on-demand the outline freezes in whichever phase the last frame had (dark or light fringe).
  - fix: Give the late pass a depth that matches its unjittered rasterisation: e.g. dilate/bias the comparison for late draws (test against the nearest of the depth neighbourhood), or keep the late pass jittered and resolve it with its own accumulation; at minimum document the fringe and let FXAA's late mask (fxaa.frag late_near) always include opaque-edge pixels adjacent to late pixels.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L139: After a TAA reset the single aliased frame keeps ~64% weight for the 30 'settle' frames**
  - description: On reset the shader writes `frag_color = vec4(original.rgb, 1.0 / MIN_HISTORY_BLEND_RATE)` (confidence 66.7). On the following still frames `confidence = min(confidence + 10.0, 1000.0)` and `factor = clamp(1.0 / confidence, 0.015, 0.1)` = 0.015 immediately, so each new jittered frame contributes only 1.5%. After TAA_SETTLE = 30 frames (js/on_demand.rs:25, 'its 8-frame jitter cycle a few times over') the picture is still 0.985^29 = 64% the first, unjittered, single-sample frame. On a geometric edge the variance clip does not help (the 3x3 box spans both sides). This is Bevy's behaviour ported as is, but Bevy renders continuously; here render-on-demand and capture() stop after 30 frames. Resets happen on the first frame, every resize/allocate (output.rs:1236), a camera switch (js/mod.rs:1196), key-output or transmission-scale changes.
  - scenario: scene.capture(camera, {width: 2*canvas.width, height: 2*canvas.height}) with TAA on: the resize reallocates the target (taa.reset = true), frame 0 is the reset, frames 1-29 blend at 0.015. An edge pixel with true coverage 0.5 ends at about 0.18 or 0.82 instead of 0.5: the captured image is visibly aliased although 30 TAA frames were spent. The same holds for the first still image shown after load or a resize with needsRender-driven rendering.
  - fix: After a reset accumulate as a running mean instead of starting at the minimum blend rate: store confidence 1.0 on reset and, while a 'frames since reset' count is small, use factor = 1/(n+1) (e.g. keep the count in the confidence channel and add 1 instead of 10 until it reaches 1/MIN_HISTORY_BLEND_RATE).

## `packages/luxel/src/animation.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L130: sample() underflows and panics when a channel's first keyframe time is NaN**
  - description: The NaN guard covers only the query time `t`. If times[0] is NaN, `t <= times[0]` is false, and for t below the last key `times.partition_point(|&x| x <= t)` returns 0, so `- 1` underflows: a panic in debug, an out-of-bounds index panic in release. The loader (luxel-gltf animation.rs:66) copies keyframe times unchecked, and the clip duration ignores NaN (f32::max fold), so the clip looks playable.
  - scenario: A corrupt or hostile glTF whose animation input accessor starts with NaN, e.g. times [NaN, 1.0]. scene.playAnimation(root, i) calls apply(.., 0.0) and then sample: a wasm trap inside a `&mut self` method of Scene, after which the whole Scene object is unusable.
  - fix: Drop channels with non-finite or non-increasing times in read_animations, and make sample robust with `partition_point(..).saturating_sub(1)` and a clamp of k to `last - 1`.

## `packages/luxel/src/brdf_lut.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L18: BRDF LUT is integrated on the main thread at every renderer creation and context restore**
  - description: brdf_lut() runs 1024 texels x (256 GGX + 512 sheen samples) with powf/exp/cos per sample synchronously inside SceneRenderer::new (render.rs:353 via brdf_lut_texture), and again on rebuild after a context loss. The result is a constant.
  - scenario: Measured natively (rustc -O) at 20-30 ms for one call; in wasm on a phone that is several tens of milliseconds of blocked main thread added to every scene start and every context restore, for a table that never changes.
  - fix: Cache the table (thread_local/OnceCell) so restores and further scenes reuse it, and/or ship it precomputed (include_bytes of 32*32*3 half floats) or bake it on the GPU with a fullscreen pass.

## `packages/luxel/src/culling.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L28: Infinite, huge or inverted Bounds make a mesh always culled instead of never culled**
  - description: `Bounds::transformed` computes `center = (min+max)*0.5` and `half = (max-min)*0.5` with no guard. With ±INFINITY, `center` is NaN. With ±f32::MAX, `max-min` overflows to inf, and `m.x_axis.abs() * half.x` produces NaN for the zero components of the axis (0*inf). `Frustum::intersects` (line 76) then compares against NaN, which is false, so the box is reported outside. With min > max, `half` is negative and the test demands the centre be deeper inside than the box size. `Scene::setBounds` (js/mod.rs:563-576) only checks the array length, and `Bounds::new` validates nothing.
  - scenario: `scene.setBounds(e, [-Infinity,-Infinity,-Infinity], [Infinity,Infinity,Infinity])` (or ±3.4e38), a common "never cull this" idiom for a vertex-displaced or skybox-like mesh. The mesh is culled every frame from the camera pass and from every shadow map (casters.rs:69-73), and from `pick`. `setBounds(e, max, min)` with swapped arguments makes the mesh pop out while still on screen near the frustum edges.
  - fix: In `transformed` (or `Bounds::new`), order min/max per component and treat non-finite extents as "always intersects" (for example, return true from `intersects` when `half` is not finite). Also validate finiteness in `setBounds`.

## `packages/luxel/src/environment.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L356: BakeJob::start never checks the image against MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE (or zero size); the bake fails later with a wrong 'no half-float targets' error**
  - description: BakeJob::start only checks rgbe.len() == w*h*4. make() then calls texture_storage_2d for the RGBA8 and RGBA16F equirect textures at the image's full size and ignores the result (luxel-gl's storage_fits refuses and only logs when width/height exceed MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE or are < 1). decode_hdr_rgbe accepts up to 16384x16384, so nothing upstream catches it. The job is returned as Ok; stage 0 then attaches the storage-less equirect, framebuffer_is_complete is false, and step returns HALF_FLOAT_TARGETS ("this device can't render to half-float textures"). In the JS facade setEnvironmentHdr therefore does not throw; the failure only shows up frames later as a console error with a misleading cause, and the previous environment stays.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr(bytes of an 8192x4096 .hdr, 256) on a device whose MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE is 4096 (or a 16384x8192 file on an 8192 device): console shows 'texture storage size ... exceeds MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE', 'texture_sub_image_2d ... ignored', then '[luxel] environment: environment: this device can't render to half-float textures'. Same path for a hand-built RgbeImage { width: 0, height: 0, rgbe: vec![] } through the public Rust API (passes the length check). A hand-built image with w*h*4 > i32::MAX also overflows the i32 multiply at line 356 (panic in debug).
  - fix: In BakeJob::start validate 1 <= w,h <= limits::max_texture_size() (and do the length check in usize/u64) and return a specific error synchronously; better, downsample an oversized image on the CPU (2x2 box in RGBE-decoded space) to fit before upload.
- [ ] **[medium/perf] L411: Bake allocates the full-resolution equirect as RGBA16F with a full mip chain regardless of the cube size**
  - description: make() uploads the RGBE image at source resolution (RGBA8) and allocates a second texture of the same size in RGBA16F with mip_levels(w, h) levels, both alive together during stage 0, on top of the decoded Vec<u8> in wasm memory. The target cube is typically 256 per face (a face covers w/4 source texels), so anything beyond roughly 4*size source width is only ever read through its mips. Transient GPU memory is w*h*4 + w*h*8*4/3 bytes.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr with a common 8192x4096 HDRI and size 256: 128 MB (RGBA8) + ~341 MB (RGBA16F + mips) of GPU memory plus 128 MB in the wasm heap for a bake whose result is ~3 MB of cubes; on phones/integrated GPUs this is enough to fail allocation or lose the context. A 16384x8192 file needs ~1.9 GB.
  - fix: Cap the equirect at about 4*size x 2*size (CPU box-downsample the RGBE before upload, or decode on the GPU into a reduced-size target by averaging source texels in the DECODE pass), and/or free the RGBA8 texture's twin earlier; at minimum bound the size and report.
- [ ] **[low/api] L103: Environment::bake silently bakes R11F_G11F_B10F cubes; docs say RGBA16F and there is no way to choose**
  - description: The synchronous Environment::bake hardcodes compact = true in BakeJob::start, so on devices with EXT_color_buffer_float the kept cubes are R11F_G11F_B10F (5/6-bit mantissas, rounded in the shader). The function and module docs describe RGBA16F cube faces, and the caller cannot opt out, unlike BakeJob::start / PendingEnvironment::start which take the flag.
  - scenario: A Rust user calls Environment::bake(ctx, &image, 256) expecting half-float cubes (e.g. to read them back or compare against a reference): gets ~1.5% quantised R11G11B10 data on desktop but RGBA16F on devices without EXT_color_buffer_float.
  - fix: Add a `compact: bool` parameter (or default to false) and document the format.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L497: A bake 'stage' is all six faces of a mip, so large sizes put hundreds of millions of cube samples in one step**
  - description: PendingEnvironment exists so a bake does not stall rendering, but step() draws all 6 faces of a specular mip in one go with 128 filtered cube samples per texel. Work per step scales with size^2; BakeJob::start allows up to 2048.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr(bytes, 2048): the mip-1 stage shades 6 * 1024^2 texels * 128 samples (~805M textureLod calls) in a single task between two frames; at 1024 it is ~201M. On integrated/mobile GPUs that is a multi-hundred-millisecond to multi-second GPU stall (visible hitch, risk of the GPU watchdog losing the context) - the thing the staged bake is meant to avoid.
  - fix: Make the unit of work a face (or a face tile for big mips) instead of a mip level, e.g. advance (mip, face) per step when side > 256.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L546: PendingEnvironment dropped without cancel leaks the bake's result cubes (public Rust API)**
  - description: PendingEnvironment has no Drop. The spawned task keeps running while state is Running and stores Done(Ok(env)) in the shared Rc; if the owner has dropped the PendingEnvironment, the last Rc goes away with the Environment inside and nothing calls Environment::destroy, so both cube textures stay in the context's pool. The JS facade always calls cancel()/take(), but PendingEnvironment is exported from lib.rs.
  - scenario: Rust user: let p = PendingEnvironment::start(...)?; drop(p); (or replaces it with a new one without cancel) -> the bake still runs to the end and its diffuse + specular cubes are never freed.
  - fix: impl Drop for PendingEnvironment that does what cancel() does (or have the task hold only a Weak to the state and cancel the job when upgrade fails).
- [ ] **[low/async] L578: A bake overtaken by a loss and restore destroys handles of the dead generation and logs stale-handle errors**
  - description: When the bake task wakes and finds `ctx.generation() != generation` (line 577), or `check_linked` fails because the baker's programs went with the lost context (line 572, then 593), it calls `job.cancel()`. That runs `ctx.destroy_texture` / `destroy_framebuffer` on handles from the lost generation. Once `on_restored` has returned, `lost_until_restored` is false, so `Context::stale` reports each one: up to five textures and a framebuffer. `PendingEnvironment::cancel` (line 635) does the same for a finished, untaken result when `Scene::restore` cancels it (`env.destroy` on two dead cubes). Nothing leaks; the objects are gone. (Listed unverified in review-night-raw.md; confirmed.)
  - scenario: `setEnvironmentHdr`, then a context loss and restore before the bake finishes, or after it finished with no frame drawn since (hidden tab). After the restore the console shows several '[luxel] destroy_texture: texture Handle(..) is destroyed or invalid; ignored' errors.
  - fix: Give `BakeJob` and `PendingEnvironment` the generation they were made in and skip the destroy calls when it differs from `ctx.generation()`, as `EnvBaker::destroy` and `Model::destroy` already do.
- [ ] **[low/async] L584: Stage spacing uses the wall clock (Date.now), which is not monotonic**
  - description: The wait between stages loops on next_poll_round while js_sys::Date::now() - last < STEP_MS. Date.now follows system clock adjustments.
  - scenario: The system clock is set back (NTP/manual/DST-less timezone fix) during a bake: Date.now() - last stays negative for as long as the clock was moved back, the task spins a 1 ms timer for that whole time and the bake (and whenReady, which awaits finished()) hangs until the clock catches up.
  - fix: Use performance.now() (monotonic), or count poll rounds / treat a negative difference as elapsed.
- [ ] **[low/async] L636: cancel() / the task destroy handles from a lost generation after the context was restored, logging stale-handle errors**
  - description: PendingEnvironment::cancel destroys a Done(Ok(env)) without checking the context generation, and the task's job.cancel() at line 578 runs for the 'generation != ctx.generation()' case too. Once the context is restored, Context::stale is no longer quiet (lost_until_restored is false), so each destroy_texture / destroy_framebuffer on the dead handles reports '[luxel] destroy_texture: texture ... is destroyed or invalid'. EnvBaker::destroy in the same file does guard on generation.
  - scenario: A bake finishes (state Done) but no frame has applied it yet; the context is lost and restored; Scene::restore calls p.cancel() -> env.destroy on two dead cube handles -> console errors. Likewise when loss and restore both happen between two poll rounds of a running bake (throttled background tab): job.cancel() on up to five dead textures and a framebuffer.
  - fix: Record the generation in PendingEnvironment/BakeJob and skip the destroys when it differs from ctx.generation() (the objects went with the old context).
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L636: Destroy paths that are not generation-aware log stale-handle errors after a restore**
  - description: Context::stale is quiet only between the lost event and the end of on_restored; the scene cleans up later, at its first call. PendingEnvironment::cancel (line 635-640) destroys the cubes of a bake that was Done but unapplied; BakeJob::cancel (517-533) is called by the bake task when it wakes after the restore (state Over or generation changed, line 577-579) and destroys up to five textures and a framebuffer of the lost generation; GpuProfiler::destroy (luxel-gl profiler.rs:156-162) destroys drained queries without resync (scene.setGpuProfiling(false) / scene.destroy() before the next frame); HdrOutput::set_fxaa(false) / set_auto_exposure(false) (output.rs:514-519, 462-464 with `lost` hard-coded false) on the still-lost self.hdr. Each handle produces a distinct console.error via error_once and a permanent entry in its dedupe set.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr, the tab is hidden (rAF stops, the bake's timer task finishes or is mid-way), the context is lost and restored in the background. On return the first renderFrame logs several '[luxel] destroy_texture: texture ... is destroyed or invalid; ignored' errors although nothing is wrong.
  - fix: Give PendingEnvironment/BakeJob and GpuProfiler::destroy the generation check the other destroy paths have (skip GPU deletes when their generation differs), and drop the lost HdrOutput in restore() before any setter can reach it.

## `packages/luxel/src/exposure.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L169: dt <= 0 is treated as 'no history' and snaps the exposure; Date.now's coarse, non-monotonic clock makes that happen in normal use**
  - description: force = !(dt > 0.0 && dt <= 1000.0), and now_ms comes from js_sys::Date::now() (integer milliseconds, clamped coarser by some browsers, and not monotonic). A second metering within the same clock tick has dt == 0, which is forced: the 1x1 result is overwritten with the instantaneous average instead of being left as is. The same happens when the wall clock steps backwards.
  - scenario: (1) Two render() calls in the same millisecond (a second view/capture right after a frame): the eased exposure is replaced by that frame's raw value - a visible jump, and TAA's weights jump with it. (2) Firefox with resistFingerprinting / Tor Browser (Date.now quantised to 16.7-100 ms) on a 120-144 Hz display: most frames see dt == 0, so the exposure snaps every frame and never eases (flicker when looking from dark to bright). (3) Clock adjusted backwards: snap.
  - fix: Force only when last_ms == 0 or dt > 1000; for dt <= 0 skip the END pass (keep the previous result) or ease with adjust 0. Feed a monotonic clock (performance.now()) from js/mod.rs:911.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L174: Auto exposure easing factor speed*dt is not clamped to 1: overshoots and oscillates between min and max**
  - description: u_adjust = speed * dt (dt up to 1 s) is used directly in exposure.frag: prev + (measured - prev) * u_adjust. Nothing clamps it to [0, 1]; set_auto_exposure_options only does speed.max(0.0). For u_adjust > 1 the result overshoots the target, and for > 2 the error grows each frame until the min/max clamp, so the exposure flips between extremes. The public doc calls speed 'the share of the way it moves per second', but larger values are accepted.
  - scenario: setAutoExposureOptions(0.4, 0.05, 10000, 5) (adapt in ~0.2 s) with frames 500 ms apart (on-demand rendering, a slow device, a throttled tab): u_adjust = 2.5, each metering lands 1.5x the error on the other side of the target; successive frames alternate darker/brighter with growing amplitude until clamped at 0.05 / 10000 (black and blown-out frames). Even speed 2 with dt 0.6 s overshoots visibly.
  - fix: Use a frame-rate independent factor clamped to 1, e.g. 1 - exp(-speed * dt), or clamp(speed * dt, 0, 1), before setting f_adjust.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L177: One Inf/NaN texel in the HDR frame poisons the metered exposure (black screen for many seconds, or for good with NaN)**
  - description: The BEGIN pass sums raw texels of the RGBA16F HDR target and the chain averages them; neither the Rust side nor exposure.frag sanitises non-finite values. An Inf texel makes the whole average Inf, which END clamps to max_luminance (10000 by default) and stores; later frames then ease down from 10000 at `speed` per second. A NaN texel (or Inf with u_adjust == 0, i.e. speed 0: Inf*0) yields NaN, clamp(NaN) is undefined in GLSL ES and by the spec's own min/max definition passes NaN through, and since END eases from u_prev the NaN is then sticky until a >1 s gap forces a reset; present.frag/taa.frag divide by it. Scene shading does not clamp to the half-float range (only env_bake.frag does, with a comment about exactly this kind of infinity).
  - scenario: Auto exposure on, physically lit scene, a sharp specular highlight from a punctual light on a low-roughness surface exceeds 65504 for one frame in one pixel -> stored as +Inf in RGBA16F -> metered luminance jumps to 10000 -> displayed exposure drops ~10000x: the screen goes black and takes ~ln(10000)/0.5 = 18 s to recover at the default speed. With a NaN texel (degenerate normal/tangent) the result becomes NaN and the image stays black while frames keep coming less than 1 s apart.
  - fix: In EXPOSURE_BEGIN clamp each fetched texel (e.g. min(c, vec3(65504.0)) and replace NaN via `c == c ? c : 0`), and in EXPOSURE_END fall back to the fresh value when prev_lum is not finite/positive.

## `packages/luxel/src/gpu_pick.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L120: GPU pick draws back faces the scene culls, so invisible surfaces are picked**
  - description: The pick render state is RenderState::new() with only the depth test set; cull_face defaults to false. The scene draws single-sided glTF materials with cull_face = !double_sided (luxel-gltf material.rs:1006). Pick ignores the mesh's own state, so a back face that is not on screen still writes its entity and depth, and wins whenever it is nearest.
  - scenario: A single-sided plane or card seen from behind, or the camera inside a single-sided closed mesh (a room with outward normals): the frame shows the objects behind it, but pick() returns the culled mesh.
  - fix: Carry the mesh's culling into the pick draw: intern two pick states (culling on and off, same cull mode and front face as the mesh's state) and choose per mesh from mesh.state.

## `packages/luxel/src/hdr.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L68: Header parser does not accept CRLF line endings**
  - description: The end of the header is detected with l.is_empty() on lines split at '\n' only. With CRLF endings the blank line is "\r", so the loop keeps going: the resolution line is swallowed as a header line and the binary pixel data is then scanned for newlines as header text.
  - scenario: A .hdr whose text header was written with \r\n (some Windows exporters/converters): decode_hdr_rgbe returns 'HDR: header ended early' or 'HDR: unsupported orientation "<binary garbage>"' for a file other loaders that trim whitespace open fine.
  - fix: Strip a trailing b'\r' from each header line (and from the resolution line) before testing/parsing.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L97: decode_hdr_rgbe allocates and zero-fills width*height*4 from the header before looking at how much pixel data exists**
  - description: The header's claimed size (up to 16384x16384 = 1 GiB of RGBE) is reserved and zero-filled before any scanline is read. try_reserve_exact turns an outright allocation failure into an error, but a successful reservation grows the wasm linear memory, which never shrinks again.
  - scenario: A truncated download or a crafted ~60-byte file '#?RADIANCE\n\n-Y 16384 +X 16384\n' passed to setEnvironmentHdr: the decoder grows the wasm heap by 1 GiB and writes it all, then fails with 'pixel data ended early'; the page keeps the 1 GiB heap for its lifetime (or the tab is killed on mobile).
  - fix: Before allocating, reject when the remaining bytes cannot hold the image: even fully run-length coded, a new-style row needs at least 4 + 4*2*ceil(w/127) bytes (4*w for flat/short rows), so check data.len() >= h * that; or grow the Vec row by row.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L203: encode_hdr mishandles infinite/huge values and does not check rgb.len() against width*height**
  - description: For an infinite component m.log2().floor() as i32 saturates to i32::MAX and `+ 1` overflows: a panic in debug builds; in release it wraps, so the exponent byte becomes 128 and the mantissas saturate to 255, i.e. the pixel is written as ~1.0. Finite values above 2^127 get exponent byte 255 with a scale computed for the true exponent (also wrong). Separately, the header is written from width/height while the pixel loop runs over whatever rgb holds, so a short or long slice produces a file whose header disagrees with its data.
  - scenario: encode_hdr(1, 1, &[f32::INFINITY, 0.0, 0.0]) panics with 'attempt to add with overflow' under cargo test / debug; in release the brightest pixel of the image round-trips as 1.0. encode_hdr(4, 4, &rgb[..30]) returns a file that decode_hdr rejects with 'pixel data ended early'.
  - fix: Clamp m to the RGBE range before taking the exponent (e.g. m.min(f32::MAX) and e.clamp(-127, 127), scaling mantissas accordingly), and assert/return an error when rgb.len() != width*height*3.

## `packages/luxel/src/hierarchy.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/perf] L215: A hierarchy rebuild restamps every WorldMatrix as changed, invalidating all shadow-map caches and object groups**
  - description: When needs_rebuild is true (one Transform added or removed, one entity despawned, one set_parent), recompute runs with all = true and writes `wm.0 = m` through Mut::deref_mut for every entity. That stamps every WorldMatrix with the new tick even when the matrix is identical. Consumers key on that tick: lighting/cache.rs:64 and :74 hash WorldMatrix::changed_tick into each shadow view's signature, and render.rs ensure_object_group rewrites each object's group when the tick moves. The doc comment's promise of touching only what moved does not hold on rebuild frames.
  - scenario: A static scene with cached shadow maps (directional cascades, spot and point lights). The app spawns or despawns one unrelated entity with a Transform, or calls setParent once. That frame every shadow view's signature changes and all shadow maps are re-rendered, and all per-object groups are rewritten. An app that spawns or despawns something every frame (pooled projectiles, markers) never gets a cached shadow frame.
  - fix: In recompute, mark an entity dirty and stamp it only if its matrix actually changed (or it is new): compare with the stored value (set_if_neq) when `all` is set, and propagate `dirty` from that comparison.

## `packages/luxel/src/instances.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L105: The batch index reallocates one Vec per batch key every frame**
  - description: `Batches` says its allocations are kept across frames, but `clear()` calls `self.index.clear()`, which drops every `Vec<usize>` value. The next frame's `entry(key).or_default()` followed by `slots.push(i)` allocates a fresh Vec for each distinct (dest, program, state, vao, material) key. `collect_shadows` does the same with `shadow_batch_index` (render.rs:1121) and also builds new `out` and `objects` Vecs each frame that shadow maps are redrawn (render.rs:1116-1117); the Vec taken from `take_caster_draws` is dropped.
  - scenario: A scene with 2000 distinct mesh/material combinations does about 2000 small heap allocations and frees per frame in the wasm allocator just for the index. This doubles on frames where shadow maps redraw.
  - fix: Keep the per-key Vecs: clear the values in place (`for v in index.values_mut() { v.clear() }`, with an occasional full clear to bound stale keys), or store the first batch index and chain batches through a `next` field in `Batch`. Reuse `out`/`objects` as fields, as `shadow_batches` already is.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L202: Growing the instance texture past MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE destroys the working texture and never recovers**
  - description: `upload` rounds the needed rows up to a power of two and calls `make_texture`. `texture_storage_2d` returns nothing, and when `storage_fits` fails (height > MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE) it only logs. `make_texture` still returns Ok with an unallocated texture. Line 203 then destroys the old, valid texture, binds the empty one, and sets `self.rows = rows`. Because `rows > self.rows` is never true again, the texture stays unallocated even after the instance count drops. Every later frame calls `texture_sub_image_2d` on it, which fails `region_fits`.
  - scenario: A device with MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE 2048 (the WebGL2 minimum) holds 2048*1024/3 = about 699k slots; a 4096 device holds about 1.4M. A crowd of skinned characters can reach this, since each primitive pushes all its joints and moving ones push them three times with TAA, as can a few hundred meshes with instanceCount >= 4096. For one frame that exceeds the limit, every instanced, skinned and batched mesh in the scene samples an incomplete texture and collapses or vanishes. It stays that way until the context is rebuilt.
  - fix: Check the limit before replacing: if `rows > ctx.limits().max_texture_size`, return Err and keep the old texture (ideally also clamp draws). Alternatively, widen the texture (a WIDTH of 2048 or 4096, passed to the shader) before adding rows. Only swap textures after the new storage is known to be allocated.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L208: Instance upload does three full passes over the data every frame anything moves**
  - description: Each frame `upload` compares all of `data` against `uploaded`, converts every float with a separate `extend_from_slice(&v.to_le_bytes())` into `bytes`, zero-pads to whole rows, uploads everything, and then copies `data` into `uploaded` again. A single moved object, or any animated skin, re-uploads the whole texture, and the unchanged-data fast path still costs the full compare. The data is held three times (`data`, `uploaded`, `bytes`).
  - scenario: 20k instances plus skin joints is about 300k floats. One spinning mesh makes every frame do a 1.2 MB compare, a 300k-iteration byte conversion, a 1.2 MB texSubImage2D and a 1.2 MB copy.
  - fix: Upload straight from `data` as bytes (wasm is little-endian; a `bytemuck::cast_slice` or a Float32Array view avoids `bytes`), padding only the last row. Limit the upload to the rows that differ from `uploaded`, or drop `uploaded` and track a dirty flag.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/animation.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L196: setAnimationSpeed is silently dropped unless a clip is playing, and playAnimation resets it to 1**
  - description: The speed lives on the Playback, not on the model: set_animation_speed only writes when `a.playing` is Some and otherwise does nothing without a report (also for a root with no animations or a dead entity). play_animation creates `Playback::new(index, looping)` with speed 1, discarding the speed in force. The setter's doc ('Seconds of animation per second of advanceAnimations (default 1 ...)') reads as a per-model setting and mentions neither; only crossfadeAnimation's doc says the new clip runs at 1.
  - scenario: `scene.setAnimationSpeed(root, 0.5); scene.playAnimation(root, 0, true)` plays at full speed. `playAnimation(root, 1, true)` while clip 0 runs at 0.5 switches back to speed 1. Neither logs anything.
  - fix: Keep the speed on ModelAnimations (applied to whatever plays, surviving play/crossfade), or document that it applies to the playing clip only and report when nothing is playing.
- [ ] **[low/api] L196: setAnimationSpeed is silently dropped when nothing plays and reset by every play/crossfade**
  - description: The speed lives on the current `Playback`. `setAnimationSpeed` does nothing, with no console message, when the root has no playing clip or is not a model root. `playAnimation` (line 81) and `crossfadeAnimation` install `Playback::new`, whose speed is 1. The README lists `animationSpeed(root)` among the settings whose getter reads back what the setter set, and its own example restarts a clip on 'finished' with `crossfadeAnimation`, which resets the speed. `playAnimation` and `crossfadeAnimation` throw for a non-model root, while `setAnimationSpeed`, `setAnimationTime` and `stopAnimation` ignore it silently.
  - scenario: `scene.setAnimationSpeed(root, 0.5); scene.playAnimation(root, 0, true);` plays at 1× and `animationSpeed(root)` returns 1. A looping slow-motion setup loses its speed whenever the app switches clips.
  - fix: Keep the speed on `ModelAnimations` (per root) and copy it into new Playbacks, or document that it applies to the playing clip only and report the no-op case in the console as other setters do.
- [ ] **[low/api] L221: advanceAnimations ignores a non-finite dt without the report every other setter gives**
  - description: advance() returns an empty list when `dt` is not finite (animation.rs:399) and advance_animations passes `seconds` straight through. setAnimationTime, setAnimationSpeed and setMorphWeights all go through options::finite and log '[luxel] <op>: not a finite number; ignored'; this one is silent.
  - scenario: The usual first-frame bug `scene.advanceAnimations((now - last) / 1000)` with `last` undefined passes NaN on every frame: animations never move, no event is returned and nothing appears in the console.
  - fix: Check `options::finite("advanceAnimations", &[seconds])` before calling advance and return the empty array when it fails.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/capture.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L79: A capture too big for the GPU permanently turns off TAA / HDR / MSAA for the live canvas**
  - description: capture() resizes the canvas and calls render_frame, which goes through prepare_output at the capture size. If HdrOutput::allocate cannot build its targets at that size (output.rs:1243 `the half-float target is incomplete`, the usual result of GL_OUT_OF_MEMORY on texStorage/renderbufferStorage), prepare_output treats it as a capability failure: with TAA on it calls give_up_taa (self.taa = false for good), otherwise give_up_hdr (self.hdr_wanted = false for good), both only with a console warning. For MSAA, allocate's fallback loop (output.rs:1172-1193) halves `self.samples` inside the HdrOutput when the multisampled framebuffer is incomplete and that lowered value is kept when the target is reallocated at the canvas size afterwards. The capture itself still resolves, with a picture drawn through the direct (non-HDR, no post) path or without antialiasing. The doc only says memory `grows with the size ... which a phone may not have`, not that the scene's settings are changed.
  - scenario: Phone or integrated GPU, HDR + TAA (setQuality('high')): await scene.capture(cam, {width: 8192, height: 8192}) -> first frame: allocation incomplete -> TAA given up; next of the 30 frames: HDR given up -> blob is an LDR direct-path image; after capture scene.taa() === false and scene.hdr() === false, AO/FXAA/sharpening/auto exposure silently gone on the live canvas until the app calls setHdr(true)/setTaa(true) again. With MSAA instead of TAA: 4x fails at 8192x8192 (about 2 GB of RGBA16F samples) -> samples drop to 0 and the canvas stays un-antialiased after the capture until setSamples is called.
  - fix: Treat failures during capture as capture failures: remember taa / hdr_wanted / fxaa / auto_exposure before the capture frames and restore them (and drop self.hdr so samples are re-derived from Scene.samples) after the canvas is back at its size; reject the promise (or retry smaller) when the capture frame did not go through the expected output path.
- [ ] **[low/api] L40: capture is documented to reject without a canvas or with a lost context, but throws synchronously**
  - description: The doc says 'Rejects without a canvas or with a lost context' and the type is `Promise<Blob>`. The function returns `Result<Promise, Error>`, so those two cases, and bad options, throw from the call itself. `pick` and `loadModel` behave the same way for their synchronous checks.
  - scenario: `scene.capture(cam).catch(showError)` in non-async code: on a lost context the exception escapes the `.catch` and becomes an uncaught error.
  - fix: Return a rejected promise for these cases (wrap the body in `future_to_promise` or `Promise::reject`), or change the doc to 'throws'.
- [ ] **[low/api] L105: capture replaces a viewport the app set on the default framebuffer with the full drawing buffer**
  - description: When the capture size differs from the canvas, capture sets the default framebuffer's viewport to the whole drawing buffer for the picture and again after restoring the canvas size (set_viewport_to_buffer), instead of putting back the viewport that was there. renderFrame sizes everything from that viewport (fb.width()/height()), so an app that set its own with defaultFramebuffer().setViewport and does not use auto-resize keeps rendering with a different viewport after the capture. In the non-resized path the capture is drawn with the custom viewport while toBlob encodes the whole canvas.
  - scenario: ctx.defaultFramebuffer().setViewport(new Viewport(0, 0, 640, 360)) on a 1280x720 canvas (half-resolution or sub-rectangle rendering); await scene.capture(cam, {width: 1920, height: 1080}) -> afterwards renderFrame draws at 1280x720 full-canvas: the app's viewport is gone.
  - fix: Read the viewport before the capture and restore that exact Viewport afterwards (only the capture frames need the buffer-sized one).

## `packages/luxel/src/js/getters.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L26: exposure() is documented 'in stops' but is a linear multiplier**
  - description: The getter's doc comment, which goes into the .d.ts, says 'The exposure (`setExposure`), in stops'. `setExposure` and `SceneRenderer::set_exposure` (render.rs:604) say 'Scales radiance before tone mapping. Default 1', and the value is passed through unchanged as a scale.
  - scenario: A UI reads `scene.exposure()` to show an EV slider, or sets `setExposure(0)` for 'no compensation' on the strength of the getter's doc, and gets a black frame.
  - fix: Fix the getter's doc: a linear scale, default 1.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/lights.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L421: setCompactEnvironment during a first bake leaves the new environment at the old intensity (0.2)**
  - description: set_compact_environment computes `keep = env_intensity_after_bake.unwrap_or_else(|| renderer.environment_intensity())` and stores it as the intensity to apply after the re-bake. While a bake started by setEnvironmentHdr is still pending and the app has not called setEnvironmentIntensity, env_intensity_after_bake is None (start_environment clears it) and the renderer still holds the intensity of the environment being replaced (DEFAULT_ENV_INTENSITY 0.2 for the default white ambient). That value is then forced onto the new environment in apply_environment, overriding the documented reset to 1 (`setEnvironmentHdr resets it to 1`). The same pattern is in js/restore.rs:105 (context restored while the first bake is pending).
  - scenario: scene.setEnvironmentHdr(bytes, 256); scene.setCompactEnvironment(false); (same tick, default compact=true so the flag differs) -> bake restarts with env_intensity_after_bake = Some(0.2) -> when it lands renderer.set_environment sets 1 and apply_environment immediately overwrites it with 0.2: the HDR environment lights the scene 5x too dark until the app calls setEnvironmentIntensity itself.
  - fix: Only carry an intensity over when there is one to carry: if a bake is pending, keep `self.env_intensity_after_bake` as is (None stays None => 1 after the swap); only when no bake is pending and an environment is set use renderer.environment_intensity(). Apply the same rule in restore().
- [ ] **[low/api] L49: setCamera validates znear/zfar but not yfov / ymag: degenerate values give an infinite or flipped projection**
  - description: set_camera throws for `!(near > 0 && far > near)` but passes any finite `yfov` and `ymag` on. Camera::projection_matrix computes `1 / tan(fov_y / 2)` and orthographic_rh_gl divides by the extents, so yfov = 0 or ymag = 0 produce infinities, yfov >= pi or negative produce a flipped or garbage projection, and those matrices reach the frame UBO, culling and the TAA history - exactly what options.rs says finite checks are there to prevent.
  - scenario: scene.setCamera(cam, {type:'perspective', yfov: 0}) or yfov: 60 (degrees by mistake: tan(30 rad) < 0 -> mirrored image), or {type:'orthographic', ymag: 0}: no error is thrown; the frame is black / NaN (and with TAA the history stays NaN until reset).
  - fix: Throw like the znear/zfar check: require 0 < yfov < pi for perspective and ymag > 0 for orthographic.
- [ ] **[low/api] L281: Option getters read back unclamped values and some options are not range-checked**
  - description: getters.rs promises values 'read back as set (clamped where the setter clamps)'. `groundShadow().size` returns the raw number although ground.rs:261 uses `size.clamp(64, 2048).next_power_of_two()`. `light(e).shadowMapSize` returns the raw number although `LightShadow::side` clamps to a power of two in 64..=layer; the .d.ts says 'a power of two up to the scene's size'. `setCamera` checks znear and zfar but not `yfov` (0, negative or ≥ π) or `ymag` (≤ 0). `setLight` checks cone angles and cascades but not negative `intensity` or `range`, or `shadowSplitLambda` outside 0..1.
  - scenario: `setGroundShadow({ size: 5000 })` then `groundShadow().size` returns 5000 while a 2048 texture is in use. `setCamera(e, { type: 'perspective', yfov: 60 })` (degrees) is accepted and renders a garbage projection, with no error naming the option.
  - fix: Store the clamped values (as `setShadowMapSize` does) and validate yfov, ymag, intensity and range next to the existing checks.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L394: Restore work done twice or needlessly: old environment re-baked then cancelled, HDR bytes cloned, BRDF table re-integrated**
  - description: set_environment_hdr and set_compact_environment (line 418) call sync_context() first; when that triggers restore(), restore decodes and uploads the *old* env_source and starts its bake (restore.rs:104-108), which the same call cancels a line later with its own start_environment. restore() and setCompactEnvironment also clone the whole .hdr byte vector (restore.rs:104, lights.rs:417) only to decode it. SceneRenderer::rebuild goes through Self::new, which re-integrates the constant BRDF table on the CPU (render.rs:353, brdf_lut.rs:18: 1024 texels x 768 samples) inside the first frame after every restore.
  - scenario: After a restore the app's handler calls scene.setEnvironmentHdr(newBytes): a 4k .hdr is copied, RGBE-decoded, uploaded and a bake job with five textures is created, then immediately cancelled and the same done for the new bytes, all in one synchronous call, on top of tens of milliseconds for the LUT.
  - fix: Take env_source by reference (decode before borrowing self mutably or keep the decoded RgbeImage), skip the restore's re-bake when the caller is about to replace/clear the environment, and cache the BRDF table (thread_local / precomputed).
- [ ] **[low/api] L396: setEnvironmentHdr (and loadTexture / createProgram) during a loss throw and are not recorded, so the restore does not bring them**
  - description: set_environment_hdr ignores sync_context()'s false, then start_environment fails in EnvBaker::new / BakeJob::start with ContextLost and returns before `self.env_source = Some(..)` (line 398). The scene restores what it has a record of, but a call made while the context is lost leaves no record: load_texture (materials.rs:378-381) and create_program (mod.rs:594) return before pushing their entry in the same way.
  - scenario: Startup: `scene.setEnvironmentHdr(new Uint8Array(await (await fetch(hdr)).arrayBuffer()), 256)` resolves its fetch during a loss. The call throws 'WebGL context is lost' in an async init (an unhandled rejection in most apps); after the restore models and other textures come back but the scene has no environment, because the bytes were never stored.
  - fix: When the context is lost, validate the input, store the record (env_source, TextureSpec, shader sources) and return a handle/OK; let restore() build it. Otherwise document that these calls must be repeated from setOnRestored.
- [ ] **[low/async] L476: setBackground / setBackgroundColor / setTaa create GL programs without sync_context: one program leaks after a context restore**
  - description: set_background (line 476), set_fill (576) and set_taa (output.rs:243, renderer.set_motion_vectors) create GPU programs through `self.renderer` without first calling self.sync_context(), unlike set_fxaa / set_auto_exposure / set_environment_hdr. Between a context restore and the scene's next syncing call the renderer is still the old-generation one: a program created now belongs to the new context but is stored in the old renderer. The following restore() builds a fresh renderer (rebuild compiles the background program again because the old one has Some(Ok(_))) and SceneRenderer::destroy returns early for a lost generation without deleting the skybox / motion programs. Called while the context is still lost, set_background instead logs a spurious `background shader failed to build`.
  - scenario: App re-applies its settings in a canvas `webglcontextrestored` listener: scene.setBackground(0.3) (first use of the background) or scene.setTaa(true) before any renderFrame -> program compiled in the new context, then dropped unreleased by the restore at the next frame; the background shader is compiled twice.
  - fix: Call self.sync_context() at the start of set_background, set_fill and set_taa (skip the program build when it returns false).

## `packages/luxel/src/js/materials.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/async] L366: loadTexture promise rejects with "can't load" when the context is lost mid-load, although the scene reloads that texture under a new handle**
  - description: `load_bitmap` (luxel-gl texture.rs:1574/1584) reports (0, 0) when the texture is no longer valid because the context was lost. The loadTexture closure treats w == 0 as a load failure and rejects (lines 368-374). But the entry is in `scene.textures`, so `restore_textures` loads the image again into a new texture and records old -> new in `texture_aliases`. The app gets a rejection and no handle, while the reloaded texture stays alive and unreachable (it can only be freed by scene.destroy()). The docs promise the opposite: "After a context loss the scene loads it again (a new handle, in the same materials)".
  - scenario: const p = scene.loadTexture(bigUrl); context is lost while the image is fetching/decoding; context restored, next renderFrame runs restore_textures (new texture B for the entry). The original fetch finishes: texture A invalid -> on_load(0,0) -> p rejects with `loadTexture: can't load <url>`. The app treats it as a failed load; texture B is loaded on the GPU and orphaned.
  - fix: Distinguish 'lost/destroyed' from 'failed to decode': when w == 0 and the texture is invalid, check whether the scene still has an entry for it (alias old -> new) and, if so, resolve the promise when the restored load lands (chain on the restore promise) or resolve with the old handle (current_texture maps it); only reject when the image itself failed or the texture was destroyed by the app.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L366: loadTexture in flight at a loss rejects with "can't load" while the scene keeps and reloads the texture; failed loads are also kept and retried at every loss**
  - description: load_texture pushes the LoadedTexture into self.textures at once (line 381). When the context is lost before the image arrives, the core reports (0,0) for the dead texture (texture.rs:1574 / 1667), and the closure rejects the promise with `loadTexture: can't load <url>`. The entry stays, so restore_textures loads it again under a new handle that no caller ever received (the promise rejected), and again at each later loss, until scene.destroy(). The same closure never removes the entry on a genuine failure either: after a 404 the URL is re-created and re-fetched by every restore, the reload closure (restore.rs:209) only logs and leaves the new storage-less texture alive.
  - scenario: `const t = await scene.loadTexture(url)` for several textures at startup; the context is lost while they download. Every promise rejects with a misleading error for a URL that is fine; after the restore the scene downloads and uploads all of them into textures nothing references.
  - fix: Tie the entry to the outcome: on (0,0) with an invalid texture caused by a loss either keep the promise pending and resolve it from the restore's reload with the new handle, or reject with ContextLostError and remove the entry; on a real failure remove the entry (and in the restore closure destroy the texture and drop the entry when the reload fails).
- [ ] **[medium/async] L366: A loadTexture in flight at a context loss rejects with 'can't load', yet the scene reloads the texture under a handle nobody receives**
  - description: The `LoadedTexture` is pushed into `self.textures` when the call returns (line 381), before the image arrives. When the context is lost during the load, luxel-gl's `load_bitmap` / `drive_image_load` find the texture invalid and call `on_load(0, 0)` (texture.rs:1574, 1667). The closure here treats every `w == 0` as a failed image and rejects with `loadTexture: can't load <url>` (lines 368-373). `restore_textures` (restore.rs:199-224) still iterates `self.textures`, loads the URL again and keeps the new texture; the app only ever saw a rejection for a URL that is fine. The new texture is reachable only through `texture_aliases[old]`, and the app never received `old`. It lives until `Scene.destroy`. (Listed unverified in review-night-raw.md; confirmed by reading both sides.)
  - scenario: `const t = await scene.loadTexture('/logo.png')` while the context is lost mid-fetch (GPU reset, tab backgrounded): the promise rejects 'can't load /logo.png'. After the restore the scene fetches /logo.png again and holds a texture no material uses and no handle names. An app that retries gets a second copy.
  - fix: In the callback, tell 'torn down' from 'failed': when the texture is invalid because of a loss (generation changed), neither reject nor destroy. Keep the resolve function with the `LoadedTexture` and resolve it with the reloaded handle from `restore_textures`' callback; or remove the entry from `self.textures` and reject with a `ContextLostError`.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L366: loadTexture in flight at a context loss rejects with 'can't load', while the scene reloads the texture under a handle nobody receives**
  - description: `load_texture` pushes its `LoadedTexture` at once (line 381). If the context is lost before the image arrives, luxel-gl reports `(0, 0)` for the dead texture (texture.rs:1573-1576 in `load_bitmap`, and the `!ctx.texture_is_valid(tex)` branch of `drive_image_load`), and the closure here treats `w == 0` as a load failure: `reject('loadTexture: can't load <url>')`. Meanwhile `restore_textures` (restore.rs:197-235) has already started the same URL again into a new texture and recorded old -> new in `texture_aliases`; the app never got the old handle, so it can neither use nor `destroyTexture` the reloaded one. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: `const t = await scene.loadTexture(bigUrl)`; the context is lost during the download and restored. The promise rejects with a 'can't load' error for a URL that is fine; the scene then downloads and uploads the image anyway, keeps it on the GPU until `scene.destroy()`, and loads it again at every later loss.
  - fix: In the closure, when `w == 0` and the texture is no longer valid, don't reject: keep the promise pending and resolve it from the restore's load (or resolve with the reloaded handle); alternatively remove the entry from `scene.textures` when rejecting.
- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L381: A loadTexture that fails stays in scene.textures forever and is retried after every context loss**
  - description: `load_texture` pushes `LoadedTexture { id, spec }` unconditionally (line 381). When the image fails, the on_load closure (lines 368-374) destroys the GL texture and rejects the promise, but it has no access to the scene and the entry is never removed. The app never received the handle (the promise rejected), so it cannot call `destroyTexture` either. `restore_textures` (restore.rs:199) iterates every entry, so each stale entry is created and fetched again on every context restore (logging `restore: can't load ...`), and if the retry succeeds (transient network error) a texture nobody can reference lives until `scene.destroy()`.
  - scenario: for (const u of urlsWith404s) scene.loadTexture(u).catch(() => {}) -> each failure leaves an entry (URL string + spec) in scene.textures; after a context loss the scene issues one fetch and one console error per failed URL, and adds each to restore_tasks so whenReady waits on them.
  - fix: Remove the entry when the load fails: e.g. keep `textures` behind an Rc<RefCell<..>> (or a shared 'failed ids' list the scene drains in sync_context/renderFrame) and drop the entry in the failure branch; in restore_textures skip/drop entries whose texture is no longer valid before the loss.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L459: setMaterialTexture stores texCoord as a shader UV slot, not as a TEXCOORD set: wrong UVs on materials whose slots were remapped at load**
  - description: After load, `Material::maps[i].1` is a slot index into `uv_sets` (luxel-gltf material.rs:1056 `assign_uv_slots` rewrites the sets to slots; e.g. a material reading TEXCOORD_1 and TEXCOORD_2 gets uv_sets = [2, 1], one reading only TEXCOORD_2 gets [2, 0]). `set_material_texture` passes the user's `texCoord` (documented in options.rs as "Which TEXCOORD set the map reads: 0 (default) or 1") straight into `set_map(slot, index, uv)` and never looks at or updates `uv_sets`, and PbrKey only checks `uv == 1` to pick v_uv1. So the value is interpreted as a slot.
  - scenario: Model material uses baseColorTexture on TEXCOORD_2 and occlusionTexture on TEXCOORD_1 (uv_sets = [2, 1]). scene.setMaterialTexture(m, 'emissiveTexture', tex) with the default texCoord 0 -> the emissive map samples slot 0 = TEXCOORD_2, not TEXCOORD_0. With uv_sets = [0, 2], {texCoord: 1} samples TEXCOORD_2 instead of TEXCOORD_1.
  - fix: Translate the requested TEXCOORD set to a slot through the material's uv_sets (position of the set in uv_sets); if the set is in neither slot, either throw a clear error or assign it to a free slot and let wanted_vertex_array pick/build the matching vertex array.
- [ ] **[low/api] L223: setMaterialProperty rejects Infinity/NaN with a misleading message, making glTF's attenuationDistance = Infinity unsettable**
  - description: `options::numbers` returns None for non-finite numbers, and line 224 turns that into `"<name>" takes a number or numbers` — wrong for an input that is a number. In particular `Material::set_property` documents and implements "An infinite attenuationDistance means no absorption (stored as 0)" (luxel-gltf material.rs:441), which is glTF's default value, but that branch is unreachable from JS because the value is rejected first.
  - scenario: scene.setMaterialProperty(m, 'attenuationDistance', Infinity) (the KHR_materials_volume default, e.g. copied from a glTF JSON) -> throws `setMaterialProperty: "attenuationDistance" takes a number or numbers`.
  - fix: Report non-finite values distinctly ("must be finite"), and let +Infinity through for attenuationDistance (map it to 0 before the finite check) or document that 0 is the only way to say 'no absorption'.
- [ ] **[low/api] L223: setMaterialProperty rejects Infinity for attenuationDistance, which glTF and the Rust setter treat as 'no absorption'**
  - description: `options::numbers` (js/options.rs:262) drops any non-finite value, so the JS setter throws '"attenuationDistance" takes a number or numbers'. `Material::set_property` handles exactly that case ('An infinite attenuationDistance means no absorption (stored as 0)', luxel-gltf/src/material.rs:427), and +Infinity is the glTF default for the property. The Rust branch is unreachable from JS.
  - scenario: Copying glTF JSON values into a material: `scene.setMaterialProperty(m, 'attenuationDistance', Infinity)` throws with a message that suggests a type error, while the getter reports 0 for the same state.
  - fix: Let Infinity through for this property (map it to 0 before the finite filter), or state in the error and docs that 0 means none.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L381: A failed loadTexture stays in the scene's texture list and is fetched again after every context loss**
  - description: On failure the callback destroys the GL texture and rejects (lines 369-373), but it cannot reach the scene, so the `LoadedTexture { id, spec }` pushed at line 381 stays in `self.textures`. The app cannot remove it: `destroyTexture` needs the handle and the promise rejected. After each context loss `restore_textures` starts the load again for that entry (restore.rs:216), logs 'restore: can't load <url>', and its failure callback (restore.rs:207-213) does not destroy the newly created texture object, which stays in the pool until `Scene.destroy`. `whenReady` after a restore also waits for these doomed loads.
  - scenario: `scene.loadTexture('/missing.png')` rejects (404); the app falls back to another image. On each later context loss the scene requests /missing.png again, logs an error, and keeps one more empty texture handle alive; N failed loads give N entries that never shrink.
  - fix: Keep failures out of the list: share a small `Rc<Cell<..>>` state between the callback and the `LoadedTexture` and drop failed entries at the next scene call, or push the entry only once the load has succeeded and keep in-flight ones in a separate pending list. In the restore callback, destroy the texture on failure.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L381: A loadTexture that failed stays in scene.textures and is fetched and re-created after every context loss**
  - description: On failure the closure destroys the texture and rejects (lines 369-372), but it cannot reach the scene, so the `LoadedTexture { id, spec }` pushed at line 381 stays. `restore_textures` iterates every entry: after each loss it creates a new GL texture and fetches the URL again, pushes a promise into `restore_tasks` (so `whenReady` waits for it), and on the repeated failure the restore closure (restore.rs:207-213) only logs, leaving the new storage-less texture alive with no handle anywhere but the scene's list. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: `scene.loadTexture('missing.png').catch(() => {})`. Later the context is lost and restored: one request and one '[luxel] restore: can't load missing.png' per failed URL, an empty texture object kept until `scene.destroy()`; if the URL has started working, a full texture nobody can reach.
  - fix: Record the outcome where the scene can see it (a shared `Rc<Cell<bool>>` per entry, or prune entries whose `id` is no longer valid at the start of `restore_textures` and in `load_texture`), and destroy the texture in the restore closure when its load fails.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L381: A loadTexture that fails stays in the scene's texture list and is fetched again after every context loss**
  - description: `load_texture` pushes `LoadedTexture { id, spec }` synchronously. When the image fails, the callback destroys the texture and rejects, but the record is never removed. The app never received the id, so it cannot call `destroyTexture` either. `restore_textures` (js/restore.rs:199) then starts a fresh load for it after each loss, logs 'restore: can't load …', and makes `whenReady` wait for it.
  - scenario: An app retries a missing or blocked texture URL several times. Each failure leaves a record. After a context loss the scene creates a texture and issues a request per failed record, and delays `whenReady` on them.
  - fix: Remove the record in the failure branch (share the `textures` list through an Rc/RefCell or flag the entry) so failed loads are forgotten.
- [ ] **[low/api] L506: Rust `set_material_values` ("setMaterialProperty from Rust") does not invalidate shadows like the JS path does**
  - description: The JS wrapper calls `self.renderer.invalidate_shadows()` (line 222) because "Frozen shadows don't see this change on their own"; the public Rust entry point documented as its equivalent only calls `self.invalidate()`. Shadow maps that are kept (frozen, or unmoved casters) are not redrawn for changes that affect them.
  - scenario: Rust app with setShadowsFrozen(true) calls scene.set_material_values(m, "alphaCutoff", &[0.9]) on an alpha-masked material -> the shadow map keeps the old cut-out until something else invalidates it.
  - fix: Move the invalidate_shadows() call into set_material_values (the JS wrapper then needs none).
- [ ] **[low/perf] L520: Every material property write rescans all meshes and re-resolves variant, vertex array and shadow caster for each user; OpenPBR names do it up to 3 times**
  - description: `set_material_values` unconditionally calls `update_variants_of`, which scans the whole MeshRenderer storage and for each user runs `update_variant`: `wanted_vertex_array` (parent walk in `model_root_of`), `pbr.is_pbr` (linear scan over all compiled variants), `PbrKey::new`, then `refresh_shadow_caster`, which clones the whole `Material` (line 579, includes the name String) and re-inserts `ShadowCaster` (marks it changed). Most properties (roughness, colours, ior ...) can never change the key. `set_material_property` calls `set_material_values` once per write, so `transmission_depth` does the full pass three times, and each call also invalidates all shadow maps (line 222) even for properties shadows cannot see.
  - scenario: A UI slider dragging `roughnessFactor` (or `transmission_depth`) on a material shared by a few thousand meshes: each input event costs O(all meshes) + per-user parent walks, a Material clone and a ShadowCaster re-insert, and forces every shadow map to be redrawn next frame.
  - fix: Compute PbrKey (and transmission > 0) before and after the write and only call update_variants_of when it changed; batch the OpenPBR multi-writes into one update; in refresh_shadow_caster borrow the record instead of cloning; invalidate shadows only when the shadow key / alpha inputs changed.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/mod.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L361: setLocalMatrix with a zero scale on any axis (or non-finite input) stores a NaN rotation that never recovers**
  - description: `set_local_matrix` passes the matrix straight to `Transform::from_matrix` = glam `to_scale_rotation_translation`, which divides the axes by the scale (`scale.recip()`): a zero-length axis gives 0*inf = NaN in the quaternion. Unlike setTranslation/setScale/setRotationEuler there is no `options::finite` check either. The NaN rotation stays in the Transform; `edit_transform` copies it on every later edit, and `set_if_neq` sees NaN != NaN as a change each time.
  - scenario: `scene.setLocalMatrix(e, scaleMatrix(0,0,0))` to hide a part (or `(1,0,1)` to flatten it), later `scene.setScale(e,1,1,1)`: rotation is still NaN, the world matrix of `e` and of everything under it is NaN, bounds-culled meshes are dropped (`Frustum::intersects` false) and the rest draw nothing; `scene.rotation(e)` returns NaNs. The same `from_matrix` is used by `spawn_model` for glTF node matrices (model.rs:86), so a node authored with scale 0 is hit too if the loader hands it over as a matrix.
  - fix: Reject non-finite input, and decompose robustly: on a (near) zero axis length keep scale 0 for it and build the rotation from the remaining axes / identity instead of dividing.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L368: lookAt, setLocalMatrix and setBounds skip the finite check every other transform setter applies**
  - description: `setTranslation`, `setRotation`, `setScale`, `setCameraAspect`, `setExposure` and the rest go through `options::finite`, whose own comment says a NaN 'would reach the shaders, and TAA's history would keep it'. `lookAt` (line 368), `setLocalMatrix` (line 352) and `setBounds` (line 564) only check the array length. `lookAt` with eye == target also yields NaN: `Mat4::look_at_rh` normalizes a zero direction and `Transform::from_matrix` stores the result unchanged.
  - scenario: The README quick start runs `const [cx, cy, cz, r] = scene.modelBounds(root); scene.lookAt(camera, [cx, cy, cz + r * 2.5], [cx, cy, cz])`. If `root` is not a model root, `modelBounds` returns an empty array, all four values are undefined, and the camera transform becomes NaN: black frames, and with TAA the history stays NaN after the camera is fixed. `scene.lookAt(cam, p, p)` gives the same result.
  - fix: Run `options::finite` on the inputs of lookAt, setLocalMatrix and setBounds, and reject eye == target in lookAt with the usual console error and no-op.
- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L595: createProgram records are never dropped: destroyed shaders are recompiled after every loss, and the recompiled programs are owned by nobody**
  - description: create_program pushes (id, vert, frag) into scene_programs and documents the program as the app's to destroy (ctx.destroyProgram). No path removes an entry. restore_programs (restore.rs:141) recompiles every entry 'used or not' under a new handle the app only reaches through currentProgram(old); ctx.destroyProgram(old) is a stale no-op after a loss, and Scene::destroy does not destroy scene_programs. program_aliases / texture_aliases also grow by one entry per object per loss and are never pruned.
  - scenario: Shader editor / hot reload: `p2 = scene.createProgram(v, f); ctx.destroyProgram(p1)` 200 times: 200 source pairs stay in memory. On a context loss the scene compiles and links 200 programs (a stall without parallel compile), 199 of them unused; they live until the context dies and the same happens at each further loss. Also: any app that destroys the scene after one loss leaks every recompiled scene program.
  - fix: Add scene.destroyProgram (removing entry and aliases) or prune entries whose id is no longer valid while the generation is current (e.g. on createProgram / per frame); destroy the scene's recompiled programs in Scene::destroy or document currentProgram as the handle to destroy.
- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L595: createProgram sources are kept forever and destroyed programs are recompiled after a context loss**
  - description: `Scene.createProgram` pushes `{id, vert, frag}` into `scene_programs` and says the program 'is yours to destroy (ctx.destroyProgram)'. Nothing ever removes an entry: there is no scene-side destroy, and `ctx.destroyProgram` does not tell the scene. `restore_programs` (js/restore.rs:141) recompiles every entry, 'used or not', after a context loss. The recompiled programs get new handles that nobody owns: the app destroyed its handle, and `Scene::destroy` does not free `scene_programs`.
  - scenario: A shader editor or hot reload calls `p = scene.createProgram(vs, fs)` on each edit and `ctx.destroyProgram(old)`. After 200 edits the scene holds 200 source pairs. On the next context loss it compiles all 200 again, logs errors for the broken ones, and leaves about 200 live GL programs that no handle the app holds refers to.
  - fix: Add `scene.destroyProgram(p)` that removes the entry and its alias, or in `restore_programs` skip and drop entries whose program the app destroyed (track validity before the loss, or prune entries on `!ctx.program_is_valid` at each frame's sync).
- [ ] **[medium/async] L618: addModel / instantiate while the context is lost spawn a model without primitives, for good**
  - description: add_model ignores sync_context()'s false and calls spawn_model with `pbr.program_for(...).ok()`. While the context is lost create_program fails (check_alive), the closure returns None and spawn_model (model.rs:127) skips the primitive entirely: no entity, no MeshRenderer, no Primitive. The later restore only touches existing MeshRenderers (restore_programs) and apply_reloads only walks existing Primitive entities, so the primitives never come back although the model is reloaded. instantiate (mod.rs:674-687) has the same closure.
  - scenario: A configurator preloads Model objects and adds them on click. The user clicks during a loss (or a load that resolved just before the loss is added a tick later): `scene.addModel(model)` returns a root with nodes but zero meshes; one console.error_once 'a PBR shader variant failed to build: WebGL context is lost'. After the restore the model is fetched again and adopted, but nothing is ever drawn for it.
  - fix: When the context is lost, spawn the primitives anyway (program NULL/placeholder plus Reloading, resolved in restore_programs/apply_reloads), or defer the spawn until sync_context() succeeds; at minimum reject the call with a ContextLostError.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L618: addModel / instantiate while the context is lost take the Model over but spawn none of its primitives, for good**
  - description: `add_model` ignores `sync_context()` returning false. While lost, `pbr.program_for` fails in `Context::new_program` (`check_alive`), the closure returns None, and `spawn_model` skips the primitive (luxel/src/model.rs:127 `let Some(program) = program_for(item) else { continue }`). The scene still keeps the Model (`keep_model`) and `reload_model` starts a reload; after the restore `apply_reloads` adopts the fresh GPU objects into the model but walks only existing `Primitive` entities (restore.rs:390-412), of which there are none. `instantiate` (line 674) has the same closure. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md).
  - scenario: `const model = await loadModel(ctx, url)` resolves just before a context loss; the continuation runs `scene.addModel(model)` while `ctx.isLost()`. A root is returned, `modelBounds`/`materials` look healthy, the model is fetched again after the restore and its buffers, vertex arrays and textures are created and owned by the scene, but no mesh entity exists so nothing is ever drawn; only despawning the root frees it.
  - fix: Spawn the primitives regardless of the program (a NULL program plus `Reloading`), and resolve the variant in `apply_reloads` / `restore_programs`; or refuse (`Result`) when the context is lost and leave the Model with the caller.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L674: instantiate()/addModel() while the context is lost spawn a model with no primitives, permanently**
  - description: Both ignore `sync_context()` returning false. While lost, `pbr.program_for` -> `Context::new_program` fails at `check_alive()` (program.rs:698), the closure returns None, and `spawn_model` skips the primitive entirely (model.rs: 'A primitive `program_for` returns None for is left out'). No primitive entity exists, so the later restore/`apply_reloads` (which walk existing `Primitive` entities) can never bring the meshes back. The call still returns a valid root and only logs 'a PBR shader variant failed to build' once.
  - scenario: Context is lost (can last seconds). A UI action calls `scene.instantiate(root)` (or a `loadModel` promise that resolved just before the loss continues into `scene.addModel(model)`). Context is restored: every other model reloads, but this root has only node entities and never draws; `modelBounds`, `materials` etc. look healthy.
  - fix: When `sync_context()` is false (or a variant fails for a lost context), still spawn the primitives with a placeholder/NULL program marked `Reloading`, and resolve their variant in `restore_programs`/`apply_reloads`; or return an error from `instantiate` and document addModel.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L951: Scene.render() into a user pass corrupts TAA motion-vector history of renderFrame**
  - description: `render()` goes through `render_passes` -> `SceneRenderer::render_split`, which unconditionally runs `motion.begin(self.last_view_proj)` (render.rs:773, swaps prev/next mesh states) and `write_frame` (overwrites `last_view_proj`, render.rs:1404). Motion vectors stay enabled between frames (`set_motion_vectors(taa)` is only switched in renderFrame/setTaa), so an offscreen `render()` call counts as a full 'previous frame' for the motion tracker.
  - scenario: TAA on, an animated/skinned mesh. Each tick: `advanceAnimations(dt); scene.render(mirrorCam, myPass); ctx.render(myPass); scene.renderFrame(camera)`. In renderFrame the tracker's `prev` states are the ones recorded by `render()` in the same tick (identical to now), so no mesh 'moved' -> `push_motion` returns 0 -> `velocity=false`; and `prev_view_proj` handed to the motion shader is mirrorCam's. Moving meshes are resolved with camera reprojection only -> ghost trails; with a real move between the two calls the vectors are computed against the wrong camera.
  - fix: In `Scene::render` suspend motion tracking (e.g. a renderer flag that skips `motion.begin`/`track` and does not overwrite `last_view_proj`), or save/restore the tracker's state around the call.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L956: Scene.render() consumes the render-on-demand state, so the canvas frame is skipped (or never idles)**
  - description: `render()` calls `self.observe(Some(camera))` and `self.rendered(camera)` exactly like `renderFrame`, although it draws nothing to the canvas. `rendered` decrements `settle` and stores the camera/size/content version (on_demand.rs:104-113).
  - scenario: (a) Non-TAA app with a secondary view: `scene.setTranslation(...)`; `scene.render(cam, offscreenPass)`; `if (scene.needsRender(cam)) scene.renderFrame(cam)` -> settle was 1, `render()` took it to 0, `needsRender` is false and the canvas never shows the change. (b) Secondary view with another camera: `render(camB, pass)` stores `d.camera = camB`; `needsRender(camA)` sees `camera != d.camera` every tick -> always true, the app never idles (and with TAA settle is reset to 30 every tick).
  - fix: Do not call `observe`/`rendered` from `render()` (keep on-demand bookkeeping to canvas frames), or track it separately.
- [ ] **[medium/perf] L1141: Depth peeling runs the full PBR fragment shader once per layer into depth-only targets**
  - description: peel_glass renders the `glass` pass `layers` times into the layer depth framebuffers (depth attachment only, drawBuffers [NONE]) with the meshes' ordinary programs: render.rs:834-839 puts transmissive meshes into `glass` with mesh.program, and pbr.frag calls peel_discard() (line 331) and then shades the whole material (IBL, lights with PCF shadows, the scene-copy refraction with its textureLod) for every fragment that survives. Nothing of that color is kept. The same draws are then shaded again in the back-to-front loop. The DEPTH_ONLY variant that exists for shadow maps is not used here, and the doc of setTransmissionLayers calls it 'a geometry pass' per layer.
  - scenario: setTransmissionLayers(4) with a glass model covering half of a phone's screen: four extra full-cost transmissive PBR passes over those pixels each frame before the four shading passes, i.e. roughly twice the glass shading cost, all of the first half discarded.
  - fix: Draw the peel passes with a depth-only variant that keeps peel_discard (and the alpha-mask discard): a DEPTH_ONLY define that calls peel_discard() before returning, selected per mesh for the peel loop (as ShadowCaster.program is for shadow maps).
- [ ] **[low/api] L199: Constructing a Scene changes how createProgram reports errors on the shared context**
  - description: `Scene::new` enables `KHR_parallel_shader_compile` on the app's Context. From then on `ctx.createProgram`, and `scene.createProgram` (line 592), which is documented only as 'Compiles a scene shader', no longer throw `ShaderError` on a bad shader where the extension exists. The error surfaces later through `programError`, `programsReady` or the console. `scene.whenReady()` uses the non-rejecting Rust future, so it resolves even when a program failed. Without the extension, creation still throws.
  - scenario: `try { p = scene.createProgram(vs, fs) } catch (e) { editor.show(e.message) }` shows errors where the extension is missing and never where it exists: the mesh just does not draw and `await scene.whenReady()` resolves.
  - fix: Say so in `Scene.createProgram`'s and the Scene constructor's docs and point to `ctx.programsReady([p])` or `ctx.programError(p)`; or have `scene.createProgram` return or await an error consistently.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L291: despawn frees a model's GPU data while entities reparented out of its subtree still draw with it**
  - description: The model is destroyed when its last root leaves `model_roots`, but only entities found by `descendants(e)` are despawned. A primitive/node of the model that the app moved elsewhere with `setParent` survives with a `MeshRenderer` pointing at the destroyed vertex array, material group (also removed from `self.materials`) and textures.
  - scenario: `const wheel = scene.findByName(car, 'Wheel'); scene.setParent(wheel, rig); scene.despawn(car);` -> `wheel`'s meshes stay alive, never draw again and report stale vao / param group handles from the render path.
  - fix: On freeing a model, also strip (or despawn) every entity whose `MeshRenderer.vao/material` belongs to it, e.g. by tagging primitives with their model key instead of deriving ownership from the hierarchy.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L328: setRotation(0,0,0,0) normalizes a zero quaternion to NaN**
  - description: The `finite` check passes for zeros, then `Quat::from_xyzw(0,0,0,0).normalize()` is NaN (glam without glam_assert). The doc says 'normalized on the way in' but a zero-length input is not handled.
  - scenario: `scene.setRotation(e, 0, 0, 0, 0)` (an uninitialised Float32Array(4)) -> the entity and its subtree get NaN world matrices and vanish; each further setter call re-marks the Transform changed because NaN != NaN in `set_if_neq`.
  - fix: Use `try_normalize` and ignore (error_once) or fall back to identity when the length is ~0.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L385: lookAt with eye == target (or non-finite input) writes a NaN transform**
  - description: `normalize_or_zero` only protects the up-axis choice; `Mat4::look_at_rh(eye, target, up)` normalizes `target - eye` unguarded, so a zero direction yields a NaN matrix, its inverse and the decomposed Transform are NaN. No `finite` check on `eye`/`target`.
  - scenario: `scene.lookAt(camera, p, p)` (an orbit controller at distance 0, or a light placed at its target): the camera's world matrix becomes NaN, `renderFrame` draws only the clear color until another full lookAt/setLocalMatrix replaces the transform (setTranslation alone keeps the NaN rotation).
  - fix: Check `finite` and `(target - eye).length_squared() > eps`; otherwise report once and ignore, as the other setters do.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L556: removeMesh does not rebake frozen shadows (setMesh, despawn and setVisible do)**
  - description: `remove_mesh` only invalidates and removes the `MeshRenderer`. With `setShadowsFrozen(true)` the lights hold the baked maps (`frozen && baked`, lighting/mod.rs:312) until `rebake()`; the sibling entry points call `self.renderer.rebake_shadows()` for exactly this reason.
