  - scenario: `setShadowsFrozen(true)`; renderFrame; `scene.removeMesh(e)` on a shadow-casting model primitive; renderFrame -> the mesh is gone but its shadow stays on the ground until something else triggers a rebake. (`ShadowCaster` and `Primitive` are also left on the entity.)
  - fix: Call `self.renderer.rebake_shadows()` in `remove_mesh` (and remove the `ShadowCaster`).
- [ ] **[low/api] L574: setBounds accepts NaN / inverted boxes: the mesh is then silently culled forever**
  - description: Only the array length is validated (`vec3`); values are not checked with `options::finite` as the transform setters are, and `invalidate()` runs before validation. `Frustum::intersects` compares with NaN -> false.
  - scenario: `scene.setBounds(e, [NaN,0,0], [1,1,1])` (bounds computed from an empty geometry: min=+Inf/max=-Inf or NaN) -> `e` is counted in `culled()` every frame and never drawn, with no console message.
  - fix: Reject non-finite components (and min > max) with an error_once, before touching state.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L595: createProgram records are never dropped: destroyed shaders are recompiled after every context loss and leak with the scene**
  - description: `create_program` pushes the sources into `scene_programs`; nothing removes an entry when the app calls `ctx.destroyProgram` (the documented owner). `restore_programs` (restore.rs:141) recompiles every entry 'used or not' under a new id known only through `currentProgram`, and `Scene::destroy` frees none of them.
  - scenario: A shader editor / hot reload calls `scene.createProgram` 200 times, destroying each old program. Sources of all 200 stay in memory; after a context loss the scene compiles 200 programs again (a stall on restore) and they stay alive on the context: the app already destroyed its handles and has no reason to ask `currentProgram` for them. Also: createProgram -> loss+restore -> `scene.destroy()`: the recompiled programs' ids were only in the scene, so they leak on a context that outlives the scene.
  - fix: Prune entries whose program is no longer valid before recompiling (`ctx.program_is_valid`), offer `scene.destroyProgram`, and destroy in `Scene::destroy` the programs the scene itself recompiled (those present as alias targets).
- [ ] **[low/api] L713: addModelWithProgram does not validate the program (setMesh does)**
  - description: `set_mesh` throws when `program` is null; `add_model_with_program` takes the id as is, consumes the `Model` and spawns every primitive with it. It also skips `refresh_shadow_caster`, so such models never cast shadows, which the doc does not say.
  - scenario: `scene.addModelWithProgram(model, undefined)` (or a handle already destroyed): returns a root, the JS `model` object is moved into the scene, and nothing ever draws; the only signal is a stale-program console error from the render path.
  - fix: Return `Result<Entity, Error>` and reject a null / invalid program before taking the model over; mention the missing shadow casting in the doc.
- [ ] **[low/api] L970: scene.setReverseZ and ctx.setReverseZ return opposite values for the same call**
  - description: `Context::set_reverse_z` (luxel-gl/src/depth_range.rs:30) returns 'whether what was asked holds': `setReverseZ(false)` returns true, with or without `EXT_clip_control`. `Scene::set_reverse_z` forwards to it but returns `ctx.reverse_z()`, 'whether reverse-Z is on', so `scene.setReverseZ(false)` always returns false. docs/review-style.md records the context's convention as intended; the scene wrapper with the same name contradicts it.
  - scenario: `if (!scene.setReverseZ(wanted)) warn('not supported')` fires for `wanted === false` on every device, while the identical check against `ctx.setReverseZ` does not.
  - fix: Return the context call's result from the scene method, or rename and document so that both levels share one convention.
- [ ] **[low/api] L1162: gpuTimings 'glass' is one layer's time, not the frame's, and eats the profiler ring; docs list only three labels**
  - description: `peel_glass` calls `p.time(glass, "glass")` once per layer per frame under one label. `GpuProfiler` folds every sample into the label's average separately (its doc: 'give each its own') and has `RING_MAX = 8` queries per label. The `gpuTimings` doc names only `opaque`, `late`, `present`, while `glass`, `motion` and `fxaa` are also emitted.
  - scenario: `setTransmissionLayers(4)` with profiling on: `gpuTimings().glass` reads ~1/4 of the real glass cost (the sum of opaque+late+glass+present no longer matches the frame), and with results arriving 2+ frames late the 8-slot ring is exhausted so some layers are not timed at all.
  - fix: Use one label per layer (`glass0..3`) and sum them in `gpu_timings`, or time the whole peel with a single query; document the extra labels.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L1236: A Scene dropped without destroy() (free() or weak-ref GC) leaks everything and leaves background work 'alive'**
  - description: All cleanup lives in `destroy(self)`; there is no `Drop`. wasm-bindgen's generated `free()` and the `--weak-refs` finalizer (enabled in the builds, review-phase2 item 2) just drop the struct: GL objects of the renderer, HDR/direct targets, models, PBR programs and loadTexture textures stay on the context, and `alive` stays true.
  - scenario: An SPA route drops its `scene` reference (or calls `scene.free()`, which the module doc's wording 'nothing to .free() but the scene itself (destroy)' invites). GPU memory is never returned while the Context lives. If a model reload is in flight, its task sees `alive == true`, pushes the reloaded `Model` into the orphaned `reloads` inbox and that model's GPU objects leak too; a queued `onRestored` callback still fires for the dead scene.
  - fix: Implement `Drop for Scene` that at least sets `alive` to false and runs the same teardown (move the body of `destroy` into a `&mut self` helper), or warn loudly when dropped undestroyed.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L1236: A Scene dropped without destroy() (free() or the weak-ref finalizer) frees nothing and leaves its background work 'alive'**
  - description: All cleanup lives in `destroy(self)`; `Scene` has no `Drop`. wasm-bindgen's generated `scene.free()` and the `--weak-refs` finalizer the builds use just drop the struct: the renderer's objects, HDR / direct / occlusion targets, models, PBR programs, `loadTexture` textures and cloned materials stay on the context, the shared compressor is not released, and `alive` stays true, so a model reload in flight pushes its fresh Model into the orphaned `reloads` inbox (restore.rs:318-326) where it is dropped undestroyed, and a queued `onRestored` still fires. luxel-gl's own helpers (`TexturePacker`, `TextureCompressor`) do free on drop, so the facade is inconsistent with the core. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: An SPA route calls `scene.free()` (the module doc's 'nothing to .free() but the scene itself (destroy)' invites it) or just drops the reference: GPU memory is never returned while the Context lives, which with the pinned shared compressor is the life of the page.
  - fix: Move the body of `destroy` into a `&mut self` teardown run from `Drop for Scene` (guarded by a 'destroyed' flag), or at least set `alive` to false and release the shared compressor in `Drop`.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/on_demand.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L106: With TAA, content that lands inside a frame gets no settle frames: needsRender goes false on an unconverged image**
  - description: rendered() ends every frame with `d.content = ctx.content_version(); d.observed = world.advance_tick();` so that what the frame wrote itself is not a change, and only decrements `settle`. But several things first become visible inside a frame without any earlier observe() seeing them: apply_environment (swaps the baked environment and calls ctx.note_content_changed(), which this same frame then swallows), apply_reloads, apply_pending_variants, and meshes / shadow casters / the background whose program finished linking (they were only tracked through stats.pending). keeps_changing() turns false right after that frame and `settle` was not re-armed, so with TAA on (TAA_SETTLE = 30 frames needed to converge the jitter) rendering stops after a single jittered sample of the new content.
  - scenario: setTaa(true); addModel(bigModel) whose PBR variants take more than 30 frames (0.5 s) to compile; loop is the documented `if (scene.needsRender(cam) || !scene.presentFrame()) scene.renderFrame(cam)`. Frames run while stats.pending > 0; settle counts down to 0 meanwhile. On the frame the program links the mesh is drawn for the first time, pending becomes 0, rendered() leaves settle at 0 -> needsRender() is false from then on: the model stays on screen with one TAA sample (aliased edges, history clipped to one jitter position) until something else changes. Same for an environment bake that takes longer than 30 frames.
  - fix: In rendered(), re-arm the settle when the frame itself changed the picture: compare ctx.content_version() with the value observe() saw (and track `stats.pending`/env_pending/pending_variants/reloads going from busy to idle, or a `landed` flag set by apply_environment/apply_reloads/apply_pending_variants) and set `settle = TAA_SETTLE - 1` when TAA is on.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L109: A frame attempted while the context is lost consumes the 'generation changed' signal; needsRender never triggers the restore**
  - description: observe() treats a new ctx.generation() as a change, but the generation is bumped at the *lost* event, not at the restore. renderFrame/render call rendered() unconditionally (js/mod.rs:742-744, 956-958), also when render_frame_inner returned 0 because sync_context() said 'lost'. rendered() stores d.generation = current and decrements settle, so the only on-demand signal for the loss is spent while nothing can be drawn. Nothing changes at the restore itself (no generation bump, no content_version bump), and needs_render() (line 68) only calls observe()/keeps_changing(), never sync_context(), although restore.rs:458 and the npm README say the scene rebuilds 'at its first call after the context is back (a frame, needsRender)'.
  - scenario: Static scene, no TAA/auto exposure, loop `if (scene.needsRender(cam)) scene.renderFrame(cam)` (needsRender's doc allows it: 'or nothing need be drawn'). Context lost: next tick needsRender is true, renderFrame draws nothing, rendered() records the new generation. Context restored: needsRender returns false forever, so Scene::restore never runs, setOnRestored never fires (the README's overlay stays up) and the canvas stays blank until the user touches something. Only the `|| !presentFrame()` form recovers, because presentFrame compares generations itself.
  - fix: Do not call rendered() (or at least do not store d.generation / decrement settle) when the frame was skipped for a lost context; and have needs_render return true while `self.generation != ctx.generation()` or the context is lost-then-restored (or call sync_context() there, as the docs claim).
- [ ] **[medium/async] L110: With TAA and render-on-demand, async work applied inside a frame gets no settle frames**
  - description: `observe()` gives `settle = TAA_SETTLE` (30) only for changes it sees before a frame. Work applied inside the frame — `apply_environment` and `apply_reloads` (both call `note_content_changed`), `apply_pending_variants`, and meshes whose program finished linking and are drawn for the first time — changes the picture during `render_passes`. Then `rendered()` sets `d.content = self.ctx.content_version()` (line 110) and decrements `settle`, so the change is swallowed. At the next `needsRender`, `keeps_changing()` is false (`env_pending` is None, `stats.pending` is 0, `pending_variants` is empty) and `settle` is 0. The comment 'what the frame wrote itself … isn't a change' holds without TAA, but under TAA the frame that first shows the new content is one jittered sample blended against history of the old content.
  - scenario: TAA on, loop is `if (scene.needsRender(cam) || !scene.presentFrame()) scene.renderFrame(cam)`. `setEnvironmentHdr` on a phone: the bake shaders take longer than 30 frames to link, so the settle from the setter has run out. The bake finishes; the next frame swaps the environment in; `needsRender` is then false, and `presentFrame` keeps showing a single unconverged TAA frame (aliased, history clipped from the old lighting) until the user moves the camera. Same when a model's shaders finish compiling more than 30 frames after `addModel`, and when a reload lands after a context loss. The viewer works around it with `scene.whenReady().then(invalidate)`.
  - fix: When a frame applies such work (the environment swap, reloads, a pending variant switched, or `stats.pending` dropping against the previous frame), set `on_demand.invalidated = true` (or `settle = TAA_SETTLE` when `taa()`) so the following frames converge; do not let `rendered()` absorb a content bump made during the frame while TAA is on.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L91: Scene.render() shares the on-demand state: with TAA and a second camera needsRender never becomes false**
  - description: `render(camera, pass)` (mod.rs:956-958) calls the same observe(Some(camera)) / rendered(camera) pair as renderFrame. observe treats `camera != d.camera` as a change and re-arms `settle`, and rendered stores the camera and decrements `settle`. An app that draws an offscreen view with render(camB, pass) and the canvas with renderFrame(camA) in each drawn frame therefore sees two camera changes per frame; with TAA each re-arms settle to 30, so after the frame settle is 29 and needsRender(camA) stays true forever (without TAA settle is 1 -> 0, so it works). Even with one camera, each render() call consumes one of the 30 TAA settle frames although it accumulates no TAA sample.
  - scenario: setTaa(true); per drawn frame: scene.render(mirrorCam, mirrorPass); ctx.render(mirrorPass); scene.renderFrame(mainCam); then scene.needsRender(mainCam) -> always true on a completely still scene (continuous rendering, the on-demand saving is lost). With a single camera and one render()+renderFrame() per frame, TAA stops after 15 accumulated frames instead of 30.
  - fix: Keep the on-demand bookkeeping (camera, settle decrement, size) for renderFrame only; let render() fold changes in (observe without the camera comparison) but not store its camera nor decrement settle.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/openpbr.rs`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L102: fuzz_weight / fuzz_color do not round-trip: weight is re-derived as max(sheenColor), so any colour whose largest channel is not 1 is corrupted**
  - description: `fuzz()` recovers (weight, color) from the single stored `sheenColorFactor` as w = max channel, color = sheen / w. That is only the inverse of `weight x color` when the colour's largest channel is exactly 1. `record.fuzz_color` (the remembered colour) is only consulted when w == 0, so it does not help once any fuzz is on. Both setters (lines 226-237) build the new sheen from this wrong decomposition, and `materialProperty` returns the wrong values. The only test (tests/api.rs:1033) uses [1, 0.5, 0], whose max is 1, so it passes. The same path loses a loaded glTF sheen colour: `register_materials` remembers [1,1,1], so fuzz_weight 0 then 1 turns a loaded sheenColorFactor [0.2,0.1,0.1] into white.
  - scenario: setMaterialProperty(m,'fuzz_weight',1); setMaterialProperty(m,'fuzz_color',[0.5,0.2,0.2]) -> sheen = [0.5,0.2,0.2] (ok). materialProperty(m,'fuzz_weight') now returns 0.5 and fuzz_color returns [1,0.4,0.4]. Then setMaterialProperty(m,'fuzz_weight',0.8) (viewer slider) -> sheen = [0.8,0.32,0.32] instead of [0.4,0.16,0.16]: the fuzz doubles in brightness. Also fuzz_weight=1, fuzz_color=[0,0,0] -> sheen 0, w reads 0; a later fuzz_color=[1,0,0] writes sheen 0: the weight is lost for good.
  - fix: Keep the fuzz weight (and colour) as real state in MaterialRecord (or in Material) and always write sheenColorFactor = weight x color from it; derive from the sheen colour only once, at register_materials (weight = max, color = sheen/max), and when sheenColorFactor is written directly by its glTF name.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L102: fuzz_weight / fuzz_color do not round-trip: weight is re-derived as max(sheenColor)**
  - description: OpenPBR fuzz is stored only as `sheenColorFactor = weight × color`. `fuzz()` recovers the weight as the largest sheen component and the color as `sheen / w`. That is only right when the color's largest component is 1. For a darker color the weight silently becomes `weight × max(color)`, and the next `fuzz_color` write multiplies by that shrunken weight. `record.fuzz_color` is only consulted when the weight is 0. The existing test (tests/api.rs:1033) uses a color whose max is 1, so it passes.
  - scenario: `setMaterialProperty(m, 'fuzz_weight', 1)`, then `setMaterialProperty(m, 'fuzz_color', [0.2, 0.2, 0.2])`. `materialProperty(m, 'fuzz_weight')` now returns 0.2 and `fuzz_color` returns [1, 1, 1]. A following `setMaterialProperty(m, 'fuzz_color', [1, 0, 0])` writes sheen = [0.2, 0, 0] instead of [1, 0, 0], so the fuzz renders five times too dim.
  - fix: Keep the fuzz weight (and color) in `MaterialRecord` as set, and derive the sheen color from them; fall back to the max-component split only for materials loaded from glTF that were never set through the OpenPBR names.
- [ ] **[low/api] L212: geometry_opacity is silently a no-op on materials loaded with alphaMode OPAQUE**
  - description: `geometry_opacity` only writes baseColorFactor alpha. The PBR shader ignores alpha under ALPHAMODE_OPAQUE (pbr.frag:337) and the render state/blend comes from `alpha_mode`, which has no setter anywhere in the scene API (set_property covers numeric properties only). So for the default alpha mode the OpenPBR parameter is accepted, reads back, and changes nothing.
  - scenario: scene.setMaterialProperty(gold, 'geometry_opacity', 0.5) on a normal opaque glTF material -> no error, materialProperty returns 0.5, the mesh is drawn fully opaque.
  - fix: Either expose alpha mode (e.g. a setMaterialAlphaMode, or switch to Blend when geometry_opacity < 1 and re-run apply_material for the users), or document on the name that it needs a BLEND/MASK material and warn once when set on an opaque one.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/options.rs`

- [ ] **[low/api] L154: Handles<T> gives the brand only on index access; iteration yields plain numbers**
  - description: `Handles<T> = Uint32Array & { readonly [index: number]: T }` leaves the `Uint32Array` iterator, `forEach`, `map` and `Array.from` typed as `number`. A plain number is not assignable to a branded handle, so the most common use of `cameras()`, `lights()`, `materials()` and `children()` does not type-check without a cast. The project's own typings check needs one: `for (const cam of scene.cameras(root)) scene.renderFrame(cam as Entity)` (scripts/typings-check.ts:42).
  - scenario: `for (const m of scene.materials(root)) scene.setMaterialProperty(m, 'roughnessFactor', 1)` is a TypeScript error ('number is not assignable to ParamGroupId').
  - fix: Declare Handles<T> with typed iteration, e.g. `Omit<Uint32Array, typeof Symbol.iterator | 'forEach' | ...> & { [Symbol.iterator](): IterableIterator<T>; forEach(cb: (v: T, i: number) => void): void; readonly [i: number]: T }`.
- [ ] **[low/api] L227: u32_or's error calls every integer option "a handle"**
  - description: `u32_or` is used for non-handle integers too (texCoord, shadowCascades, shadowMapSize, contact shadow steps, ground shadow size, capture width/height/frames), but its range/fraction error is always `"<key>" must be a handle (a non-negative integer)`.
  - scenario: scene.setContactShadows({steps: 16.5}) -> `setContactShadows: "steps" must be a handle (a non-negative integer)`; scene.setMaterialTexture(m, 'baseColorTexture', t, {texCoord: -1}) -> `"texCoord" must be a handle ...`.
  - fix: Word the message neutrally ("must be a non-negative integer"), or split into handle_or / u32_or.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L264: Finite check is done on the f64, then cast to f32: large finite numbers become Infinity and pass**
  - description: `numbers()` (lines 263-276) and `f32_or()` (lines 198-202) test `x.is_finite()` on the f64 and then do `x as f32`. Any |x| > f32::MAX (about 3.4e38) is finite as f64 but casts to +/-inf, which is exactly what the comment at line 196 says must not reach the shaders and TAA history.
  - scenario: scene.setMaterialProperty(m, 'emissiveStrength', 1e39) or scene.setLight(e, {type:'point', intensity: 1e39}) -> accepted; the stored f32 is Infinity, written into the material group / light block; the frame gets inf/NaN pixels that TAA history keeps.
  - fix: Check finiteness after the cast: `.map(|x| x as f32).filter(|x| x.is_finite())` in both f32_or and numbers (scalar and element paths).

## `packages/luxel/src/js/output.rs`

- [ ] **[low/leaks] L75: Turning ambient occlusion and contact shadows off never frees the occlusion passes and depth copy**
  - description: set_ambient_occlusion(null) / set_contact_shadows(null) only clear the settings; `self.occlusion` (three programs with layouts/groups plus two targets at AO size, occlusion.rs) is only destroyed in Scene::destroy or replaced after a context loss, and the HDR output's `screen_depth` texture + framebuffer (a full-resolution depth copy made by apply_occlusion, output.rs:895) lives until the HDR target is next reallocated. setHdr(false) also leaves `self.occlusion` allocated while nothing can use it.
  - scenario: scene.setQuality('high') (AO on), render, then scene.setQuality('low') on a phone to save memory: the GTAO programs, both AO targets and the canvas-sized depth texture stay allocated for the rest of the scene's life (the depth copy until a resize).
  - fix: When both ambient_occlusion and contact_shadows become None (and in set_hdr(false)), take self.occlusion and destroy it, and add an HdrOutput method to release screen_depth; they are remade on first use already.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L243: Setters called between the restore and the scene's first sync create GL objects inside structures that are then discarded as lost**
  - description: Only some setters call sync_context(). The others run against last generation's SceneRenderer / PbrShaders / HdrOutput and create new-generation GL objects in them; the restore that follows frees those structures as 'lost' and skips GL deletes. set_taa (line 243) -> MotionVectors::new compiles a program in the old renderer, rebuild's destroy passes lost=true (render.rs:1315). set_background / set_fill (js/lights.rs:476, 576) -> skybox program in the old renderer, rebuild compiles another. setMaterialProperty / setMaterial / setMaterialVariant / setShadingModel -> update_variant (js/materials.rs:715, 589) compile new PBR and shadow variants into the old table, which restore_programs replaces and drops without destroying (restore.rs:135). set_fxaa / set_auto_exposure do sync, but restore() leaves the lost self.hdr in place, so they compile an FXAA program / three auto-exposure programs plus their targets into a lost HdrOutput whose destroy skips them (output.rs:1386-1418).
  - scenario: In ctx.setOnRestored (fires before any scene frame) the app re-applies its settings: scene.setTaa(true), scene.setBackground(0), scene.setFxaa(true), scene.setMaterialProperty(m, 'transmissionFactor', 1). Each leaves one or more linked programs (and for auto exposure a mip chain of textures and framebuffers) alive on the new context that nothing references; repeated at each loss.
  - fix: Call sync_context() at the top of every setter that can create GL objects (or in one common entry), and make restore() drop the lost hdr / direct / occlusion / motion objects itself; when replacing self.pbr destroy the programs of the old table that are still valid.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L421: prepare_output scans every mesh against every PBR variant each frame, even with contact shadows off**
  - description: Called from `render_frame_inner` (js/mod.rs:818) every frame: `pbr_only` is computed eagerly over all `MeshRenderer`s, and `PbrShaders::is_pbr` is a linear `programs.values().any(..)` (pbr.rs:403). The result is only used as `self.contact_shadows.is_some() && pbr_only`.
  - scenario: A scene with 5,000 opaque meshes and 30 shader variants does ~150k hash-map-value comparisons per frame on the hot path, with contact shadows disabled (the default).
  - fix: Short-circuit: compute `pbr_only` only when `contact_shadows.is_some()`; make `is_pbr` O(1) (a HashSet of program ids) or cache the answer and refresh it when the MeshRenderer storage changes.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L421: prepare_output scans every mesh against every PBR variant each frame, even with contact shadows off**
  - description: `pbr_only` is computed unconditionally every HDR frame: it iterates the whole MeshRenderer storage and calls PbrShaders::is_pbr for each opaque mesh, which is a linear search over all compiled variants (pbr.rs:402 `self.programs.values().any(...)`). The result is only used as `self.contact_shadows.is_some() && pbr_only`, and contact shadows are off by default and in every quality preset.
  - scenario: Any scene with HDR output: e.g. 5,000 opaque meshes and 30 PBR variants -> up to 150,000 comparisons per frame on the hot path for a value that is discarded because contact_shadows is None.
  - fix: Short-circuit: compute pbr_only only when self.contact_shadows.is_some() (`let key = self.contact_shadows.is_some() && { ...scan... }`), and make is_pbr a set lookup.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L596: apply_occlusion walks all shadow casters once or twice per frame for values that rarely change**
  - description: Every frame with AO or contact shadows on, apply_occlusion calls crate::lighting::scene_bounds (= views::caster_bounds: a pass over every ShadowCaster with a world-matrix lookup, bounds transform, visibility check, and joint loops for skinned meshes) just to derive `scene_size` for the automatic radius/length; with contact shadows it also calls key_light (line 558), which calls caster_bounds a second time for the scene centre. Nothing is cached between frames even when no caster moved.
  - scenario: setAmbientOcclusion({}) + setContactShadows({}) on a 10,000-mesh static scene: two extra full caster traversals (20,000 box transforms) on the CPU every frame, in addition to the shadow system's own.
  - fix: Compute caster_bounds once per frame and pass it to key_light, or cache the bounds in the lighting system (it already knows when casters moved) and reuse it here.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/picking.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L111: screenToRay / raycastBounds are broken for cameras with an infinite far plane**
  - description: Both go through crate::pick::camera_ray -> Ray::through (pick.rs:22-30), which unprojects ndc at z = +1 with the inverse view-projection. For the infinite projection (Camera::projection_matrix with far = INFINITY: setCamera({zfar: Infinity}) as documented in CameraOptions, and glTF perspective cameras without zfar) the z = 1 plane is at infinity: the unprojected w is exactly 0, the far point is (+-inf / NaN, ..., -inf), and `(far - near).normalize_or_zero()` returns Vec3::ZERO. I ran this with glam 0.30.10 for several fov/aspect/near/view combinations: dir is (0,0,0) in every case. pick_bounds then feeds a zero direction to ray_aabb (recip = inf), which yields either None or a hit with t = +inf (far = inf >= near = inf) and point = origin + 0*inf = NaN.
  - scenario: scene.setCamera(cam, {type:'perspective', zfar: Infinity}) (or a glTF camera with no zfar, via scene.cameras(root)); scene.screenToRay(cam, 0.3, -0.4) -> [ox, oy, oz, 0, 0, 0]; scene.raycastBounds(cam, x, y) -> null for meshes in front of the camera, or a PickHit with distance Infinity and NaN point for whatever mesh happens to straddle the origin on the other axes. GPU pick() is unaffected.
  - fix: In Ray::through don't unproject z = 1: unproject two finite depths (e.g. z = -1 and z = 0) or build the direction from the inverse projection of (ndc, -1) relative to the eye for perspective cameras; add a test with far = INFINITY.
- [ ] **[low/api] L25: pick, worldToScreen, screenToRay and raycastBounds use a stale camera aspect**
  - description: Only renderFrame and frameBounds call sync_camera_aspect. The four query methods read Camera.aspect as stored: 1.0 for a camera that has not been rendered yet (js/lights.rs:31), or the old value between a canvas resize and the next frame. pick() also narrows with the current framebuffer width and height while projecting with the old aspect, so the two disagree.
  - scenario: Create a camera on a 16:9 canvas and call worldToScreen or pick before the first renderFrame (or right after a resize in on-demand rendering): x is off by the aspect ratio and pick hits the wrong mesh away from the centre.
  - fix: Call self.sync_camera_aspect(camera) at the start of these four methods, as frame_bounds does.
- [ ] **[low/api] L29: pick / worldToScreen / screenToRay / raycastBounds use a stale camera aspect (1 for a camera not rendered yet)**
  - description: Only renderFrame and frameBounds call sync_camera_aspect; set_camera gives a new camera aspect 1.0 (`map_or(1.0, ...)`). pick (line 29), world_to_screen (99), screen_to_ray (110) and raycast_bounds (123) only run transforms.update and then use the stored Camera.aspect, although their docs describe x, y in the canvas's NDC and pick even passes the canvas's width/height to the renderer next to that stale aspect. frame_bounds has the fix and its comment names the problem (`a new camera has 1 until then`).
  - scenario: Canvas 1600x800. const cam = scene.spawn(); scene.setCamera(cam, {...}); scene.lookAt(...); scene.worldToScreen(cam, p) or scene.screenToRay(cam, 0.8, 0) before the first renderFrame(cam) (e.g. laying out hotspots at init, or a camera used only for queries): results are for a square view, x is off by a factor 2. Likewise for a query between a canvas resize and the next frame.
  - fix: Call self.sync_camera_aspect(camera) at the start of pick, world_to_screen, screen_to_ray and raycast_bounds, as frame_bounds does.

## `packages/luxel/src/js/restore.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L105: Loss during an environment bake restores the environment at the pre-bake intensity instead of 1**
  - description: restore() reads `self.renderer.environment_intensity()` and stores it as env_intensity_after_bake for the re-bake. That is right when the environment had already landed, but when the loss hits while the bake of a newly set environment is still pending (or Done but not yet applied), the renderer still holds the intensity of what was shown before: the default 0.2 flat ambient, or the previous environment's custom value. setEnvironmentHdr is documented to reset the intensity to 1 (render.rs:468), and the pending bake's own env_intensity_after_bake (None, or what the user set while baking) is discarded by start_environment. js/lights.rs:421 (setCompactEnvironment) has the same fallback.
  - scenario: new Scene; scene.setEnvironmentHdr(bytes, 256); the context is lost before the bake is applied (startup on a memory-pressed phone). After restore the bake runs again and apply_environment sets intensity to the saved 0.2: the HDR environment lights the scene five times too dark, permanently, although the app never touched the intensity. Same with replacing an environment that was at intensity 3: the new one comes back at 3 instead of 1.
  - fix: Before cancelling, decide from the pending state: if a bake was pending, keep `self.env_intensity_after_bake` (None meaning 'reset to 1'); only when no bake was pending use the renderer's current intensity.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L105: A loss while an environment bake is pending restores the environment at the pre-bake intensity instead of 1**
  - description: restore() reads renderer.environment_intensity() and stores it as env_intensity_after_bake for the re-bake. That is right once the environment has landed, but while the first bake of a new environment is still pending (or Done and not yet applied) the renderer still holds the intensity of what was shown before (0.2 for the default ambient). set_environment would have reset it to 1; the pending bake's own env_intensity_after_bake is discarded by start_environment. setCompactEnvironment (js/lights.rs:421-426) uses the same fallback.
  - scenario: new Scene; scene.setEnvironmentHdr(bytes, 256); the context is lost before a frame applies the bake (startup, or the tab was hidden). After the restore the re-bake lands and apply_environment sets the intensity to the saved 0.2: the HDR environment lights the scene five times too dark for the rest of the session. Same without a loss: setEnvironmentHdr immediately followed by setCompactEnvironment(false).
  - fix: If a bake was pending, carry over self.env_intensity_after_bake as it is (None meaning 'reset to 1'); only use the renderer's current intensity when no bake was pending.
- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L141: scene_programs keeps and recompiles shaders the app already destroyed; alias maps never shrink**
  - description: createProgram pushes (id, vert, frag) into scene_programs (mod.rs:595) and documents the program as the app's to destroy with ctx.destroyProgram. Nothing ever removes the entry: the sources stay for the scene's life, and restore_programs recompiles every entry after a loss ('Every one, used or not'). The recompiled program has a new handle the app does not hold (only reachable through currentProgram(old)), and Scene::destroy does not destroy scene_programs. program_aliases and texture_aliases also gain one entry per program/texture per loss (lines 160, 233) and are never pruned, including for textures removed with destroyTexture.
  - scenario: A shader editor / hot reload: on each edit `p2 = scene.createProgram(v, f); ctx.destroyProgram(p1)`. After 200 edits the scene holds 200 source pairs. On a context loss restore_programs compiles and links 200 programs (a long stall without parallel compile), 199 of which nothing uses and nobody will ever destroy; they live until the context dies. Each further loss repeats it.
  - fix: Drop a scene_programs entry when its program is no longer valid at restore time is not possible (all are dead then), so track destruction: add scene.destroyProgram (removing the entry and aliases) or check validity of each entry before a loss is observed (e.g. prune entries whose id is invalid on createProgram / each frame while generation is current); destroy remaining scene programs' aliases on destroy and prune alias entries whose target is gone.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L148: A loss in the middle of Scene::restore loses shaders permanently: scene programs are dropped, PBR meshes stop being recognised as PBR**
  - description: restore_programs is not re-entrant across generations. (1) scene_programs.retain_mut returns false on any create error, including ContextLost, so the source pair is forgotten and no later restore recompiles it or maps its alias. (2) PBR meshes are recognised by `old_pbr.is_pbr(mesh.program)` where old_pbr is the table of the previous restore. If pbr_program(e) failed for a mesh (line 168-175, context lost again while rebuilding), the mesh keeps its generation-0 program id; at the next restore old_pbr is the partial generation-1 table, the id is not in it, the mesh is treated as an app shader, `remap.get` finds nothing and it keeps a stale program forever. refresh_shadow_caster and update_variant then also bail out (`!is_pbr`). Renderer, textures, environment and models all retry on the next generation; only programs do not.
  - scenario: Memory-pressed phone: context restored, renderFrame -> restore() starts recreating everything, the driver drops the context again half way (isContextLost flips synchronously in Chrome). Lost + restored events follow, restore() runs again. Result: every createProgram shader compiled after the failure point is gone (currentProgram returns the dead handle), and every model mesh after that point is never drawn again ('render: program ... is destroyed or invalid'), does not cast shadows and no longer follows material variant changes.
  - fix: Keep scene_programs entries when the error is a context loss (retain unless a real shader error), and keep 'is built-in PBR' as per-mesh state (a component/flag or a set of entities) instead of deriving it from membership in the last generation's program table; for a failed pbr_program leave a marker so the next restore retries.
- [ ] **[medium/async] L216: loadTexture in flight at a loss rejects with 'can't load', yet the scene reloads the texture under a handle nobody gets**
  - description: loadTexture pushes its LoadedTexture into self.textures right away (materials.rs:381). If the context is lost before the image arrives, texture.rs:1574 reports (0, 0) for the dead texture, so the loadTexture closure (materials.rs:366-374) rejects the promise with `loadTexture: can't load <url>`. The entry stays in self.textures, so restore_textures starts the load again into a new texture and records old->new in texture_aliases, but the app never received the old handle (its promise rejected), so it cannot use, alias or destroy the reloaded texture.
  - scenario: App starts `const t = await scene.loadTexture(url)` for several textures; the context is lost while they download. Every promise rejects with a misleading 'can't load' error for a URL that is fine. After the restore the scene downloads and uploads all of them again; they sit in GPU memory unreferenced until scene.destroy(), and are reloaded again at every later loss.
  - fix: On a (0, 0) for a texture that is no longer valid because of a loss, either keep the promise pending and resolve it from the restore reload (with the new handle), or reject with a ContextLostError and remove the LoadedTexture entry so restore does not reload an orphan.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L308: App meshes parented under a model get Reloading and never lose it**
  - description: reload_model (both loops, lines 297-303 and 306-312) inserts `Reloading` on every entity in own_entities(root) that has a MeshRenderer. own_entities is every descendant whose nearest model root is this one, so it includes entities the app parented under a model node and drew with setMesh. apply_reloads (line 391) only removes `Reloading` from entities that have a `Primitive` (`else { continue }`), and nothing else in the crate removes it (setMesh only inserts MeshRenderer). render.rs:824 skips Reloading meshes and counts them as pending.
  - scenario: App: root = addModel(m); e = spawn(); setParent(e, findByName(root,'Hand')); setMesh(e, own program/vao). Context is lost and restored. restore() marks e Reloading; the app rebuilds its GL objects in setOnRestored and calls setMesh(e, ...) again; the model reload lands and its primitives come back, but e keeps Reloading forever: it is never drawn again, scene.pending() stays > 0 and needsRender() stays true (on_demand.rs:117).
  - fix: Mark only entities that have a `Primitive` (the model's own primitives) as Reloading in both loops (and in instantiate), or remove Reloading from every own entity in apply_reloads regardless of Primitive, and in setMesh.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L308: App meshes parented under a model are marked Reloading at a restore and never unmarked**
  - description: reload_model inserts Reloading on every own_entities(root) entity with a MeshRenderer (both loops, lines 297-303 and 306-312). own_entities is every descendant whose nearest model root is this one, so it includes entities the app hung under a model node and drew with setMesh. apply_reloads only removes Reloading from entities that have a Primitive (line 391-393 `else { continue }`), and nothing else removes it (setMesh only inserts a MeshRenderer). render.rs:824 leaves Reloading meshes out and counts them as pending.
  - scenario: root = addModel(m); e = spawn(); setParent(e, findByName(root, 'Hand')); setMesh(e, own program/vao). Context lost and restored: restore() marks e; the app rebuilds its GL objects in setOnRestored and calls setMesh(e, ...) again; the model reload lands. e stays Reloading: never drawn again, scene.pending() stays > 0 and needsRender() stays true.
  - fix: Mark only entities with a Primitive (in both loops and in instantiate), or remove Reloading from every own entity in apply_reloads and in set_mesh.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L308: App meshes parented under a model are marked Reloading after a loss and never unmarked**
  - description: `reload_model` inserts `Reloading` on every entity of `own_entities(root)` that has a `MeshRenderer` (both loops, lines 297-303 and 306-312). `own_entities` is every descendant whose nearest model root is this one, so it includes entities the app spawned and hung under the model with its own `setMesh`. `apply_reloads` removes `Reloading` only after `let Some(item) = self.world.get::<Primitive>(e) ... else { continue }` (lines 391-409), so an entity without a `Primitive` keeps the marker for ever. The module doc says such meshes are 'the app's to rebuild', but the scene has taken them out of frames. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: `root = scene.addModel(m); e = scene.spawn(); scene.setParent(e, scene.findByName(root, 'Hand')); scene.setMesh(e, ...)`. Context lost and restored: `e` gets `Reloading`. The app rebuilds its vertex array and program in `setOnRestored` and calls `setMesh(e, ...)` again; the model comes back, `e` is never drawn again.
  - fix: Mark only entities that have a `Primitive` (in both loops and in `instantiate`), or drop `Reloading` from every own entity in `apply_reloads` before the `Primitive` lookup.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L346: A failed model reload leaves the model invisible for good, with needsRender stuck true and no signal to the app**
  - description: When a reload result is Err (network error on the refetch, or adopt failing at line 366 because the file changed), apply_reloads only logs to the console and `continue`s. The model's meshes keep `Reloading`, the model stays lost, and nothing retries: reload_model is only called from restore() (next loss) and addModel. The reload task resolves normally, so whenReady() resolves as if everything were back. Reloading meshes count as stats.pending (render.rs:824), which on_demand.rs:117 treats as 'still changing'.
  - scenario: Mobile tab comes back from the background; the context is restored while the network is still down. The model refetch fails once. Result: the model never reappears, `await scene.whenReady()` resolves successfully, scene.pending() stays > 0 and scene.needsRender() returns true on every frame forever (an on-demand app renders continuously), and the app has no API to learn of the failure or to retry short of despawn + loadModel + addModel.
  - fix: Surface the failure (reject the restore task so whenReady rejects, or an event / getter listing roots whose reload failed), and either retry or take the failed model's meshes out of the `pending` count so needsRender can settle; expose a way to retry a root's reload.
- [ ] **[medium/api] L346: A failed (or disabled) model reload leaves the meshes Reloading forever: needsRender stuck true, whenReady resolves as ready, no retry**
  - description: When a reload result is Err (network error on the refetch) or adopt fails (line 366-369), apply_reloads logs and continues. The meshes keep Reloading, the model stays lost, nothing retries (reload_model is called only by the next restore and addModel). The reload task resolves normally, so whenReady() resolves as if everything were back. Reloading meshes are counted in stats.pending (render.rs:824, lighting/casters.rs:61), and on_demand.rs:117 treats pending > 0 as 'keeps changing'. The same permanent state is entered on purpose with setRestoreModels(false) / forgetSource (lines 291-304).
  - scenario: A mobile tab returns from the background, the context is restored while the network is still down; the refetch fails once. The model never reappears, `await scene.whenReady()` resolves, scene.pending() stays > 0 and needsRender() is true on every tick, so an on-demand app renders continuously (battery) a scene without the model; the app has no signal and no retry short of despawn + loadModel + addModel. With setRestoreModels(false) the app idles never until it has replaced every model.
  - fix: Report the failure (reject the restore task so whenReady rejects, or an event/getter with the failed roots), offer a retry, and do not count meshes whose reload has failed or will never be attempted as pending.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L98: Cancelling a finished-but-unapplied bake at restore destroys stale handles and logs errors**
  - description: restore() calls `p.cancel()` on env_pending. If the bake had finished (PendingState::Done(Ok(env))) but no frame applied it before the loss, PendingEnvironment::cancel (environment.rs:635) calls env.destroy(&ctx), i.e. ctx.destroy_texture on two handles from the lost generation. restore() runs at the first scene call after the restored event, when Context::stale is no longer in its quiet spell (context_loss.rs:115), so each call reports '[luxel] destroy_texture: texture ... is destroyed or invalid; ignored' via console.error. Unlike EnvBaker::destroy and SceneRenderer::destroy, this path is not generation-aware.
  - scenario: setEnvironmentHdr, then the tab is hidden (rAF stops, the bake's own timer task finishes the bake), the context is lost in the background and restored when the tab returns. The first renderFrame logs console errors about invalid textures although nothing is wrong.
  - fix: Make PendingEnvironment::cancel (and BakeJob::cancel) skip GPU destroys when their generation differs from ctx.generation(), as the other destroy paths do, or drop the pending result without destroying in restore().
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L141: createProgram shaders the app destroyed are kept, recompiled after a loss and owned by nobody**
  - description: `create_program` (js/mod.rs:592-600) stores `(id, vert, frag)` in `scene_programs` and documents the program as the app's to destroy with `ctx.destroyProgram`. Nothing removes the entry then. `restore_programs` recompiles every entry ('Every one, used or not') under a new handle reachable only through `currentProgram(old)`, and `Scene::destroy` (js/mod.rs:1236) frees none of them. `program_aliases` and `texture_aliases` also gain one entry per program / texture per loss and are never pruned. Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: A shader editor calls `scene.createProgram` on every edit and `ctx.destroyProgram` on the previous one, 200 times: 200 source pairs stay in memory; after a context loss the scene compiles 200 programs again and they stay on the context, since the app holds no live handle for them. Also: createProgram -> loss + restore -> `scene.destroy()` leaves the recompiled program alive on a context that outlives the scene.
  - fix: Offer `scene.destroyProgram` (dropping the entry and its aliases), prune entries whose program is no longer valid while the generation is current (e.g. in `create_program` and before a restore is observed), and destroy in `Scene::destroy` the programs the scene itself recompiled.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L199: Textures whose loadTexture failed are re-created and re-fetched at every restore**
  - description: When a loadTexture image fails to load, materials.rs:369 destroys the texture and rejects, but the LoadedTexture stays in self.textures. restore_textures iterates all entries, so after each context loss it creates a fresh GL texture for the failed URL and fetches it again; on the repeated failure the restore closure (line 209) only logs and leaves the new, storage-less texture alive. No app handle maps to it.
  - scenario: scene.loadTexture('missing.png') rejects (404). Later the context is lost and restored: the scene requests missing.png again, logs '[luxel] restore: can't load missing.png', and keeps an empty texture object until scene.destroy(); the same again on every loss.
  - fix: Remove the LoadedTexture entry when its first load fails (in loadTexture's failure branch), and in the restore closure destroy the texture / drop the entry when the reload fails.
- [ ] **[low/api] L238: No currentTexture for JS although loadTexture handles change after a loss**
  - description: Programs get a public `currentProgram` to map a pre-loss handle; textures have only the crate-private current_texture. loadTexture's doc says the texture comes back under 'a new handle', but the only JS calls that map the old handle are setMaterialTexture and destroyTexture. The app cannot obtain the live handle for a loadTexture texture that is not currently assigned to a material map.
  - scenario: After a loss the app passes its loadTexture handle to a ctx call (its own param group / batch, ctx.textureSetWrap) or compares `scene.materialTexture(m, 'baseColorTexture') === myTex`: the ctx call reports a stale handle and the comparison is false, with no API to translate the handle.
  - fix: Expose `currentTexture(texture)` to JS next to currentProgram (and mention it in loadTexture's doc).
- [ ] **[low/api] L293: Warning blames setRestoreModels(false) when the model simply has no source**
  - description: `model.reload_request().filter(|_| self.restore_models)` is None both when restore_models is off and when the model has no request (the app called the public Model.forgetSource() before addModel). The warning text is fixed: 'models aren't fetched again (setRestoreModels(false))'. It is also warn_once, so only the first model is ever reported.
  - scenario: App calls model.forgetSource() on one large data:-URI model and leaves restore models on. After a loss that model silently stays out of frames; the console says setRestoreModels(false) is set, which it is not, and names no model.
  - fix: Distinguish the two cases in the message and name the model (model.source()); warn per model rather than once.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L342: A reload landing does not re-arm the TAA settle count: the model is drawn for one TAA frame only**
  - description: apply_reloads runs inside the frame (render_passes) and its changes (note_content_changed, Reloading removed, vertex arrays switched) are deliberately swallowed by on_demand::rendered(), which resets d.content and d.observed after the frame (on_demand.rs:110-111). observe() ran before apply_reloads and saw no change, so `settle` stays 0. With TAA on, every other visible change gets TAA_SETTLE (30) frames to converge; the frame in which a reloaded model first appears gets exactly one. Images arriving later bump the content version again, so only models without late-arriving images are affected.
  - scenario: TAA on, on-demand loop (`needsRender() || !presentFrame()`), a model without textures (or whose images decoded before that frame). After a context loss the reload lands: keeps_changing() is true only until the inbox is drained, one jittered frame with the model is drawn against a history that never contained it, then needsRender() is false and presentFrame keeps showing that single unconverged frame.
  - fix: When apply_reloads applied at least one model, re-arm the settle count (e.g. call self.invalidate() after the frame's `rendered`, or have apply_reloads set a flag that rendered() turns into settle = TAA_SETTLE).
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L342: A reload (or environment bake) landing inside a frame does not re-arm the TAA settle count**
  - description: apply_reloads and apply_environment run inside render_passes and bump content_version there; on_demand::rendered() then resets d.content and d.observed after the frame ('what the frame wrote itself isn't a change'), and observe() ran before them. keeps_changing() is true only while the inbox / env_pending is non-empty, so the frame that first shows the reloaded model is the last one requested. With TAA every other visible change gets TAA_SETTLE (30) frames.
  - scenario: TAA on, on-demand loop, model without late images (or images decoded earlier), context lost and restored. The reload lands: one jittered frame with the model is drawn against a history that never contained it, then needsRender() is false and presentFrame keeps showing that single unconverged, aliased frame. Same for an environment whose bake takes longer than 30 frames.
  - fix: When apply_reloads / apply_environment applied something, set a flag that rendered() turns into settle = TAA_SETTLE (or call invalidate() after rendered()).
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L349: apply_reloads reports a reload aborted by a second loss as an error: its `!ctx.is_lost()` guard is always true**
  - description: The comment says a second loss mid-reload just starts another reload and anything else is reported. But apply_reloads is only reached from render_passes after sync_context() returned true, so `self.ctx.is_lost()` is always false there; the Err('WebGL context lost while the model loaded') produced by luxel-gltf lib.rs:909 for the superseded reload is logged as '[luxel] restore: a model: ...' although the newer restore has already started the reload again.
  - scenario: Loss, restore, model refetching; second loss before the files are in; second restore. The first frame after it logs a console error per model for a reload that is in fact being retried and succeeds.
  - fix: Decide by the error kind (ContextLost) or by `model.is_lost` / the generation the reload was started in, not by the context's current state.
- [ ] **[low/async] L349: A reload that failed because of a second loss is reported as a model error once the context is back**
  - description: `apply_reloads` tells 'a second loss mid-reload' from a real failure with `!self.ctx.is_lost()` evaluated when the result is applied, not when the load failed. A reload that returned `Err(context lost while the model loaded)` during the second loss sits in the inbox, because `render_passes` returns before `apply_reloads` while the context is lost. On the first frame after the restore, `restore()` starts the new reload and then `apply_reloads` processes the old Err with the context no longer lost, so it logs `[luxel] restore: a model: WebGL context lost while the model loaded`. The same happens for a model passed to `addModel` while the context is lost.
  - scenario: Lose the context, restore it, lose it again while the models are still downloading, restore it. The console shows 'restore: a model: …context lost…' once per model although every model comes back a moment later; an app watching console errors or the user sees a failure that did not happen.
  - fix: Decide by the error's kind (`Error::context_lost`), or stamp each reload with the generation it started in and drop results from an older generation silently.
- [ ] **[low/api] L458: setOnRestored doc says needsRender triggers the rebuild; it does not**
  - description: The doc comment says the callback fires 'at its first call after the context is back (a frame, `needsRender`)'. needs_render (on_demand.rs:68) only calls observe() and keeps_changing(); it never calls sync_context(), so it neither rebuilds the scene nor schedules onRestored. Only renderFrame/render, createProgram, addModel, instantiate, loadTexture, setMaterialTexture, setEnvironmentHdr, whenReady, picking etc. do.
  - scenario: An app that polls `scene.needsRender()` and, on true, does other work before rendering (or that waits for onRestored to re-create its meshes before it resumes rendering) never gets onRestored from needsRender alone; the scene stays un-restored until some other call happens.
  - fix: Either call sync_context() at the top of needs_render, or correct the doc to list the calls that actually rebuild.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/cache.rs`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L37: Shadow cache never sees async texture content landing: alpha-masked casters keep a solid shadow**
  - description: A map's signature hashes the epoch, the view, and per caster the program, vao, material group id, draw command and transform/joint/morph ticks. glTF images are filled into an existing TextureId asynchronously (luxel-gltf textures.rs build_texture -> texture_load_bytes), so nothing in the signature changes when they arrive. The core does bump Context::content_version at that point (luxel-gl texture.rs:623/1581/1688) and js/on_demand.rs uses it for needsRender, but nothing in lighting/ reads it and no image callback calls invalidate_shadows (only the material setters in js/materials.rs do). The DEPTH_ONLY fragment stage discards on base_color().a < u_alpha_cutoff (shaders/pbr.frag:324-326); a texture with no storage yet is incomplete and samples (0,0,0,1), so the mask passes everywhere. The same signature drives the ground shadow (mod.rs:381), and frozen shadows bake in this state because `waiting` counts only linking programs and Reloading models (casters.rs:61).
  - scenario: loadModel() resolves before its images; addModel(root) with an ALPHAMODE_MASK material (foliage, fence), a spot/point light or a directional light whose single fitted map is used, static scene. The first frames draw the shadow map with the base-colour texture still empty: full quads cast. The image lands, the lit pass shows cut-out leaves, but the map's signature is unchanged so it is never redrawn: solid rectangular shadows (and ground shadow) stay until a caster moves or a material setter runs. With setShadowsFrozen(true) they stay for good.
  - fix: Fold ctx.content_version() into what the signatures hash (e.g. in LightingSystem::prepare compare it with the last seen value and bump `epoch`, clear `baked` and call ground.invalidate() when it differs), including in the frozen-and-baked early path.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L68: Signatures walk every joint of a skin once per primitive per view, every frame**
  - description: For each caster draw with a Skin, the newest joint tick is recomputed with a row lookup per joint (lines 68-77). Draws are per (view, primitive), so a skin shared by P primitives seen by V views costs V x P x joints lookups each frame, even when nothing moves (the result is the same for all of them). The ground shadow runs the same code again for its view (mod.rs:381).
  - scenario: A character of 30 primitives sharing a 150-joint skin under a 4-cascade sun plus the ground shadow: 5 x 30 x 150 = 22,500 sparse-set lookups per frame just to conclude the maps are unchanged.
  - fix: Memoise the newest joint tick per skin (keyed by the Rc/slice pointer of skin.joints or by entity) for the duration of one signatures() call, or better once per frame shared between the maps' and the ground's signature passes.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/casters.rs`

- [ ] **[low/perf] L54: Per-caster visibility, link and reload checks are repeated for every shadow view**
  - description: cull() nests the caster loop inside the view loop, so for each of up to 16 views every caster repeats the MeshRenderer lookup, the hidden() walk up the Parent chain (a world.get::<Visible> and world.get::<Parent> per ancestor, whenever anything in the scene is hidden), ctx.program_link_pending (takes the context's resource borrow) and the Reloading lookup. None of these depend on the view; only the frustum test does. It runs every non-frozen frame, before the cache can tell which maps are unchanged, and again for the ground view.
  - scenario: Scene with 5000 casters in a 4-deep hierarchy, one hidden entity anywhere, a 3-cascade sun plus two shadowing point lights (15 views): 75,000 hidden() walks (~600k component lookups) plus 75,000 link-pending checks per frame, instead of 5000.
  - fix: Build the list of eligible casters once (mesh, row, world bounds; counting waiting casters once), then loop views over that list doing only the frustum test.
- [ ] **[low/api] L63: Waiting casters are counted once per shadow view, inflating stats.pending**
  - description: `waiting += 1` sits inside the per-view loop and before the frustum test, so one caster whose depth program is still linking is counted once for every view this frame. LightingSystem::waiting() is documented as 'Casters this frame left out of their maps' and render.rs:789 adds it to stats.pending described as pending meshes.
  - scenario: One model with 20 primitives whose shadow variants are linking, a 3-cascade sun and one shadowing point light (9 views): the frame reports 180 pending shadow casters on top of the 20 pending meshes, and the count jumps as cascades come and go.
  - fix: Count each waiting caster once (see the single eligibility pass above), or document the value as view x caster pairs.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/ground.rs`

- [ ] **[low/async] L148: The ground shadow's darkness and blur programs are not in the readiness list: first use can stall on a synchronous link**
  - description: Ground::new creates three programs, but LightingSystem::programs() (mod.rs:253) reports only plane_program, and Ground::plane() checks link-pending only for that one. Ground::render submits the darkness and blur full-screen passes unconditionally; the core's run_pass does not skip a program that is still linking, it waits on it (renderer.rs ensure_linked: 'waited on, not skipped'). So whenReady()/pending counts do not cover them and the first frame that draws the ground shadow blocks on getProgramParameter(LINK_STATUS) if the background compile has not finished, which KHR_parallel_shader_compile and the scene's 'leave out rather than stall' policy are meant to avoid.
  - scenario: new Scene(...); setGroundShadow({}); await whenReady(); renderFrame() right after construction on a slow mobile driver: the meshes and the plane wait politely for their shaders, but the darkness/blur programs are forced to finish linking synchronously inside the first frame (a visible hitch).
  - fix: Return all three programs from Ground (and LightingSystem::programs()), and in Ground::prepare treat the shadow as not drawable (leave `drawn` unset, count it as waiting) while ctx.program_link_pending(darkness) or (blur).
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L258: Ground shadow textures stay allocated after the ground shadow is turned off**
  - description: Ground::prepare with view None only clears the pass and returns; free_textures is called only from allocate (size change) and destroy. set_ground_shadow(None) (mod.rs:231) just clears ground_on. The depth24 texture, two R8 textures and three framebuffers (up to 2048^2: ~25 MB) stay until the scene is destroyed.
  - scenario: setGroundShadow({size: 2048}) for one frame, then setGroundShadow(null): the three textures remain allocated for the rest of the session although nothing samples them.
  - fix: Free the textures when the ground shadow is turned off (set_ground_shadow(None) -> a Ground::release(ctx) at the next prepare that sees ground_on == false, resetting `drawn`).
- [ ] **[low/api] L261: GroundShadow.size is silently clamped and rounded, and the getter reports the unclamped value**
  - description: The texture side is settings.size.clamp(64, 2048).next_power_of_two(), applied only inside Ground::prepare. Neither the Rust doc ('The darkness texture's side in texels', line 55) nor GroundShadowOptions.size in js/options.rs mentions the 64..2048 range or the rounding up to a power of two, setGroundShadow (js/lights.rs:256) validates every field except size, and groundShadow() returns the stored raw value. The raw value is also what is passed to ground_view (mod.rs:368) and stored in ShadowView.size.
  - scenario: setGroundShadow({size: 4096}) -> groundShadow().size reads 4096 but the textures are 2048; setGroundShadow({size: 600}) allocates 1024 (2.9x the memory asked for); setGroundShadow({size: 0}) is accepted and yields 64.
  - fix: Clamp/round once when the setting is stored (as set_shadow_map_size does with ShadowOptions::clamp_size), so the getter and ground_view see the effective size, and document the range in both the Rust field and GroundShadowOptions.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/maps.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L152: The shadow array is never shrunk back once no light casts shadows**
  - description: ShadowMaps::resize is only reached from LightingSystem::plan after its early returns (mod.rs:485 no LightShadow, :488 no casters, :584 views_used == 0). When the last shadowing light is removed, hidden, or loses its LightShadow, plan returns before the resize, so the full-size depth array (size^2 x layers x 4 bytes, e.g. 2048^2 x 8 x 4 = 128 MB, or 4096^2 x 3 = 192 MB) and its framebuffers stay allocated and bound as u_shadow_map for the life of the renderer. shadow_bytes() documents 'a 1x1 placeholder while no light casts shadows' (mod.rs:291), which is only true before the first shadow.
  - scenario: setLight with shadows on a point + directional light at shadowMapSize 4096 -> array of several layers allocated. Later remove the lights or turn their shadows off: shadowViews() is 0 but the shadow memory getter still reports (and the GPU still holds) hundreds of MB; nothing short of destroying the scene frees it.
  - fix: In plan, when no view is planned (all three early-return paths) and maps.size() > 1, call maps.resize(ctx, 1, 1, false) and rebind SHADOW_MAP in the frame group (possibly after a few idle frames to avoid churn).
- [ ] **[low/perf] L315: A render state is interned per view per frame by linear search, and bias edits leave states behind**
  - description: LightingSystem::prepare calls ShadowMaps::state for every view each non-frozen frame (mod.rs:356-359), including views that are not dirty and will not be drawn. Context::render_state (luxel-gl render_state.rs:711) interns by a linear `iter().find` comparing whole RenderState values over every state the context holds, so each frame costs views x states comparisons (up to 16 x N). Interned states are never released, so every distinct (slope_bias, constant_bias) pair ever used stays in the pool.
  - scenario: A viewer with a bias slider bound to setLight's shadow options: dragging it creates one new pooled RenderState per distinct value (hundreds per drag), each of which then lengthens the per-frame search done 3-16 times a frame for the rest of the session.
  - fix: Keep the StateId per view next to its Held entry and re-intern only when the light's slope/constant bias or reverse-Z changed (and only for dirty views); or cache by (slope_bias bits, constant_bias bits, reverse) in ShadowMaps.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/mod.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/leaks] L584: Shadow map array is never shrunk or freed once no light casts shadows**
  - description: plan() returns early (no LightShadow storage / settings.is_empty() at 485, no caster bounds at 488, views_used == 0 at 584) before the resize block at 589-600, which is the only place the depth array is reallocated. Once a light has cast shadows the array stays at map_size^2 x layers for the life of the renderer, even after every shadowing light is removed, hidden, or set to castShadow:false. The doc of shadow_bytes() (line 291: 'a 1x1 placeholder while no light casts shadows') describes a state that is only true before the first shadow. A later set_shadow_map_size / set_shadow_depth16 to a smaller format is also not applied while no light casts.
  - scenario: Scene with a 3-cascade sun and a shadowing point light at shadow map size 4096: 5 layers x 4096^2 x 4 bytes = ~335 MB of depth texture. The app calls setLight(..., {castShadow:false}) on both (or hides them, or removes the model so caster_bounds is None). plan() returns at 485/488/584 every frame; shadowBytes keeps reporting ~335 MB and the GPU memory is held until the context is rebuilt.
  - fix: On the early-return paths (views_used == 0), resize the maps back to the 1x1 placeholder (self.maps.resize(ctx, 1, 1, false)) and rebind SHADOW_MAP on the frame group, possibly after a few idle frames to avoid thrash when a light is toggled.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L301: prepare() doc says only directional / spot lights cast shadows**
  - description: The doc comment of prepare() ('the directional / spot lights with a `LightShadow`, while `MAX_SHADOW_LAYERS` last') predates point-light shadows: plan() gives point lights six faces (513-518), and the limit that stops allotting is MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS as well as the layers. The same stale wording is in settings.rs:6 ('directional or spot') and views.rs:150 ('None when nothing can be in its shadow (no casters in front of a spot, a point light)') reads as if point lights never shadow.
  - scenario: A reader of the public LightShadow / module docs concludes point lights cannot cast shadows and that only the layer count limits shadowing lights, while a point light actually consumes 6 of the 16 views.
  - fix: Update the comments to name point lights and both limits (MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS and MAX_SHADOW_LAYERS).
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L358: Every distinct bias pair interns a render state that is never released**
  - description: prepare() calls ShadowMaps::state(ctx, &view.state) for each view each frame; Context::render_state (luxel-gl render_state.rs:711) interns by a linear search and inserts a new pool entry for every new (slope_bias, constant_bias) pair. Nothing ever removes them, and every later call scans the whole pool once per view per frame.
  - scenario: An editor drags shadowSlopeBias / shadowConstantBias sliders: each distinct float value adds a render state (the renderStates stat grows by one per step) and they stay for the life of the context, while each frame's 1-16 lookups scan the growing list.
  - fix: Keep one StateId per light slot in LightingSystem and update/destroy it when the biases change, or key a small cache on the bias bits and destroy states that fall out of use.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L502: Lights past MAX_LIGHTS still get shadow maps planned, drawn and stored**
  - description: plan() iterates every Light with a LightShadow and allots views until MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS / MAX_SHADOW_LAYERS run out. LightsBlock::write (pack.rs:97) packs only the first MAX_LIGHTS (8) visible lights and breaks; slots of later lights are never read by any shader. Both loops walk the same storage in the same order with the same skip rules, so any shadowing light at index >= 8 has its maps culled, drawn and counted in `could`/layers although nothing samples them.
  - scenario: 10 visible lights; lights 0-7 cast no shadows, lights 8 and 9 are spots with castShadow:true. Two full layers are allocated (2 x 2048^2 x 4 = 32 MB), casters are culled and drawn into both whenever they move, and shadowViews reports 2, while those two lights are not shaded at all ('more than 8 lights; the rest are ignored').
  - fix: Count visible lights in plan() with the same rule as pack.rs and stop planning once MAX_LIGHTS visible lights have been seen.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/pack.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L97: Lights past MAX_LIGHTS still get shadow maps planned and drawn, and can be picked as the key light**
  - description: LightsBlock::write stops at the first 8 visible lights, but LightingSystem::plan (mod.rs:502) walks every light with a LightShadow in the same storage order with no such cap, and key_light (light.rs:102) picks among all visible lights. A shadowing light that is 9th or later is not in u_lights, yet its views are laid out in the atlas, its casters culled, hashed and drawn every time they change, and it can grow the array by whole layers (a point light: 6 views, 1.5 layers) or push the atlas over MAX_SHADOW_LAYERS/MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS so that the pop loop at mod.rs:580 drops shadows. If the strongest light at the scene centre is 9th or later, key stays -1 (pack.rs:103-105) while js/output.rs:558 still computes contact shadows toward it: the share output is zero and contact shadows show nothing.
  - scenario: A scene with 9 visible lights where the 9th is a point light with shadows at the default size: 6 extra maps (1.5 layers of 2048^2, ~24 MB) are allocated and redrawn whenever a caster moves, for a light the shaders never evaluate; shadowViews()/shadowDrawCalls() count them.
  - fix: Share one notion of 'the lights this frame shades with': have plan and key_light consider only the first MAX_LIGHTS visible lights (same filter and order as write), e.g. by collecting that list once in prepare and passing it to plan, write and key_light.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L84: LightsBlock::write rescans every caster each frame via key_light, also on frozen frames**
  - description: write() calls super::key_light(world) unconditionally, which calls caster_bounds(world): a walk over every ShadowCaster with a Bounds transform (and an ancestor walk per caster when anything is hidden, plus every joint of skinned casters without bounds). plan() already computed the same bounds this frame (mod.rs:488), prepare_ground computes them a third time through ground_bounds, and js/output.rs:558 a fourth with contact shadows. In the frozen-and-baked path (mod.rs:312-327), documented as 'no shadow pass after the first frame', write() still does the full scan. It also allocates four Vecs per call before the `data == self.held` early-out, and when the block does change (every frame while cascades follow a moving camera) rewrites all ~120 members through format!()-built path strings.
  - scenario: A scene of 20k casters with one light, camera orbiting: each frame walks the caster storage 2-4 times just to recompute the same box, and formats ~120 uniform path strings to upload a block in which only the cascade matrices changed.
  - fix: Compute caster_bounds once per prepare and pass it (or the key entity) into write; cache the key light while lights/casters are unchanged (storage change ticks). Write only the members whose Vec4s differ from `held`, with precomputed paths or offsets.

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/settings.rs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L6: LightShadow docs still say only directional and spot lights cast shadows**
  - description: The LightShadow doc opens with 'Makes the light on the entity (directional or spot) cast shadows', and LightingSystem::prepare's doc (mod.rs:301) says 'the directional / spot lights with a LightShadow', while the same struct's map_size doc, maps.rs and plan() handle point lights (six faces, kind 2). The README was corrected for this in the style review ('point lights cast shadows too') but these doc comments were not.
  - scenario: A Rust user reading LightShadow's doc concludes that inserting it on a point light does nothing (or is unsupported) and does not budget for the six views / 1.5 layers it takes out of MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS = 16.
  - fix: Reword both comments to 'directional, spot or point', and note the point light's cost (six views, a quarter layer each by default).

## `packages/luxel/src/lighting/views.rs`

- [ ] **[low/correctness] L201: Spot (and point) light with a range shorter than the nearest caster builds an inverted/empty shadow frustum instead of no map**
  - description: shadow_view for a spot caps far to light.range (196-199) but computes near from the nearest caster corner afterwards: near = (nearest * 0.99).max(far * 1e-3). When every caster is farther than the range, near > far, and perspective_rh(_gl) is built with swapped planes. Nothing can land inside that clip volume, but the function still returns Some, so the light takes a whole layer (or six tiles in point_views, where far is likewise capped to range at 95-97 with no check that any caster is within range), is laid out in the atlas, culled every frame and can push later lights past MAX_SHADOW_VIEWS / MAX_SHADOW_LAYERS so they lose their shadows.
  - scenario: Spot light with range 5 and castShadow:true; all casters sit 10-20 units in front of it. depths: nearest = 10, far = min(20, 5) * 1.01 = 5.05, near = 9.9 > far. A 2048^2 layer is allocated and counted for a light that cannot shadow anything; with 8 such lights the 9th (useful) shadowing light is dropped with the 'shine unshadowed' warning.
  - fix: After applying the range, return None when the nearest caster depth (spot) or nearest box distance (point) is beyond light.range, i.e. when near >= far.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L521: caster_bounds collapses to a point for casters without Bounds and for single-joint skins, so the directional fit covers nothing**
  - description: A caster without a Bounds component contributes only its world origin (520-523), and a skinned caster contributes its joints' box padded by a third of the box's largest side (515-518) - zero padding when the skin has one joint or all joints coincide. If such casters are the only ones, lo == hi; shadow_view accepts it (min.cmpgt(max) is false), uses radius = 1e-4 and builds an orthographic map about 2e-4 world units wide. The mesh is not culled from the map (no Bounds) but almost all of it falls outside the map, so the light renders effectively unshadowed. With other casters present the box is merely too small and the unbounded mesh is clipped by the map's sides and near/far.
  - scenario: A model whose only mesh is skinned to a single joint (rigid-skinned prop), lit by a directional light with castShadow:true: caster_bounds returns (p, p), the shadow map spans ~0.0002 units around the joint and no shadow appears. Same for a Rust-API mesh given a ShadowCaster but no Bounds.
  - fix: Give the skin box a minimum pad (e.g. from the mesh's rest bounds size, kept alongside Skin) and treat a bounds-less caster as unknown extent (fall back to rest bounds or skip it with a once-only warning) instead of a zero-size point.

## `packages/luxel/src/model.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L85: Node TRS is re-derived from the matrix, so mirrored nodes animate wrongly when a clip targets only rotation or only scale**
  - description: The node Transform is decomposed from the composed local matrix, not taken from the file's TRS. For negative scales glam picks a different but matrix-equivalent decomposition: it only ever negates x. Checked in a scratch crate: file S=(1,-1,1), R=identity decomposes to scale (-1,1,1) with a 180 degree rotation about Z; S=(-1,-1,1) decomposes to scale (1,1,1) with the same 180 degree rotation. The static pose is right, but animation.rs write() replaces one component at a time with the file's channel values, which are expressed against the file's TRS, not glam's.
  - scenario: A node with scale [1,-1,1] (a mirrored limb or part) and identity rotation, and a clip with a rotation channel only. After the first sample the entity has the file's rotation with scale (-1,1,1) instead of (1,-1,1): the part is mirrored about the wrong axis for as long as the clip plays. Likewise a node with scale [-1,-1,1] and a scale-only channel ends up with the channel's scale on top of the spurious 180 degree rotation.
  - fix: Carry translation, rotation and scale per node in SceneNode (from gltf's decomposed transform; decompose only nodes given as a matrix) and build the Transform from them in spawn_model.
- [ ] **[medium/perf] L178: spawn_model deep-copies all animation keyframes for every spawn and every instantiate**
  - description: `clips: model.animations().into()` converts a `&[Animation]` into `Rc<[Animation]>`, which clones every Animation, including each channel's `times` and `values` Vecs. Model::animations() returns a slice (luxel-gltf lib.rs:613), so nothing is shared. The Rc in ModelAnimations suggests sharing was intended, and Scene.instantiate documents instances as sharing the model's data. Skin.inverse_bind is also rebuilt per spawn (model.rs:120).
