  - scenario: A character with 20 MB of keyframes: addModel leaves two copies in wasm memory (the Model's and the root's ModelAnimations). Each scene.instantiate(root) adds another 20 MB, so 50 instances of a crowd hold about 1 GB of duplicate keyframes, and each instantiate pays the copy time.
  - fix: Store the animations in the Model as `Rc<[Animation]>` (and inverse bind matrices as Rc) and hand out clones of the Rc; spawn_model then only clones the pointer.

## `packages/luxel/src/motion.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L144: Motion-vector history is per renderer, so any extra render() between renderFrames wipes the moved-mesh detection**
  - description: MotionVectors::begin swaps prev/next on every SceneRenderer::render_split (render.rs:773), and track() records states on every call. The JS Scene.render(camera, pass) (js/mod.rs:951) goes through the same render_split while motion vectors stay enabled; they are only switched in renderFrame (js/mod.rs:823) and for capture. After such a call, prev holds this frame's states and prev_view_proj is the other camera's (last_view_proj is overwritten in write_frame, render.rs:1404).
  - scenario: TAA on, an animated model, and an app that draws a second view of the scene each frame with scene.render(cam2, pass) (minimap, picture-in-picture, offscreen thumbnail) after advanceAnimations and before renderFrame. In renderFrame every mesh's prev state equals its current state, so no motion draws are emitted (frameStats moving = 0) and TAA reprojects moving or skinned meshes as if static: ghost trails. If instead the order makes a state differ, the motion draw uses cam2's view-projection as u_prev_view_proj and writes wrong velocities.
  - fix: Only advance the motion history for the frame that feeds TAA: do begin/track when the caller asks for it (a flag from renderFrame), or keep the history keyed by camera and skip tracking in plain render().
- [ ] **[low/perf] L167: track() rebuilds every mesh's state each frame, including a second full joint-matrix pass per skinned primitive**
  - description: For every drawn single-instance mesh, track() builds a fresh State, and for a skinned primitive allocates a Vec and computes world(joint) x inverse_bind for all joints, the same products write_deformers (render.rs:1222) computes again for the instance texture. Each State is then inserted into a HashMap that is cleared and refilled every frame. The work happens before the `if !opaque { return; }` check at line 198, so transparent and transmissive meshes pay for a State that is dropped at once. The module comment's claim that a static scene costs nothing holds for the GPU only.
  - scenario: TAA on, a character of 20 skinned primitives sharing a 150-joint skin: about 3000 extra Mat4 multiplies, 20 Vec allocations and 20 HashMap inserts per frame even when nothing moves, and the same again for any transparent skinned parts that are never given motion vectors.
  - fix: Return before building the state for non-opaque meshes. Reuse the previous frame's State buffers (swap the Vecs instead of reallocating), and compute a skin's joint matrices once per frame per skin, shared with write_deformers.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L219: Motion variant is chosen from the Skin component, not the primitive's skin attributes**
  - description: track() sets VARIANT_SKIN whenever the entity has a Skin. spawn_model inserts Skin whenever the node has a skin (item.skin() is node-level, model.rs:139), also for a primitive without JOINTS_0 / WEIGHTS_0. The PBR variant for such a primitive has no HAS_SKIN (pbr.rs keys on ATTR_SKIN) and draws with the node's model matrix. motion.vert with HAS_SKIN reads locations 6 and 7, which the vertex array does not provide, so the generic attribute value (0,0,0,1) is used: every vertex goes through joint 0's matrix. The same primitive also gets no Bounds (model.rs:139-146), so it is never frustum-culled although it is drawn rigid.
  - scenario: A glTF node with both a skin and a mesh whose primitive has no joint attributes (valid, a validator warning), TAA on, skeleton animating. Every frame State.joints differs, so a motion draw is pushed; its geometry is placed by joint 0, not where the scene drew the mesh. Fragments mostly fail the depth-equality test (the mesh gets no velocity when its node really moves, and ghosts), and where depths happen to match a wrong velocity is written.
  - fix: Decide skinning in track() the way pbr.rs does: require `Primitive.attributes & ATTR_SKIN != 0` before reading Skin. In spawn_model, attach Skin (and skip Bounds) only when the item's attributes carry ATTR_SKIN.

## `packages/luxel/src/occlusion.rs`

- [ ] **[low/leaks] L312: Occlusion targets and programs stay allocated after AO and contact shadows are turned off**
  - description: free_targets is reached only from allocate (resize) and destroy. Scene keeps self.occlusion until the scene is destroyed or the context is lost: setAmbientOcclusion(null), setContactShadows(null), setHdr(false) and setQuality('low') leave the two RG8 targets, their framebuffers and three programs alive. By contrast the direct target is destroyed as soon as HDR takes over (js/output.rs:437).
  - scenario: An app turns AO on once at 4K and then off for the rest of the session: about 2 x (1920 x 1080 x 2 bytes) of textures at half resolution, or four times that at full resolution, are never released.
  - fix: When both settings are None (or HDR is off), take self.occlusion and destroy it, or at least free its targets.

## `packages/luxel/src/output.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/api] L685: The canvas clear color goes through exposure, auto exposure and tone mapping in HDR, so it is not shown as set**
  - description: prepare_split writes the canvas clear color into the HDR target as linear premultiplied radiance; present.frag then runs it through full_exposure() and tone_map() like scene radiance. Nothing undoes either (the background fill is composited after tone mapping for exactly this reason, the clear color is not). renderFrame's doc and this module's header both say the target is cleared to the canvas's clear color, and with setHdr(false) the same clear color is displayed unchanged. With the default Neutral curve the toe (x < 0.08: offset = x - 6.25x^2) darkens dark colors strongly; exposure/auto exposure scale it; ACES/AgX shift it further.
  - scenario: defaultFramebuffer().setClearColor(0.2, 0.2, 0.2, 1) with defaults (HDR, neutral): linear 0.033 -> 0.0068 -> about sRGB 0.05, i.e. #333333 is shown as roughly #0d0d0d. setHdr(false) shows #333333. setExposure(2) or setAutoExposure(true) changes the background again, frame to frame.
  - fix: Treat the clear color like the fill: clear the target to transparent (0,0,0,0 coverage) and lay the clear color under the scene in present.frag after tone mapping (it already has that path for u_fill_a), or document that only setBackgroundFill is display-referred.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] L789: Transparent meshes vanish behind glass with MSAA and a scaled transmission copy (the "medium" preset)**
  - description: depth_into_grab returns false whenever the target is multisampled and grab_size != size, because a multisampled depth can only be blitted 1:1. grab() then skips the see_through pass entirely, so alpha-blended meshes are not drawn into the copy that glass refracts. The limitation is only in a code comment; setTransmissionResolution's doc and render_split's doc promise that glass shows them. setQuality("medium") is exactly this combination (4x MSAA, transmission 0.5), while "low" (no MSAA, 0.5) and "high" (TAA, 1.0) do show them.
  - scenario: setQuality('medium') (or setSamples(4) + setTransmissionResolution(0.5)); a scene with a transparent (alpha-blend) mesh behind a transmissive one. Through the glass the transparent mesh is missing; switch to setSamples(0) or resolution 1 and it appears.
  - fix: When multisampled and scaled, go through a single-sampled depth first: resolve fb's depth 1:1 into the existing screen_depth target (or a dedicated one), then blit that (NEAREST, scaled) into grab_fb. Single-sampled depth blits may scale.
- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L797: An incomplete grab framebuffer keeps its depth renderbuffer attached, breaking the whole transmission copy**
  - description: depth_into_grab attaches the new depth renderbuffer to grab_fb, and if framebuffer_is_complete is false it only records grab_depth_complete = false; the renderbuffer stays attached ("kept either way"). grab_fb is then incomplete for every later use: the color blit resolve_fb -> grab_fb at the top of grab() raises INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION each frame and the copy is never updated, so the fallback the comment describes ("transparent meshes behind glass then go without") turns into glass refracting a stale/black copy. The earlier fix (review-style: leak of the renderbuffer every frame) stopped the leak but left the attachment in place.
  - scenario: A device that refuses RGBA16F color + DEPTH24/32F renderbuffer at the grab size (the case the code says exists); a frame with a transmissive mesh and at least one transparent mesh. From that frame on, every grab() blit fails with a GL error and transmission shows the first frame's copy.
  - fix: On an incomplete result call ctx.framebuffer_detach_depth(self.grab_fb) (keep the renderbuffer id, or destroy it and remember the failure in a flag) so the color-only framebuffer stays complete.
- [ ] **[medium/webgl] L797: An incomplete grab framebuffer keeps its depth renderbuffer, so every later scene copy blit fails**
  - description: depth_into_grab attaches a new depth renderbuffer to grab_fb and, when framebuffer_is_complete says no, only records grab_depth_complete = false; the renderbuffer stays attached ("kept either way"). grab_fb is then incomplete for good, but grab() starts every frame with ctx.blit_framebuffer(resolve_fb -> grab_fb, color) (line 741), which now raises INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION and copies nothing. grab_fb1 (level 1) and generateMipmap still run on the stale level 0. The intended fallback ("transparent meshes behind glass then go without") becomes glass refracting a frozen first copy. Also listed unverified in docs/review-night-raw.md; confirmed by reading grab(), depth_into_grab() and free_target().
  - scenario: A device that refuses RGBA16F color + DEPTH24/DEPTH32F renderbuffer at the copy's size (the case the code's own comment names). Scene with one transmissive mesh and one alpha-blended mesh, HDR on: the first frame with see-through draws attaches the depth buffer; from then on each renderFrame logs a GL error at the grab blit and glass shows the scene as it was in that first frame until the target is reallocated (resize, setSamples).
  - fix: When the pair is incomplete call ctx.framebuffer_detach_depth(self.grab_fb) (keep the renderbuffer id or a 'refused' flag so it is not retried every frame); grab_fb stays a complete color-only target.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L510: set_fxaa / set_auto_exposure on an HdrOutput of a lost generation leak the new GL objects (or destroy stale handles)**
  - description: Scene::set_fxaa and set_auto_exposure (js/output.rs 262-266, 287-290) call sync_context(), which rebuilds the scene after a restore but leaves self.hdr of the old generation in place (it is only replaced in prepare_output). HdrOutput::set_fxaa(on=true) then compiles the FXAA program in the new context and stores it in the stale output; the next prepare_output sees is_lost and calls destroy(), which, because self.generation != ctx.generation(), frees only the param group/layout and skips destroy_program / free_target (line 1410). Same for AutoExposure (a.destroy(ctx, lost=true) skips its three programs and targets). Turning the feature off on the stale output instead calls destroy_* with dead handles, reported as '... is destroyed or invalid'. Neither method checks is_lost.
  - scenario: Context lost and restored; in onRestored (or any time before the next renderFrame) the app calls scene.setFxaa(true) / setAutoExposure(true) / setQuality('low') when it was off: one FXAA program (or the auto-exposure programs) is leaked per occurrence. Calling setFxaa(false) in the same window logs stale-handle errors.
  - fix: In HdrOutput::set_fxaa and set_auto_exposure return Ok(()) early when self.is_lost(ctx) (prepare_output re-applies both on the rebuilt output), or have the JS setters drop a lost self.hdr before touching it.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L510: setFxaa / setAutoExposure between a restore and the next frame build GL objects into an HdrOutput of the lost generation, which then drops them undeleted**
  - description: `Scene::set_fxaa` and `set_auto_exposure` (js/output.rs:262-270, 286-294) call `sync_context()`, which rebuilds the scene after a restore but leaves `self.hdr` of the old generation in place: it is only replaced in `prepare_output` (js/output.rs:385-389). `HdrOutput::set_fxaa(ctx, true)` / `set_auto_exposure(ctx, true)` then create a program (and for auto exposure its textures and framebuffers) in the new generation inside the old object. At the next frame `out.destroy(ctx)` sees `self.generation != ctx.generation()` and frees only the CPU-side groups and layouts (output.rs:1407-1414, exposure.rs:186-190), so the new programs / textures / framebuffers leak. Turning them off in the same window calls `destroy_program` / `free_target` on stale handles (console errors). Confirmed against the code (listed unverified in the night-raw doc).
  - scenario: Context lost and restored; in `scene.setOnRestored` / `ctx.setOnRestored` (or any UI handler before the next `renderFrame`) the app calls `scene.setFxaa(true)`, `setAutoExposure(true)` or `setQuality('low')` when the effect was off: one FXAA program, or the auto-exposure programs and targets, leaked per occurrence.
  - fix: Return early from `HdrOutput::set_fxaa` / `set_auto_exposure` when `self.is_lost(ctx)` (prepare_output applies both to the rebuilt output), or drop a lost `self.hdr` in the JS setters before touching it.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L605: Per-frame uniform/texture sets by name in the hot path**
  - description: taa_resolve (605-609, 619, 632-633), present (1040-1041, 1044, 1050-1051, 1085, 1093-1094) and grab (771) use param_set_texture_by_name / param_set_floats_by_name, which the core documents as 'convenience for setup code': each call resolves the string through the layout. That is about 15-18 name lookups per frame with TAA + FXAA, plus one per peeled layer, while the same structs already cache ParamField for their other uniforms.
  - scenario: Every renderFrame / presentFrame with HDR output; with TAA + FXAA all of the listed calls run each frame.
  - fix: Resolve the texture and fill fields once in new()/set_fxaa (ctx.param_field) and use param_set_texture / param_set_floats with the cached fields.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L716: The reused frame pass keeps resolve attachments [0, 1] after contact shadows' key output goes away (MSAA)**
  - description: `render_frame_inner` (js/mod.rs:812) reuses `frame_pass` across frames. With key output on and MSAA, `prepare_split` calls `pass.set_resolve_attachments(&[0, 1])`. Once key output is off, `finish_pass` only calls `pass.set_resolve(self.resolve_fb)`, and `Pass::set_resolve` does not reset `resolve_color` (only `clear_resolve` does), so the opaque pass keeps asking to resolve attachment 1 of a target that has only attachment 0.
  - scenario: samples=4, no TAA: `setContactShadows({})`, renderFrame, `setContactShadows(null)` (or add a custom-shader opaque mesh so `pbr_only` is false), renderFrame -> console error '[luxel] resolve: color attachment 1 is missing on the target or the resolve target; skipped', and every later frame resolves through the per-attachment path (readBuffer/drawBuffers juggling) instead of the single blit. `set_resolve_attachments` also allocates a Vec every frame while key output is on.
  - fix: In `finish_pass` call `pass.clear_resolve()` before `set_resolve` (or set attachments to `&[]`) so stale lists never survive; only set `[0,1]` when it changes.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L717: Stale resolve attachment list [0, 1] left on the opaque pass after the key-light output goes away (MSAA)**
  - description: With MSAA and contact shadows, prepare_split calls pass.set_resolve_attachments(&[0, 1]). The pass is the Scene's persistent frame_pass. When the key output is later off (or build_key_targets found the pair incomplete), finish_pass only calls pass.set_resolve(self.resolve_fb), which does not reset resolve_color (only clear_resolve does). The renderer then takes the per-attachment path, finds no attachment 1 on fb/resolve_fb, logs '[luxel] resolve: color attachment 1 is missing on the target or the resolve target; skipped' and keeps using the slower per-attachment blit (read/draw buffer switches) every frame.
  - scenario: Default 4x MSAA: setContactShadows({}) -> renderFrame -> setContactShadows(null) -> renderFrame. Or contact shadows on and a mesh with a custom (non-PBR) program is added. A spurious console error appears and the resolve stays on the per-attachment path.
  - fix: In finish_pass (MSAA branch) call pass.set_resolve_attachments(&[]) after set_resolve, or call pass.clear_resolve() before set_resolve.
- [ ] **[low/webgl] L717: Reused frame pass keeps resolve attachments [0, 1] after the key-light output goes away**
  - description: Outside my scope, found while tracing the occlusion target. prepare_split calls pass.set_resolve_attachments(&[0, 1]) when opaque_fb exists. The frame pass is reused across frames (js/mod.rs:812, 939). With MSAA, finish_pass only calls set_resolve, which does not reset resolve_color; clear_resolve runs only on the non-MSAA and non-HDR paths. Once the key output is off, the pass targets self.fb (one colour attachment) with resolve_color still [0, 1]. The core reports 'resolve: color attachment 1 is missing on the target or the resolve target; skipped' once; attachment 0 still resolves, so the image is right.
  - scenario: HDR with samples > 1: setContactShadows({}), render a frame, then setContactShadows(null) (or add a mesh with a custom shader). The next frame logs a console error.
  - fix: In finish_pass, reset the list on the MSAA branch (pass.set_resolve_attachments(&[])) before prepare_split sets [0, 1] for the key output.

## `packages/luxel/src/pbr.rs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L197: fit_texture_units doc says the frame uses four samplers; the code reserves six**
  - description: The doc comment says 'the frame's own four included'. FRAME_SAMPLERS is 6 (environment diffuse, specular, BRDF table, scene copy, peeled depth, shadow maps), and the test counts 6. A reader sizing a material from the doc expects 12 maps on a 16-unit device; the code allows 10.
  - scenario: On a 16-unit device a material with 11 or 12 maps has maps dropped, which the comment says should fit.
  - fix: Change the comment to six, or refer to FRAME_SAMPLERS.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L247: shadow_key keeps normal and UV attribute bits for opaque materials, splitting shadow programs**
  - description: For materials that are not alpha-masked, shadow_key still keeps ATTR_NORMAL, ATTR_UV0 and ATTR_UV1. The DEPTH_ONLY path of pbr.frag reads none of them unless ALPHAMODE_MASK is set (pbr.frag:322-329), so up to eight identical depth-only programs are compiled and linked, each from the full pbr.frag source. The doc comment says variants that differ only in shading share one.
  - scenario: A model whose opaque primitives differ in having normals, TEXCOORD_0 or TEXCOORD_1 compiles a separate shadow program for each combination.
  - fix: Keep the normal and UV bits only in the Mask branch (UV0/UV1 are needed there for the base colour map); otherwise keep only the deformer bits.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] L333: PBR variants (and their depth-only twins) are never released while the scene lives, whatever is despawned**
  - description: `PbrShaders` keeps every compiled program 'until destroy'. `Scene::despawn` frees a model's GPU data and materials but not the variants compiled for it, and `setShadingModel` or a material edit that changes the key (`update_variant`) compiles further ones beside the old. Nothing counts users, so `shaderVariantCount` only grows.
  - scenario: The viewer's flow, `scene.despawn(root); root = scene.addModel(next)` over a catalogue of products with different attribute / map / extension combinations, with a shading-model switch now and then: every variant and shadow variant ever linked stays on the GPU (each a full pbr.vert + pbr.frag link) until `scene.destroy()`.
  - fix: Count the meshes using each variant (or sweep variants no MeshRenderer / ShadowCaster / pending switch refers to after a despawn) and destroy the unused ones, perhaps past a small cap so a quick re-add doesn't recompile.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L402: is_pbr is a linear scan and runs for every mesh on every HDR frame**
  - description: is_pbr walks programs.values(). js/output.rs:421-428 computes pbr_only on every HDR frame by calling it for each MeshRenderer, before testing whether contact shadows are on at all (the && comes after). That is meshes x compiled variants comparisons per frame even with contact shadows off.
  - scenario: A scene with 5,000 meshes and 40 PBR variants does about 200,000 comparisons per frame for a value that is discarded when contact shadows are off (the default).
  - fix: Evaluate self.contact_shadows.is_some() first so the scan is skipped, and keep a HashSet<ProgramId> (or cache pbr_only until meshes or materials change) for is_pbr.

## `packages/luxel/src/pick.rs`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L26: Ray::through returns a zero direction for a camera with an infinite far plane**
  - description: Ray::through unprojects NDC z = +1 for the far point. With the infinite projection that Camera::projection_matrix builds (far = INFINITY: a glTF camera without zfar, or setCamera with zfar: Infinity), z = 1 lies at infinity, so the inverse gives w = 0, project_point3 divides by zero, and (far - near).normalize_or_zero() yields Vec3::ZERO. Checked numerically with glam 0.30.10 in a scratch crate: dir was [0,0,0] for every view matrix, near value and ndc tried, while a finite far gave the right direction. camera_ray and pick_bounds inherit it: ray_aabb with a zero direction only hits when the origin is already inside the box.
  - scenario: scene.setCamera(e, {type:'perspective', zfar: Infinity}), or a glTF camera with no zfar (model.rs:227). screenToRay(cam, x, y) returns [ox, oy, oz, 0, 0, 0]; raycastBounds(cam, x, y) returns null over any mesh the near-plane point is not inside.
  - fix: Take the second point at a finite depth (for example NDC z = 0, or any z < 1), or for a perspective projection use the direction from the eye to the near-plane point. Add a test with far = INFINITY.

## `packages/luxel/src/render.rs`

- [ ] **[medium/async] L773: A second render call in the same frame wipes the motion-vector history, so moving meshes ghost under TAA**
  - description: `render_split` calls `MotionVectors::begin` on every call (line 773), which swaps `prev`/`next` and clears `next`. `begin` assumes one call per displayed frame. `Scene::render(camera, pass)` (js/mod.rs:951, the documented way to draw into a pass of your own) goes through the same `render_split`, and motion vectors stay on while TAA is on. After such a call, the following `renderFrame` compares each mesh against the state recorded a moment earlier in the same frame, finds `*prev == now`, and emits no motion draw (motion.rs:201-205).
  - scenario: TAA on, an animated or moving mesh. Each frame the app calls `scene.render(mirrorCamera, myPass)` for an offscreen view and then `scene.renderFrame(mainCamera)`. `stats.moving` is always 0, `push_motion` returns 0, and TAA reprojects moving meshes with camera motion only, so they smear. Meshes the extra camera culls are missing from `prev` entirely and also get no velocity.
  - fix: Advance the motion history only for the frame that feeds TAA: either have the caller pass a flag (or call `begin` from `render_frame_inner`), or skip `motion.begin`/`track` when the call is not the TAA frame (for example when `late` is None or jitter is zero).
- [ ] **[medium/async] L824: Meshes left Reloading for good count as pending every frame: needsRender never goes false**
  - description: `render_split` counts a mesh with the `Reloading` marker in `stats.pending` (line 824; `lighting/casters.rs:61` adds its shadow caster too), and `keeps_changing()` returns true while `stats.pending > 0` (js/on_demand.rs:117). `Reloading` is removed only by a successful `apply_reloads`. It stays for good when the reload fails (`apply_reloads` Err branch, restore.rs:346-353: network error, 404), when `adopt` fails ('the file has changed'), and by design with `setRestoreModels(false)` or `forgetSource` ('their meshes stay out of frames until replaced'). `Scene.pending()` then also reports them as shaders still compiling.
  - scenario: `scene.setRestoreModels(false)`, on-demand loop. The context is lost and restored (a phone tab sent to the background). From then on `needsRender(camera)` is true every frame until the app despawns the old model roots, so the page draws full frames continuously, which the on-demand API exists to avoid. Same after a loss when the model's URL no longer answers.
  - fix: Count reloading meshes separately from link-pending ones (a `stats.reloading`), and make `keeps_changing` depend on reloads actually in flight (`restore_tasks` unsettled or the inbox non-empty) rather than on the marker. Clear the marker, or move to a 'failed' marker, when a reload gives up.
- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L98: The public LIGHTING_BLOCK doc for u_output.z disagrees with what is written**
  - description: The doc for the block, which custom scene shaders declare, says `u_output` z is "1 with a scene copy". `write_lighting` writes `1.0 + scene_color_min_lod` (lines 1357-1361), and the built-in shader reads it as `u_output.z > 0.5` and `u_output.z - 1.0` (shaders/lib/environment.glsl:31-78). review-style.md lists stale block docs as fixed for `u_shading`, but this field still drifts.
  - scenario: A custom shader written from the Rust doc tests `u_output.z == 1.0` to detect a scene copy. With HDR on and neither MSAA nor TAA, the scene sets the minimum LOD to 1, z becomes 2.0, and the shader stops refracting.
  - fix: Update the doc comment: "z 0 without a scene copy, else 1 + the least mip to read it at".
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L777: Early return on a bad camera leaves last frame's shadow passes to be rendered again, possibly with freed object groups**
  - description: When `write_frame` returns None, `render_split` returns before `lights.prepare`, so `ShadowMaps::begin` never clears the shadow passes or their dirty flags. `release_removed` has already run (line 770) and may have destroyed object groups those passes reference. The caller (`Scene::render_passes`, js/mod.rs:1345-1357) calls `render_shadows` unconditionally, and `ShadowMaps::render` replays every pass still marked dirty from the previous frame.
  - scenario: `renderFrame(e)` where `e` has lost its Camera or Transform (or is the wrong entity), after a frame that drew shadow maps. Every such frame re-renders the previous frame's shadow maps and ground-shadow pass, which is wasted GPU work. If a caster was despawned in between, the core also logs "param group ... is destroyed or invalid".
  - fix: On the early return, tell the lighting system the frame has no shadow work (a `lights.skip_frame()` that clears passes and dirty flags, like `hold`), or resolve the camera before `release_removed` and have `render_shadows` do nothing for an aborted frame.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] L1165: Shadow casters with instance_count above MAX_COPIES draw unclamped and read other objects' matrices**
  - description: The camera path clamps a self-instanced command to the slots it wrote (`cmd.instance_count.clamp(1, MAX_COPIES)`, line 884). `collect_shadows` writes at most `MAX_COPIES` (4096) copies via `push_copies` (line 1160), or reuses the camera pass's base, but pushes the caster with the original `d.mesh.command` (lines 1165 and lighting/mod.rs:427-433). The shadow draw then runs `instance_count` instances, and `u_instance_base + gl_InstanceID` runs past the 4096 slots written for this object.
  - scenario: `setMesh` with a command whose `instanceCount` is 10000, a scene-prelude (PBR) material, and a shadow-casting light. In the shadow map, instances 4096..9999 read the instance-texture slots that follow: other meshes' matrices, joint matrices, morph weights, or texels outside the uploaded rows. The result is stray or missing shadows, while the camera pass draws 4096 copies.
  - fix: Clamp the caster's command the same way before `out.push`: copy `d`, set `d.mesh.command.instance_count = count.clamp(1, MAX_COPIES)` when the command is instanceable. Better, share one helper with the camera path.
- [ ] **[low/perf] L1225: Joint matrices are recomputed and written once per primitive, not once per skin**
  - description: `write_deformers` runs for every drawn mesh entity with a `Skin`. For each one it looks up every joint's `WorldMatrix`, multiplies by the inverse bind matrix and pushes the result into the instance texture. Primitives of one glTF mesh share the same skin, so the same palette is computed and stored once per primitive. With TAA, `MotionVectors::track` computes it again and pushes it twice more for moving meshes.
  - scenario: A character with 25 primitives and 120 joints does 3000 ECS lookups and matrix multiplies and writes 3000 slots per frame instead of 120. With TAA and animation this is roughly tripled. Crowds also bring the instance texture closer to its size limit.
  - fix: Cache the joint base per skin for the frame, keyed by the skin's joint list identity or the node entity that owns the skin. Reuse the base for the other primitives, and let motion tracking reuse it too.
- [ ] **[low/async] L1519: pick() draws meshes that are still Reloading after a context restore, using stale vertex arrays**
  - description: `render_split` leaves out entities with the `Reloading` marker (line 824), and so does `casters::cull`, because their `MeshRenderer` still holds the lost context's vao until `apply_reloads` swaps it. `SceneRenderer::pick` filters only on `mesh.vao.is_null()` and visibility. It creates object groups for these entities and pushes draws with the stale `mesh.vao` into the pick pass.
  - scenario: Context lost and restored; models are re-fetching (meshes carry `Reloading`). The user clicks, `scene.pick(camera, x, y)` runs. The core reports "render (draw): vertex array ... is destroyed or invalid" for each such mesh (renderer.rs:304) and the pick resolves null or the wrong (farther) entity. Nothing indicates that the scene is not ready.
  - fix: Skip `Reloading` entities in `pick` the same way `render_split` does, using the storage lookup filtered on non-empty.
- [ ] **[low/async] L1519: pick() draws meshes marked Reloading, whose vertex arrays are from the lost context**
  - description: SceneRenderer::render leaves out entities with Reloading (render.rs:824). pick() filters only a null vao and hidden entities. After a context restore, meshes waiting for their model (or left for good with setRestoreModels(false)) still hold vertex array ids of the previous generation. The pick pass pushes draws with them; the core skips each one and logs 'vertex array ... is destroyed or invalid' once per distinct handle. The pick result itself is still correct for the other meshes.
  - scenario: Context lost and restored with a large model still refetching; the app picks on pointermove. The console gets one error per reloading mesh.
  - fix: Skip entities with Reloading in pick(), as render() does.
- [ ] **[low/async] L1519: pick() draws meshes that are still Reloading, with vertex arrays of the lost context**
  - description: render_split leaves out entities with Reloading (line 824); pick filters only a null vao and hidden entities. After a restore, model meshes waiting for their reload (or left for good with setRestoreModels(false)) still hold vertex array ids of the previous generation; the pick pass pushes them and the core reports each through report_stale, which is never silenced in the render path.
  - scenario: Context lost and restored, a large model still refetching; the app picks on pointer events. One console error per reloading mesh ('render: vertex array ... is destroyed or invalid') and the pick sees through the model.
  - fix: Skip Reloading entities in pick() the way render_split does.
- [ ] **[low/async] L1519: pick() draws meshes that are still Reloading, with vertex arrays of the lost context**
  - description: The pick loop filters on `mesh.vao.is_null()` and visibility only. After a restore, a model's meshes keep their old `MeshRenderer.vao` (a stale handle) and carry `Reloading` until `apply_reloads`; `render_split` skips them (line 824) but `pick` pushes them. The core renderer reports each stale vertex array through `report_stale`, which is not silenced in the render path, and those meshes cannot be hit. (Listed unverified in review-night-raw.md; confirmed.)
  - scenario: Context restored, models still downloading; the user clicks, the app calls `scene.pick(camera, x, y)`. The console shows 'render: vertex array … is destroyed or invalid; ignored' and the promise resolves null although a model will be there a moment later.
  - fix: Skip entities that have `Reloading` in the pick loop, as `render_split` and the caster collection do.

## `packages/luxel/src/transform.rs`

- [ ] **[high/correctness] L43: Transform::from_matrix returns a NaN rotation for any matrix with a zero scale axis**
  - description: from_matrix calls glam 0.30.10 Mat4::to_scale_rotation_translation, which divides the axes by the scale (scale.recip()). With a zero scale on any axis that is 0 * inf = NaN, and the whole quaternion comes out NaN (checked in a scratch crate: scale (0,0,0) and (1,1,0) both give Quat(NaN, NaN, NaN, NaN); glam only guards this with glam_assert, which is off). spawn_model builds every node Transform (model.rs:85) and every EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing instance Transform (model.rs:133) through this function from the loader's composed matrix (luxel-gltf scene.rs:98-103), so the file's own TRS is lost. Transform::matrix() is then NaN, and TransformSystem propagates it to the node's WorldMatrix, all descendants and every skin joint matrix that uses the node. js/mod.rs:361 (set a matrix from JS) and framing.rs:96 go through the same function.
  - scenario: Load a glTF where a node has scale [0,0,0] at rest (the usual Blender way to hide a prop or bone until an animation scales it up) or a flattened node with scale [1,1,0]. The node's Transform.rotation is NaN from spawn. Playing a clip that animates only the scale back to 1 writes scale only; rotation stays NaN, so the part and its subtree never appear. If the node is a joint, the skinned vertices weighted to it get NaN positions.
  - fix: Pass the node's translation / rotation / scale from the loader (gltf node.transform().decomposed()) instead of decomposing a matrix. In from_matrix itself, handle zero-length axes: keep scale 0 and build the rotation from the remaining axes, or fall back to identity, and never return a non-finite quaternion.

## `scripts/brand-handles.mjs`

- [ ] **[low/cleanliness] L413: Brand declarations are spliced between the first export and its JSDoc**
  - description: `first` is the first line that starts with `export `, and the declarations go in directly before it. In the generated .d.ts that line is `export interface CaptureOptions {`, preceded by its doc comment `/** How `capture` takes a picture. */` (examples/viewer/pkg/luxel.d.ts lines 4-5). The comment 'After the leading comments' is therefore wrong: the insertion lands after the first declaration's doc comment too.
  - scenario: After build-npm, the published luxel.d.ts reads: `/** How `capture` takes a picture. */`, `// Handle brands …`, `/** A handle … */`, `export type BufferId = …`. CaptureOptions has lost its documentation in editors, and a stray JSDoc sits above the first brand type.
  - fix: Walk back from `first` over the contiguous comment block (lines starting with `/**`, ` *`, `*/` or `//`) and insert before it, or insert right after the tslint/eslint header lines.

## `scripts/typings-check.ts`

- [ ] **[low/api] L41: Material map, property and semantics names and gpuTimings are untyped; the typings check itself passes a name that throws**
  - description: `setMaterialTexture(map: string)`, `materialTexture`, `setMaterialProperty(name: string)` and `setMaterialSemantics(semantics: string)` accept any string, while tone mapping, quality and shading model have literal unions. The typings check calls `scene.setMaterialTexture(m, "baseColor", tex, undefined)`: `"baseColor"` is not in `MAP_NAMES` (the name is `"baseColorTexture"`), so this call, which type-checks, throws 'no map "baseColor"' at run time. `gpuTimings()` is typed `object` (js/mod.rs:1090), so `scene.gpuTimings().opaque` does not type-check.
  - scenario: A user writes `scene.setMaterialTexture(mat, 'baseColor', tex)` following the typings check or by guessing. TypeScript accepts it and it throws at run time.
  - fix: Add `MaterialMapName` and `MaterialSemanticsName` unions (generated from `MAP_NAMES`) and `unchecked_return_type = "Record<string, number>"` for gpuTimings; fix the name in the check.

---

# Добор: файлы, не попавшие в ночные куски

Два дополнительных ревьюера (Fable, 20.11): `fullscreen.rs`, `loader.js`, `decoders.js`; примеры (Rust и JS), `serve.mjs`, `ubo-bench.js`, `trace-summary.mjs`. Тоже не проверены.

- [ ] **[medium/api] `examples/js-cube/README.md` L22: The documented `tsc --checkJs` command fails for js-cube**
  - description: programsReady(programs: Uint32Array | ProgramId[]) (luxel-gl/src/program.rs:864) references ProgramId, declared only by brand-handles.mjs -> TS2304 in pkg/luxel_gl.d.ts. Against branded typings main.js fails: number not assignable to FramebufferId/ProgramId at main.js:166,174,177,245 (NULL constants).
  - scenario: Run the two README commands in order; tsc exits non-zero.
  - fix: Declare handle type names in the crate's own typings (typescript_custom_section), or brand the example pkg and type the NULL constants.
- [ ] **[medium/correctness] `examples/js-cube/main.js` L167: Render loop stops for good if the MSAA target is rebuilt while the context is lost**
  - description: frame() calls framePass every frame, also while lost; a stale MSAA target makes it call createFramebuffer/createRenderbufferMultisample, which throw on a lost context (check_alive). The throw skips requestAnimationFrame(frame) at line 257; on_restored does not restart the loop.
  - scenario: Press M, L, then M again within 500 ms (or resize while lost): uncaught 'WebGL context is lost', canvas frozen after restore.
  - fix: At the top of frame: if (ctx.isLost()) { requestAnimationFrame(frame); return; } or schedule rAF first / try-finally.
- [ ] **[medium/api] `examples/js-scene/README.md` L29: The documented `tsc --checkJs` command fails for js-scene**
  - description: The plain wasm-pack .d.ts uses Entity/ProgramId/StateId/VertexArrayId/ParamGroupId/TextureId, declared only by scripts/brand-handles.mjs (13x TS2304); main.js:36 TS2554 (loadModel options not optional, luxel-gltf/src/lib.rs:633); main.js:110 TS2345 (toneMappers is string[], setToneMapping takes ToneMappingName). Against branded dist/npm/luxel.d.ts: plain numbers not assignable to Entity (main.js:82,84,125).
  - scenario: Follow the README: wasm-pack build, then the tsc command -> ~15 errors.
  - fix: Run brand-handles.mjs on the example pkg in the README and type toneMappers/picked; or declare handle names in a typescript_custom_section and make options optional so plain wasm-pack output is self-contained.
- [ ] **[low/leaks] `examples/cube/src/main.rs` L106: Each context restore leaks a parameter layout and group (same in examples/scene/src/main.rs:98-105)**
  - description: build_scene runs again from on_restored and creates a new param layout/group; those are CPU-side and survive loss (Resources::forget_all, context.rs:167-180), so the old ones stay in the pools. Comment at lines 45-46 is wrong about them.
  - scenario: Press L repeatedly: one more layout and group per cycle.
  - fix: Create layout/group once and re-point the block on restore, or destroy them before rebuilding.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] `examples/js-cube/serve.mjs` L18: Dev server exits on a malformed request URL (same in examples/js-scene/serve.mjs:18)**
  - description: decodeURIComponent(new URL(...).pathname) runs outside try in an async handler; URIError becomes an unhandled rejection and Node exits. examples/viewer/serve.mjs already guards with handle(...).catch.
  - scenario: GET /% -> process exits with code 1.
  - fix: Use the viewer's handle(req,res).catch(...) wrapper, answer 400 on URIError.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] `examples/npm-consumer/serve.mjs` L29: Dev server can be killed by one malformed request, from the LAN with --lan**
  - description: Same pattern as js-cube server; with --lan it listens on 0.0.0.0.
  - scenario: node serve.mjs --lan; curl http://<ip>:8091/% from another machine -> server exits.
  - fix: Same wrapper as examples/viewer/serve.mjs.
- [ ] **[low/api] `examples/texture/src/main.rs` L101: Commented reference snippet for `texture_load_image` does not compile as written**
  - description: Closure::<dyn FnMut()>::new(..).into_js_value() then cb.into() where js_sys::Function is expected (texture.rs:1159-1167); callback is (width,height); targets tex that already has immutable storage (line 96).
  - scenario: Uncomment the lines -> trait-bound error.
  - fix: Closure::<dyn FnMut(u32,u32)>::new(|_w,_h|{}).into_js_value().unchecked_into::<js_sys::Function>() on a fresh texture.
- [ ] **[low/async] `packages/luxel-gltf/src/loader.js` L36: Without `AbortSignal.any`, `cancel()` stops nothing when the app gave a signal, and a second signal is dropped**
  - description: Line 36 falls back to `this.signal ?? this.stop.signal` when `AbortSignal.any` is missing (Safari < 17.4, Chrome < 116, Firefox < 124). If the app passed a signal, `cancel()` (lib.rs:846) aborts `stop` without reaching any running fetch. Line 31: when both `signal` and `request.signal` are given, only `signals[0]` is used.
  - scenario: On Safari 16, a .gltf with five external buffers where the first returns 404: the load rejects, but the other four downloads run to completion.
  - fix: Fallback for `any`: a new AbortController aborted by `abort` listeners on each source signal.
- [ ] **[low/api] `packages/luxel-gltf/src/loader.js` L156: `onProgress` reports `total == loaded` for responses without Content-Length, though the docs say `total` is 0 while unknown**
  - description: With no Content-Length `expected` is 0, and every chunk adds `grow = length - counted` to the total (lines 156-158), so the callback gets `(n, n)` on every chunk. The viewer works around it with `total > loaded`.
  - scenario: A chunked 200 MB .glb without Content-Length: an app drawing `total ? loaded / total : indeterminate` shows a full bar from the first chunk.
  - fix: Add unknown sizes to `total` only when the stream ends, or change the docs.
- [ ] **[low/correctness] `packages/luxel-gltf/src/loader.js` L185: A dropped file whose URI has a bare '%' is never found in `files` and is fetched from https://files.invalid/ instead**
  - description: `lookup` runs `decodeURIComponent(new URL(url).pathname…)` inside a try and returns null on any throw. The URL parser keeps a lone `%` as it is, so a glTF URI such as `wood 50%.png` resolves to `https://files.invalid/wood%2050%.png`, and `decodeURIComponent` throws URIError. Returning null skips both the map lookup and the `isn't among the files given` error at line 192. `bytes()` / `blob()` then send a real request to the placeholder host.
  - scenario: The user drops `model.gltf` and `wood 50%.png`. `loadModel(file, { files })` requests `https://files.invalid/wood%2050%.png`, fails with `Failed to fetch`, the texture stays empty. For a `.bin` named this way the whole load rejects.
  - fix: Decode per segment with a fallback on URIError; for a URL under FILES_BASE never fall through to the network: throw the `isn't among the files given` error.
